# Run of Practice — Build Status

**Last updated:** 2026-08-08 (forty-first session, see Session Log for the full list of what happened that day)

**Status:** Live in production at [runofpractice.com](https://www.runofpractice.com), single real user (solo coach), actively iterating ahead of August testing. Twenty-sixth session shipped all five Goals & Insights enhancements from the coach's own spec doc in one pass: Skill-Development Trends, Next Practice Guidance, combined Builder Goal Guidance, the Practice Execution Scorecard, and Drill Performance Insights — all built on one new shared SQL attribution layer so every report uses identical category-minute math. Twenty-seventh session, same day, was direct feedback from actually testing that work live: 14 items, spanning a real default-permissions bug (library sharing was defaulting to shared, should default to private), a real z-index bug (a drill's ellipsis menu was visually clipped by the row below it), a new coach-accepted-invite notification, a Builder library switcher, drill-recommendation drill-downs in Next Practice Guidance, a drill-library sort picker, and several smaller UX/copy fixes. Twenty-eighth session shipped Development Pulse, a new dynamic single-CTA Home widget from the coach's own spec doc — reusing the twenty-sixth session's shared goal-attribution math rather than a "fifth variation," and finding/fixing two real bugs along the way (a spec-violating over-duration minute recommendation, and a navigation bug that silently loaded the wrong practice in Builder). Twenty-ninth session, same day, was a small 3-item feedback batch: fixed Goals & Insights' side margins, added a "Tag Untagged Drills" CTA linking Overview to a new filtered/tag-as-you-go Library view, and gave Development Pulse a collapsible caret with persisted state and a new-insight dot — plus one real, pre-existing rendering bug found and fixed along the way (the insufficient-history state's goal-mix bar was invisible due to a camelCase/snake_case prop mismatch). Continued same day with one more direct-feedback fix: Permissions' sharing/delegation toggles weren't visually reflecting a tap until the modal was closed and reopened, a stale-object-snapshot bug (see Gotchas). Thirtieth session, same day, fixed a keyboard-visibility/lost-text bug in the Skill Tags picker and gave Create Template proper margins plus inline location creation, matching Builder. Thirty-first session, same day, another Goals & Insights feedback batch: an accurate "all drills tagged" message, a Delete Practice action in History, no-date-picker time editing for a logged drill, and the window-weeks control relocated into its report's own header -- plus two real bugs found along the way (History's delete not actually excluding the practice, and the window-weeks control silently reverting after navigating away due to a stale client-side cache). Thirty-second session (2026-08-04) was an 8-item Builder/Home direct-feedback batch: equipment a coach doesn't own now survives "Add Drill Anyway" instead of being silently dropped, and is flagged (not hidden) everywhere it appears -- the drill's own library view, Builder's add-to-practice flow, Practice Setup, and the public Practice Preview link; Home's Upcoming Practices always shows the next 4 practices regardless of date, and the separate 14-day "needs a plan" nudge is gone; a player marked out for a practice is now blocked (not just visually ignored) from station assignment in Builder; two real friction bugs in station-block editing were root-caused and fixed (see Gotchas); Builder's duration pill is now tap-to-toggle between "planned/total" and "N minutes still needed"; Practice Components default to Intro/Closer/Station Block for a new coach with a helpful inline hint replacing the blank grid quadrant; Run Now is disabled in Builder more than an hour before a scheduled practice; and the cancel-practice confirm dialog lost its em dash and got clearer button labels. Thirty-second session continued, same day, was a much larger 17-item batch led by a real broken experience Jax hit as an assistant coach (joining a practice while the head coach was in setup blanked their screen and demoted the head coach to read-only) -- fixed by making "Practice Setup" a real shared pre-live stage on the live session itself (new `setup_confirmed_at` column, an atomic `start_or_join_live_session` RPC replacing a race-prone create-or-check pattern, and a `PreviewView` routing fix so a coach is never demoted into the anonymous helper view), plus a smart Join-Practice/Run-Now button everywhere a practice can be started. The other 16 items were smaller live-view UX fixes (attendance-pill icon, blank vs. "0/0 covered," a shorter-segment audio-cue skip, an Overview caret, a restyled Library sort control, a leaner ellipsis menu, a dark color scheme distinguishing stations-intro/transition from actual coaching, a bigger reordered live header, coach-defaults-to-own-station, interactive stations-overview reassignment, a clickable upcoming-drill preview, a Home hero fix, and a checklist-flash fix) and a full History consolidation (removed from the Schedule Agenda tab, rebuilt in Goals & Insights with Run Now/Save Template/PDF/Delete-with-confirmation, a single edit-mode toggle, unread-notes indicators, and actual- rather than scheduled-time display) -- plus one real latent correctness bug found and fixed (an `.insert().select()` response zipped by array index instead of a stable key) and an extended, ultimately-false-alarm debugging investigation that reconfirmed a known console-message-staleness gotcha for a third time (see Gotchas). No automated test suite exists — every change is verified empirically in a live browser against production using disposable Supabase test accounts (see Working Conventions below). Thirty-third session (2026-08-05), without access to that account-creation tooling, was a 13-item direct-feedback batch led by a real bug root-caused via read-only production queries: a practice run a day early silently marked its own real scheduled slot completed instead of staying scheduled, fixed with a shared 2-hour "is this practice actually happening now" rule and a 3-choice guard (Cancel/Run as New Practice/Run Now Anyway) reused across Home's hero and Practice Detail. Also this session: Practice Setup rebuilt as a black, countdown-and-equipment-rich "Run of Practice" rundown (merging the old public preview link's informational richness into the real shared collaborative pre-live stage); a real Save-as-Template data-loss bug root-caused to `saveActivityTree` silently swallowing insert/update failures (now surfaced, not just logged); two distinct causes of a "glitchy Home" flash (a stale-default `runStatus` briefly showing an already-completed practice as upcoming, and Development Pulse's own documented team-guessing fallback visibly swapping teams); Insights' false "untagged drills" nagging traced to non-station Practice Components inflating the attribution denominator (extended the existing `'break'`-type exclusion to also cover them, both in SQL and its client-side mirror); a live-header layout fix (title now full-width, controls consolidated onto one row); a person-icon attendance pill and a properly-sized audio toggle; a library-source picker for Builder's station drill picker (previously a silent, unlabeled merge of every accessible library); and Library's blue sport-header text (one of four identical headers was missing an explicit color, falling back to the browser/OS default) recolored green to match the rest of the app. This session's changes have not yet been live-verified in a browser against production the way this file's other entries are — flagged explicitly in its own Session Log entry, not glossed over. Forty-first session (2026-08-08) was a stability sweep ahead of the planned August test rather than new feature work: audited every Gotchas entry that said "check for this shape elsewhere" plus one new pattern (discarded Supabase write errors), and found/fixed five real, previously-unswept instances — a stale-cache bug in History's Delete Practice, two more ancestor-state-setter infinite-loop-risk instances (`HistoryViewer`, `PracticeDetail`, both only live-risky via Schedule), a silently-swallowed update error in `savePracticeTree`'s own update branch (the most central write path in the app), a silently-swallowed error in `setPlannedAbsences`/`AbsencePicker`, and two more `useState`-only reentrancy races (`FuturePracticeGuardModal`, `AbsencePicker`). Same day, continued: all five fixes live-verified end-to-end with a disposable coach account (this file's own documented convention) — confirmed working with no regressions, plus one new FK gotcha found during teardown (`planned_absences.noted_by` has the same missing-`ON DELETE`-action gap already tracked for `session_attendance.player_id`). See Gotchas and Session Log for detail.

## Standing instructions for Claude Code (read this section every session, before touching anything else)

**What this document is.** This is the single, permanent source of truth for the state of the Run of Practice codebase — not a one-off handoff note. It exists so a brand-new Claude Code session (or Jax, months from now) can pick up exactly where the last session left off without re-deriving context from scratch or re-learning a bug that's already been fixed once. Treat it as living documentation you are expected to both read from and write to, every session, not just a status report someone else maintains.

**Before doing anything else this session:**
1. Read this entire file top to bottom. It's long on purpose — skimming risks re-building something that already exists or re-hitting a bug that's already documented below.
2. Run `git log --oneline -20` and check for anything more recent than the "Last updated" line above — the file is only as current as the last session that bothered to update it.
3. Check the Known Gaps and Gotchas sections specifically before starting on anything that sounds like it might already be a known limitation or a previously-hit bug.

**House style, permanent: no em dashes.** Not in code comments, not in commit messages, not in this file, not in any in-app copy (dialog text, button labels, error messages, etc.). Use a period, comma, or parentheses instead. This has been asked for more than once — check your own writing before it goes in, don't rely on being reminded again.

**How this file is organized, top to bottom:** what the app is and how it's built → what already works today (Feature Inventory) → the database/permissions architecture → Known Gaps (this is the closest thing to a roadmap — things deliberately not built yet) → Working Conventions (house rules for how code gets written and tested here) → Gotchas (bugs and tooling quirks not to re-learn) → Persistent Design Themes (the "why" behind decisions that evolved over several sessions) → the Session Log (a dated, chronological record of every session's work, newest entry first). Keep new content in the section it actually belongs to — don't let the Session Log become the only place new facts live, since that makes them easy to miss.

**How to log in and test as a real user.** This app has no local Supabase environment and no automated test suite — everything is verified by actually clicking through the live app in a browser against production, using a disposable throwaway account, never the real coach's account. To do this:
- Check for a local `.env` file in the repo root for a `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` (it's gitignored — never commit it, never put it in this file, never paste it into chat). If it's missing, ask Jax for it rather than guessing or working around its absence.
- Use the Supabase Admin API to mint a session for a throwaway account (`generate_link` + `verifyOtp`/`verifyEmailOtp` — see Working Conventions below for the exact pattern), then inject the resulting tokens into the browser's `localStorage` under the key `sb-<project-ref>-auth-token` to land signed in as that account.
- Click through the real app end to end (a local dev server against the same production Supabase backend is fine, or the deployed site directly) rather than stopping at "the code looks right" — this file's own Session Log has multiple entries where a change looked correct on review but failed live (a missing DB grant, an RLS gap, a stale cache), so live verification is not optional for anything touching data access or a real user flow.
- Delete the throwaway account and all its data via the Admin API when done. Never leave test data sitting in production, and never test a destructive flow (account deletion, hard-delete cascades, etc.) against the one real coach's account.

**What's expected of you before you end a session:**
1. **Append a new entry to the top of the Session Log** — newest first, following the voice and level of detail already established there: what was asked, what was actually built (call out anything that turned out to already exist, or that you deliberately chose not to build and why), any real bug found along the way as distinct from requested changes, and exactly how it was verified live.
2. **Update the sections above the log directly**, not just in the log entry — if you shipped a feature, add it to the Feature Inventory; if you closed a known gap, strike it through or remove it from Known Gaps; if you hit a new footgun worth remembering, add it to Gotchas; if you established a new house rule, add it to Working Conventions. The log is history — everything above it should reflect the *current* state of the app without anyone having to read the whole log to reconstruct it.
3. **Update the "Last updated" and "Status" lines at the very top of this file** to reflect the latest session.
4. Do not create new companion doc files for handoff notes, specs, or status summaries — this file replaced five of those already (see the footer note at the bottom of this document) specifically because parallel docs drift out of sync with each other and with the code. Extend this file instead.

A `BUILD-STATUS.original.md` sits alongside this file — the pre-reorganization version, kept only so Jax can diff against it if something looks off. No content was removed when this file was reorganized; sections were regrouped and the log was sorted into strict chronological order for scannability.

---

## What this is

A live-practice-orchestration app for youth sports coaches: build a practice plan (drills, stations, timing), run it live with a timer/rotation system, share a read-only view with parent helpers, track attendance, and plan on a recurring schedule. React/Vite frontend, Supabase (Postgres + Auth + Realtime) backend, deployed on Vercel.


## Stack & repo layout

- **Frontend:** React 18, Vite, no CSS framework (hand-rolled CSS-in-JS injected via a `<style>` tag, Barlow Condensed/DM Mono fonts). No component library. `react-router-dom` v6 (data router) for navigation.
- **Backend:** Supabase — Postgres with row-level security on every table, Auth (email OTP), Realtime for live-session sync.
- **Deploy:** Vercel, auto-deploys `main` to production.
- **Bundle splitting** (Phase 2 continued): `FounderMetricsScreen.jsx` (the only consumer of `recharts`, by far the heaviest single dependency at ~9MB unminified with its `d3-*` subpackages) is `React.lazy()`-loaded behind `FounderAdminRoute`'s existing `isAdmin` check, instead of a static top-level import — a non-admin's browser now never fetches that chunk at all (confirmed via network requests: zero requests for it on a non-admin visit to `/admin/metrics`, which still correctly redirects to `/`). `vite.config.js` also splits `react`/`react-dom`/`react-router-dom`/`@supabase/supabase-js` into a separate `vendor` chunk via `manualChunks`, so it can stay cached across deploys that only touch app code. Net effect on a normal user's first-load gzip: one ~377KB chunk before → ~121KB (vendor) + ~140KB (app) ≈ 261KB after, a ~31% cut, with the ~116KB recharts/d3 chunk now deferred entirely for everyone except the founder-admin. Next candidates if further cuts are wanted: lazy-loading Terms/Privacy/FAQ (low-traffic, self-contained), and possibly splitting `@dnd-kit` out of `ActivityConfigs.jsx` (used by Builder/Library, so a smaller win, deferred for now).
- `src/App.jsx` — still the largest file; holds the router, top-level `App()`, and several screens not yet split out (`TeamsScreen`, `BuilderScreen`, `PlayerProfile`, `RostersTab`, `AuthScreen`, `NameScreen`). Other major screens live under `src/components/`.
- `src/supabase.js` — the entire data-access layer. Every DB read/write goes through a named export here; nothing calls `supabase.from(...)` directly from a component.
- `src/constants.js` — pure helpers + shared constants (`uid()`, `TEAM_COLORS`, grouping math, `INIT` shape, `localDateStr()`).
- `src/Layout.jsx` — the authenticated app shell: global bottom tab bar, team-workspace top tab row, live-resume bar.
- `supabase/migrations/*.sql` — 147 migrations as of this session, each dated and mostly with inline comments explaining *why*, not just *what*. Applied via `npx supabase db push` (CLI is linked); some early ones were applied via the raw Management API and later reconciled with `migration repair`.
- `supabase/functions/*` — Edge Functions (Deno). First one added 2026-07-10 (`notify-team-staff-added`); a second (`notify-org-invite`) followed with the org model. A `_shared/emailTemplate.ts` holds the common Resend-email formatting both use. Secrets set via the Management API, never committed to migrations.
- `scripts/import-public-library.mjs` — one-time/rerunnable Node import script for the public drill catalog (see Library section below).

## Feature inventory (what actually works today)

### Auth & account
- Email OTP sign-in (code, not magic link — magic links broke for the installed-homescreen-PWA case). Branded email via Resend SMTP for both regular sign-in (`magic_link` template) and first-ever signup (`confirmation` template, added seventeenth session — Supabase routes a brand-new email's first `signInWithOtp()` through this template, not `magic_link`, and it had no custom template before, so new coaches got Supabase's stock unbranded/link-based default instead of the branded code).
- One-time name capture on first sign-in (covers pre-existing accounts too). First name required (asterisk), last name optional (no "(optional)" suffix, a small "* required" note instead) — reworded seventeenth session.
- Soft account deactivation ("Deactivate Account" section in Settings > Account, no longer labeled "Danger Zone") and team deletion. Reactivation is no longer silent: signing in on a deactivated account now shows a blocking prompt (reactivate, or exit back out) before the rest of the app renders, replacing the old auto-clear-on-sign-in behavior that surprised the one real user in testing.

### Navigation
- Global bottom tabs: **Home / Teams / Library**, plus a gear icon (not a tab) to `/settings`. This replaced an earlier chips-on-Home team picker and a Manage-tab junk drawer — see Session Log.
- Inside a team workspace (`/team/:teamId/*`), the tab bar switches to a flattened horizontal top row of 4 direct routes: **Schedule / Roster / Equipment / Goals & Insights**. There is no more nested Plan/Team toggle and no Build tab — a `/team/:teamId/build` route existed as dead code (superseded by Home/Schedule's own "+ Practice" entry points) until this session removed it.
- A persistent live-resume bar renders across every route (not just certain screens) whenever a live session is in progress and the coach has navigated away without ending it.
- **Active-tab underline** (seventeenth session) on the 3 global bottom tabs — color alone wasn't clear enough per direct feedback. While inside `/team/:teamId/*`, the Teams tab now shows as active (previously none of the three did, since that route doesn't literally start with `/teams`) — tapping it already correctly returned to `/teams` since `onClick` always navigates to the fixed path, so only the `active` check needed fixing. The global `/schedule` route ("My Schedule" from Home) had the same gap (left every tab dark) until the twenty-seventh session counted it as part of Home. Underline thickness increased 50% (2px→3px) the same session, both here and on the team-workspace top tab row, for better visibility on a PWA install.
- **Team workspace back button**: the team-color header strip had no persistent way back to the Teams list (a deliberate omission from an earlier nav restructure — "the bottom Teams tab already covers it") — direct feedback said that wasn't enough. Added one in the same left slot `subViewBack` uses for a drilled-in sub-view (Practice Detail, etc.); `subViewBack` still takes priority when set, un-drilling one level first.

### Teams & roster
- Teams have sport, timezone, season dates, and a color (auto-assigned from a curated palette, editable, includes black/dark gray) — shown as a Teams-list stripe and as the team-workspace header-bar background.
- The header bar's ellipsis is the only way to Edit Team; Delete Team (with a confirm step) lives inside the edit modal. Hidden entirely (`canManageTeamInMode`) for a coach viewing a team they can't actually manage (e.g. an assistant), so it never implies edit access they don't have.
- Players, staff (head coach/assistant/helper roles), structured "Player Focus" areas (renamed from "Skill Notes") — skill-tag taxonomy + coach freeform sub-tags.
- Team creator is automatically head coach (trigger on team insert, personal teams only; one-time backfill already run for pre-existing teams).
- **Staff invite linking**: adding a coach by email (`add_team_staff` RPC) silently links to an existing account if one exists; a `claim_pending_team_staff` trigger auto-links any pending invite the moment the invited person signs up. Re-adding someone who left revives their archived row instead of duplicating.
- Invite notification email via a `pg_net`-triggered Edge Function + Resend (never Supabase's `admin.inviteUserByEmail`, which is a magic link).
- One-time welcome card + self-serve `leave_team` RPC (narrow — archives only the caller's own row, refuses for team owners). The reverse direction exists too: `leave_team` also writes a `team_departures` row, surfaced to whoever can manage the team as a Home notification ("{name} ({role}) left {team}", Acknowledge/View Coaches) via `acknowledge_team_departure` — a head-coach-initiated removal (`archiveStaff`) does NOT create one, only a self-initiated leave.
- **A third one, symmetric with the two above, for accepting an invite** (twenty-seventh session, direct feedback: this used to be silent beyond the roster growing): `accept_team_invite` now also writes a `team_join_notices` row (`20260803010000_team_join_notices.sql`, same shape/RLS/ack pattern as `team_departures`), surfaced on the head coach's Home as "{name} accepted your invite to {team} as a{n} {role}. Set up what you share and delegate with them." with **Set Up Permissions** and **Dismiss**. Set Up Permissions deep-links to `/team/:teamId/roster` with `location.state.openPermissionsForUserId` (same `location.state` deep-link convention Settings' Terms/Privacy back button already established) — `RostersTab` reads it once on mount, switches to the Coaches tab, and auto-opens `PermissionsModal` for that exact coach. Verified live end-to-end with two real accounts: invited, accepted, confirmed the notification's copy/team/role, clicked through, and confirmed the Permissions modal opened pre-targeted at the right coach with both toggles correctly defaulting off.
- Staff-add suggestion chips (tap-to-fill staff already added on your other teams, deduped by email).
- Role-aware UI: assistants/helpers view everything and can run live sessions, but Builder/schedule-wizard/team-settings/staff-management entry points are hidden for teams they don't head-coach — checked per-team (`isHeadCoach`), not globally.
- **Home's "Your Teams" card row shows the caller's per-team role** (Head Coach/Assistant Coach/Helper, low-emphasis text, via the existing `myTeamRole()` — no new query) — Coach mode only, hidden in Org mode where the row lists every team in the org regardless of the viewer's own role on each one.
- **Settings → "My Team Assignments"** already listed every team a coach is on (any role, any org) with a `show_on_home` toggle and the same role label — this was never actually Org-mode-gated despite an earlier assumption that it was; nothing needed building here.

### Organizations (Coach/Org mode)
- `org_staff` is the org-membership table. Today it has exactly **one** role: the CHECK constraint only allows `'director'` — `admin` was proposed in the original design as a future extension (adding it later is a one-line constraint change, not a migration) but does not exist as a usable value yet. Don't assume an `admin` role is already live anywhere.
- A persisted (localStorage), per-device **Coach/Org mode toggle** on Home: Coach mode shows your own teams across every org; Org mode shows every team in one selected org plus a weekly-practices rollup. Only Home/Teams/Library change per mode — team-workspace screens never do. Labeled "Coach Mode"/"Organization Mode" (seventeenth session, was bare "Coach"/"Organization").
- **Org mode no longer reverts to Coach mode on reload, fixed** (Phase 2 continued): `App.jsx`'s guard effect that resets a persisted-but-now-invalid org selection back to Coach mode was gated only on `library.myOrgs` being present — but that array's own `useState` default is `[]`, which is already truthy and already fails `.some(...)`, so the guard fired and wrongly reset a perfectly valid Org mode selection on every fresh page load, well before `refreshLibrary()`'s real fetch had a chance to populate it. Fixed by also requiring `loaded` (the existing combined-fetch-gate flag, already true only once teams/library/planning have all resolved at least once) before the guard runs. Verified live: set Org mode via `localStorage`, reloaded twice in a row, confirmed it stayed in Org mode both times (previously reverted to Coach mode every time).
- **Org creation gated** (seventeenth session): Settings' "+ Create Organization" was instant self-serve for anyone with no approval step. Regular coaches now see a "Request a Consultation" card instead, in Settings > Account under "Membership" (moved there in the twentieth session; originally on the main Settings list) -- a real form (name/email/phone/team count, plus encouraged free text), not a mailto link, since the twentieth session. Only founder-admins (existing `is_admin()` gate, same one used for Public Library curation) get the real create-org form, still on the main Settings list, which also takes an optional director email and sends them a real `org_invites` director invite right away (`orgInviteCoach(...,"director")`) so the admin can hand off control immediately rather than needing to touch `org_staff` rows by hand.
- **`org_create_team` no longer auto-inserts a `team_staff` head_coach row** (Phase 2, seventeenth-session follow-up): the insert added by `20260721050000_org_create_team_head_coach.sql` is removed (`20260730060000_org_create_team_no_auto_head_coach.sql`) — a director overseeing an org team manages it purely through org membership now, matching `can_manage_team`'s server-side RLS, which already had this branch. This was previously deferred because `RostersTab`, `GoalsScreen`, `PracticeDetail`, `ScheduleScreen`, `SeriesWizard`, and `SchedulePracticeModal` all gated management on personal `isHeadCoach`/`myTeamRole`, not the mode-aware `canManageTeamInMode` (which already existed but was only actually used by `HomeScreen.jsx`). All six now use `canManageTeamInMode(team, coachId, mode)` instead, so a director sees +Add Player/Coach, can edit Goals, and can schedule for an org team with zero personal `team_staff` rows there. Verified live (fresh director → new org team → zero `team_staff` rows → Roster/Schedule/Goals all correctly open for editing).
- Org Details screen: name, sports (multi-select, see below), color, member list with an ellipsis-menu for add/remove/role-change ("Add Org Member" — an earlier "Invite Coach" label was renamed once it stopped being a no-op).
- **Orgs are multi-sport** (Phase 2, seventeenth-session continued): `organizations.sport` (a single nullable text column, added 2026-07-22) replaced with `organizations.sports text[] not null default '{}'` (`20260730070000_org_multi_sport.sql`, existing single values backfilled into one-element arrays before the old column was dropped — no parallel columns left behind, on purpose, per the stale-column drift this project already hit once with `team_goals`/`get_team_goal_report`). Org Details' edit form is a multi-select chip row (`SPORTS.map` toggle buttons, not a dropdown) instead of a single "-- Select a sport --" picker. Adding a team inside an org that has sports configured now restricts *and* defaults the new team's Sport field to just that org's sports (`ModalLayer.jsx`'s addTeam form); an org with no sports configured yet (empty array) falls back to the old unrestricted full-sport-list behavior. Not server-enforced (`org_create_team`'s `p_sport` still accepts any text) — sport isn't a security dimension here, same reasoning as every other client-gated UX convention in this app.
- **Coach invite flow** uses real consent, not the informal `team_staff` auto-link pattern: `org_invites` (pending/accepted/declined) → `accept_org_invite`/`decline_org_invite`/`cancel_org_invite`, with the caller's email matched server-side against `auth.jwt()`, never client-asserted.
- **Multi-org drill sharing** via `activity_library_org_shares` (a many-to-many join table, not a single-org column) + batch RPC `set_drill_org_shares` (all-or-nothing). `promote_drill_to_org_library` does a full-copy fork (director-only) — not a live reference.
- Library has a 5-tab sub-nav (Drills, Templates, Locations, Equipment, Skill Tags), each mode-filtered.
- Settings has "My Team Assignments": every team across every org, with a per-team `show_on_home` toggle to hide a team from Home without leaving it.
- **Known, unfixed gap**: Builder's own team picker is not mode-scoped (shows all teams regardless of Coach/Org mode).
- **Org equipment auto-merges into a team's equipment picker when the location matches** (Phase 2, seventeenth-session continued): Builder/live-run's per-activity and per-station equipment pickers (`ActConfig`/`StationConfig` in `ActivityConfigs.jsx`) used to filter `data.assets` by sport and location only, with **no owner/org check at all** — under RLS that meant every asset for *any* org the coach belonged to leaked into every practice's equipment picker regardless of team or location, the same class of bug already fixed once this session in `EquipmentTab`'s Team-tab view. A new `ownedOrOrgAtLoc(asset,coachId,team,loc)` helper now scopes both pickers to the coach's own equipment, plus — only if this specific team belongs to an org and the asset is tagged to the activity's own location — that org's shared equipment too, so a coach can use the club's on-site gear without switching into Org mode. An org asset with no location tags at all does not merge in this way (untagged isn't "everywhere" here, unlike a coach's own untagged equipment); an activity with no location set gets none of the org merge either. Verified live: a director's org-owned "Club Cones" (tagged to a "Club Gym" location) appeared in a Station's equipment picker for a practice scheduled there, while a second org asset tagged to a different location did not.
- **Org name shown on team cards** (seventeenth session): Home's "Your Teams" cards and the Teams tab list now show which org a team belongs to (Coach mode only — redundant in Org mode, which already says the org's name once at the top). Needed a small `organizations_select_member` RLS relaxation (`20260730030000_org_name_visible_to_team_members.sql`) — previously only an org_staff member or the org's creator could read the org's own name row, so an assistant/head coach who's plain `team_staff` on an org team (not personally in `org_staff`) got `null` back for it.
- **Leave-team bug, fixed**: the global `/schedule` route (`ScheduleLegacyRoute`) passed raw, unscoped `data.teams`/`data.practices` straight through, unlike Home/Teams which already scope via `teamsForMode`/`homeTeamsForMode`. A team a coach had genuinely left (their own `team_staff` row archived) stayed visible there as a filterable option and kept showing its practices, because `can_access_team`'s RLS also grants access via org membership independent of that row (correct, by design, for org oversight) — the fix was scoping the client, not the RLS.
- **Added-to-team banner redesigned**: Home's "You've been added to X" card no longer has a raw "Leave" link — it's now "Accept" (local dismiss only; `welcomed_at` is already set the moment the banner would show) and "Manage Team Assignments" (deep-links into Settings' assignments section via `location.state.openSection`, same mechanism Terms/Privacy's back button uses to return to Settings > Account instead of Home).
- There is no standalone Org Home page — it existed briefly (`/org/:orgId`, `OrgHomeScreen.jsx`) but was retired and folded directly into `HomeScreen.jsx` under Org mode. Don't recreate it as a separate route.
- **Club Library carries the org's own color** (Phase 2, seventeenth-session continued): a 4px colored bar under the "Club Library" title, same `activeOrg.color` already used for the Org-mode bottom tab bar (Layout.jsx) — "so it looks like their org" applied consistently in a second place. No bar renders if the org hasn't picked a color (falls back to nothing, not a default color).
- **Per-team Equipment tab dropped in Org mode** (Phase 2, same session): the team workspace's tab row (`Layout.jsx`'s `teamWorkspaceTabs`) no longer shows Equipment when in Org mode — a club's equipment is now managed centrally from Club Library's own Equipment tab, not duplicated at the team level. `TeamEquipmentRoute` (`App.jsx`) redirects to that team's Schedule tab if reached directly while in Org mode (stale link/back-nav), the same guard pattern already used there for a since-deleted team. Coach mode is unaffected — the tab is still there for a coach managing their own team's equipment.

### Library
- Drills, equipment/assets, templates — coach-owned, with org-sharing (see above) and manual drill reordering.
- **"Add Drill Anyway" on the equipment-mismatch dialog no longer silently deletes the missing equipment reference — it's kept, flagged, and surfaced everywhere the drill/practice shows equipment** (thirty-second session, direct feedback). `assets.acquired` (boolean, default `true`, `20260805020000_assets_acquired_flag.sql`) distinguishes "coach genuinely owns this" from "a placeholder created because a copied/added drill called for it and the coach chose Add Anyway." `resolveEquipmentAgainstPool` (`supabase.js`, shared by `copyDrillToMyLibrary` and Builder's add-to-practice flow) branches on whether the equipment id already belongs to the caller's own pool: if so, it updates that asset's `acquired` flag in place (never duplicates); if it's from a foreign source (another coach/org/catalog), it creates a new asset in the caller's pool with `acquired` set per the coach's choice. `findMissingEquipment` treats `acquired:false` as still-missing, so the mismatch dialog re-triggers on a later add until the coach actually resolves it. Surfaced with amber "· Need to acquire" chip styling plus an inline "✓ Got it" (calls `updateAsset(id,{acquired:true})`) in the Edit Drill modal's Team Equipment/Player Gear pickers (`ModalLayer.jsx`), in Practice Detail's equipment summary and per-activity/per-station equipment lines (red bold + "NOT ACQUIRED"/"(not acquired)", `PracticeDetail.jsx`), and on the public Practice Preview link (`get_preview_view` RPC now returns `{name,acquired}` objects instead of bare name strings, `20260805030000_preview_view_equipment_acquired.sql`; rendered as a red-tinted chip with "· not acquired" in `CommandScreen.jsx`'s `PreviewView`). Builder's add-to-practice equipment-mismatch dialog (`EquipmentMismatchDialog`, `context="practice"`) already existed from a prior session and needed no changes — it already re-triggers correctly now that `findMissingEquipment` treats an unacquired match as still-missing.
- **Library "+ Build Practice" now offers a real choice** (seventeenth session): used to always jump straight into an empty unscheduled build (`goToBuilder(null)`). Now asks first — build an unscheduled practice (unchanged Save/Template/Run Now flow) or pick an upcoming scheduled practice to plan instead, via a new `SchedulePracticePicker` (agenda/month view, read-only picker, hands off straight to Builder — deliberately not a reuse of the full `ScheduleScreen`, which owns its own PracticeDetail drill-in that isn't what a row-tap here should do).
- **Equipment duplication/leak, fixed** (seventeenth session): a real production bug, root-caused live — the Team-tab Equipment view (`EquipmentTab` with `sportFilter` set) filtered the *entire* RLS-scoped `data.assets` array by sport only, no owner/org check at all. That leaked in Public Library catalog equipment (rows with `source_catalog_id` set, made visible to every coach by a deliberate RLS carve-out for catalog content) alongside any other coach's owned assets that happened to share a sport — this is what produced "9 baseball items already there, cones twice" for a coach/org that had added none. Fixed by reusing the same `visibleEquipment()` scoping the Library tab's own Equipment view already used (own/org, catalog rows excluded), narrowed to the one team's sport. `TeamEquipmentRoute` also needed `mode` threaded through, which it wasn't receiving before.
- **Skill tags**: `skill_categories` exists today for **6 sports** — Baseball and Basketball (seeded 2026-07-07) plus Football, Soccer, Lacrosse, and Volleyball (added 2026-07-18 alongside the Public Library taxonomy). Softball, Hockey, Tennis, and Swimming (the rest of the app's 12-entry sport list in `constants.js`) still have zero categories. All 6 categorized sports now work identically: real `scope='global'` tag rows, visible to every coach immediately, no per-coach action needed, and not individually archivable by any one coach (consistent across all 6 — see Session Log for the Baseball/Basketball duplication this replaced). `ensureDefaultSkillTags` (client-side, runs on every sign-in) still exists and is still called, but is currently a no-op for every sport — kept in place rather than removed, in case a future sport ever wants true per-coach starter tags again.
- `SkillTagPicker`: selected tags show as removable chips; an "Add/Edit Skill Tags" button opens a full category-grouped, searchable picker in a bottom-sheet overlay. **Search and "add a new tag" merged into one field** (thirtieth session, direct feedback): used to be two separate inputs — a search box at the top, and a name field + category select + Add button in their own row below every category's chip list. On mobile, that bottom row could end up hidden behind the on-screen keyboard with no way to scroll it into view, and it was easy to tap the sheet's own Done button (right at the top) instead of Add, silently losing whatever had been typed. Now: typing something that doesn't exactly match an existing tag shows a compact category-select + "+ Add '{text}'" affordance immediately below the search box itself — always on-screen, no scrolling past results required. Both "Done" buttons (top-right and the bottom primary one) route through one `tryClose()` check that confirms (`window.confirm`) before discarding unsubmitted, addable text.
- **Public Library** (built and shipped — the planning doc for this feature was written before implementation and is now stale on this point): a `content_catalogs` table backs a search-first Public Library browser (`PublicLibraryScreen.jsx`, 2026-07-19) — pick a sport first, never a dump of every drill; working Source/Publisher filters (currently one option each, future-proofed for multi-publisher catalogs); founder-admin add/edit/archive affordances built in. Seeded via `scripts/import-public-library.mjs` from the per-sport JSON files in the repo root (`ROP-Public-Library-*.json`). `copyDrillToMyLibrary` resolves a copied drill's equipment into the recipient's own asset pool by name-match-or-create; it does **not** currently copy skill tags of any scope onto the copy (a known gap, not yet fixed).
- **My Library publisher filter** (2026-07-29): `activity_library` now has `copied_from_owner_user_id`/`copied_from_organization_id`/`copied_from_catalog_id`, set once by `copyDrillToMyLibrary` at copy time — otherwise a drill copied from an assistant's shared library, an org library, or the Public Library became indistinguishable from a self-authored row the moment it landed in "My Library." `NewLibraryScreen.jsx`'s "mine" shelf now shows a "From {publisher}" line on copied drills and a Publisher section in the existing Filter modal (multi-select, alongside skill tags). Lineage only, same convention as `template_activities.library_activity_id` — never updated after the copy, and the FKs are `on delete set null` so a since-deleted source doesn't block the copy.
- **Library restructured: Explore lives under Drills only, and its per-org shelf is now per-coach ("Team Libraries")** (Phase 2, seventeenth-session continued): the 5-tab content-type row (Drills/Templates/Locations/Equipment/Skill Tags) is always visible now — it used to hide behind a page-wide My-Library-vs-Explore pill, so switching to Explore hid Templates/Locations/Equipment/Skill Tags entirely even though Explore never applied to any of them (only Drills has cross-coach/org content to browse). That pill moved down into just the Drills tab, relabeled "My Drills"/"Org Drills" vs. "Team Libraries." Within Team Libraries, the old flat "From {org}" shelf (every coach's shared drills lumped together) is now one shelf per coach who's shared something, labeled with their name and count (e.g. "Jane Smith (3)") — a director can now tell whose library is whose. Coach-name resolution deliberately checks `team_staff` (which stores names directly) as a fallback after `profiles`, since `profiles_select_org_co_member` only resolves a fellow *org_staff* member's name — a coach who's merely `team_staff` on one of the org's teams (the more common case) wouldn't otherwise resolve to anything but "A coach."
- **`copyDrillToMyLibrary` is mode-aware** (Phase 2, same session): copying a drill while in Org mode now lands it in the org's library (`organization_id` set, `owner_user_id` null) and resolves its equipment against the org's own asset pool instead of the director's personal one — previously every copy went to the copier's personal library regardless of mode, so a director "copying to the club" from Org mode was quietly building a personal collection instead. Button/screen copy read "Copy to Org Library" instead of "Copy to My Library" when in Org mode (`NewLibraryScreen.jsx` and `PublicLibraryScreen.jsx`, both driven by the same `doCopy`). Verified live: copying a coach's shared drill while in Org mode produced a row with `organization_id` set and `owner_user_id` null, visible on the "Org Drills" shelf with "From A coach" attribution.
- **Peer library sharing (Permissions) now defaults to off** (twenty-seventh session, correcting the twenty-fifth session's original default): `team_staff.head_coach_shares_library`/`assistant_shares_library` default `false`, not `true` — a head coach no longer automatically sees an assistant's drills (or vice versa) the moment an invite is accepted. Reaching another coach's drills is still fully possible, just opt-in: Library → Explore → find their shelf → copy in what's wanted, or either side turns sharing on from Permissions. The drill-menu toggle for a single drill's own private flag is now labeled "Keep Private"/"Make Public" (was "Hide from My Coaches"/"Share with My Coaches" — "Make Private" was already taken in the same menu for the unrelated org-share-clearing action, hence "Keep"). Going public shows a real confirm prompt first ("This drill will be visible to coaches you share your library with."). A small lock icon now sits next to a private drill's name on its card for at-a-glance visibility.
- **Drill card heat-color icon removed** (twenty-seventh session, direct feedback: "too distracting"): usage insights are reachable only via the drill's ellipsis menu ("View Drill Insights") now, never a colored icon on the card itself. The heat-tier math/colors (`drillUsageHeatTier`) still exist in `constants.js` for potential reuse but nothing currently renders them.
- **Drill list sort** (twenty-seventh session): a `Sort` picker above "My Drills"/"Org Drills" — Custom (drag-to-reorder, unchanged default), Most Frequently Used (trailing-12-month completed-use counts, the same batch summary the removed heat icon used to consume), Alphabetical, and Suggested (ranks a drill by the largest goal deficit — target minus current — among the categories any of its tags roll up to, for a team the coach picks if they have more than one; a team with no goals or a drill with no matching tags/categories sorts last). Only Custom keeps the drag handle active; the other three are derived, not persisted. **Restyled to match the filter row** (thirty-second session continued, direct feedback: it looked out of place next to the app's other buttons): the separate label+`<select>` row is gone — the sort `<select>` now renders as a `btn ghost` merged into the same flex row as Filter/+Add Drill, with shortened "Sort: Custom"/"Sort: Most Used"/"Sort: Alphabetical"/"Sort: Suggested" option labels; the conditional suggested-team picker got the same treatment.
- **Untagged-drills deep link from Goals & Insights** (twenty-ninth session): `NewLibraryScreen.jsx` accepts `location.state.untaggedForSport`/`teamId`/`returnTo` and, when present, scopes "My Drills" to that one sport and (by default, toggleable) to drills with zero skill tags — plus a "Back to Goals & Insights" button that returns to the exact team/tab the coach came from. Tagging a drill via the existing ellipsis → Edit → Skill Tags flow needs no extra plumbing to "return" the coach to the untagged list: since the filter is a plain derived value recomputed from `data.activityLibrary` on every render, a drill that just got tagged simply falls out of the filtered list the next time it re-renders, in place, without any navigation at all. Empty state reads "All caught up -- every {sport} drill has a skill tag" specifically for this mode (vs. the generic "No drills match the selected filters" every other filter combination uses).
- **`TemplateWorkspace` margins and inline location creation** (thirtieth session, direct feedback: "revise the create template screen to match our other experiences aka modern builder"): the screen's wrapping div (`NewLibraryScreen.jsx`'s `if(editingTpl)return...`) had zero horizontal padding at all — every other Library sub-screen uses `padding:"0 16px"` at minimum, so this one alone read edge-to-edge. Fixed to match. The Default Location field also had no way to add a location inline — a coach whose real location wasn't in the dropdown yet had to abandon the template, go add it from the Locations tab, then come back. Now offers the exact same "+ Add New Location..." dropdown option (real locations exist) / "+ Add a Location" fallback button (none exist yet) that Builder's own Location field already does, opening the same `AddLocationDialog` and auto-selecting the new location on save.

### Planning & scheduling
- **Builder**: practice/template editor — drills, checklist activities (7 selectable "Practice Components" types: Intro/Closer/Checklist/Water Break/Stretch/Station Block/Other, customizable per coach via a persisted `localStorage` picker, collapsible section), station blocks with rotation (each station individually collapsible), grouping (whole/partners/N groups), equipment, coaching points, per-activity coach/sublocation assignment. My Drill Library is collapsible too. A newly-added activity auto-expands and stays open until the coach taps elsewhere (anywhere outside it collapses it). The Run of Practice section is solid dark green (a measured backdrop, not a literal wrapping box, so the just-added row's "stay pinned while scrolling" sticky behavior has real room to work) with an animated stopwatch mark that spins once per activity added/removed. Practice Details collapses by default for an already-scheduled practice (open by default for a fresh build), takes on the team's own color, and includes an editable Date/Duration for a scheduled practice. Long-press-to-preview existed for one session and was removed -- it was being confused with scroll gestures on touch.
- **Practice Components defaults for a new coach** (thirty-second session, direct feedback): `getVisibleComponentTypes()` (`constants.js`) falls back to the 3 components flagged `defaultOn:true` (Intro/Closer/Station Block) rather than showing all 7 the first time a coach opens Builder. The 2-column grid's blank cell on an odd count (3, 5, or 7 visible types) is now light-green helper text ("Tap the ⋯ above to edit your default practice components") instead of empty space, and reappears any time the coach's own selection count goes back to odd — a coach who explicitly picks an even number sees a normal filled grid with no filler cell.
- **Builder duration pill is tap-to-toggle** (thirty-second session, direct feedback): the existing `.pill`/`.pill.over`/`.pill.exceeds` "35/60 min" display in Builder's Run of Practice header is now a real `<button>` when the practice has a target duration (`scheduledDurationMinutes` set) — tapping it swaps to "Needs N more min" (or, once over, "N min over"), tapping again swaps back. Purely a local `useState` toggle, nothing persisted; a practice with no target duration keeps the old static, non-interactive span (there's nothing to toggle to). The read-only agenda-list version of this pill (Home, Schedule) is unchanged — only Builder's own copy, which the coach is actively editing against, got the toggle.
- **Run Now disabled in Builder more than an hour out** (thirty-second session, direct feedback): a scheduled practice's Run Now button in Builder (`App.jsx`'s `BuilderScreen`) is now `disabled` with a `title` tooltip ("Run Now unlocks within 1 hour of the scheduled time") whenever `scheduledAt` is more than 60 minutes in the future. Practice Detail's own Run Now button (the one on the non-editing practice-overview screen) is intentionally untouched — the user's request was specifically about Builder.
- **Cancel-practice confirm dialog reworded** (thirty-second session, direct feedback): the em dash in the body copy and the ambiguous "This Only"/"This & Future" button pairing are gone. Body now reads "This marks the slot as cancelled. The plan stays saved." with no punctuation trick; buttons are "Never Mind" / "Cancel Practice" (single occurrence) or "Never Mind" / "Cancel This Practice" / "Cancel This & Future" (a series occurrence) — every button is a real verb phrase, never a bare scope label. Same `PracticeDetail.jsx` confirm box used from both Schedule and Home.
- **Recurring schedules**: a wizard (days of week → time/duration → date range defaulted from team season → location → preview with per-date deselect) backed by `create_practice_series` — atomic, server-capped (≤150 occurrences, ≤400 day range), timezone-correct (DST-safe via `AT TIME ZONE`).
- **Home screen**: adaptive hero card for the next practice (Plan/Review/Run depending on state), and an "Upcoming Practices" list showing the **next 4 practices by date, unbounded by any rolling window** (thirty-second session, direct feedback — previously a 7-day window plus a separate "N practices in the next 14 days need a plan" nudge button; both are gone, replaced by this one always-populated list of exactly the next 4, planned or not, however far out the 4th one falls — "My Schedule" is the way to see anything beyond that).
- **Smart Join Practice / Run Now button** (thirty-second session continued, direct feedback, `findActiveLiveSession` + `isSessionLive` state added to `HomeScreen.jsx`, `PracticeDetail.jsx`, and Builder's `BuilderScreen` in `App.jsx`): any Run Now/Start Practice button now checks whether a live session already exists for that practice and, if so, relabels itself "Join Practice" and routes into the existing session (`/run/:practiceId`) instead of racing to create a new one — this is the direct fix for the reported bug (an assistant's "Run Practice" tap force-starting the drill timer out from under the head coach mid-setup). Home's hero button row was also simplified from a 3-way Practice Setup/Review Plan/Start Practice conditional split down to a fixed `[Review Plan][smart primary button]` pair, since Practice Setup is no longer a separate destination from the live session itself (see the Live Sessions section above).
- **Home hero's "nothing on the schedule" empty state, made defensive** (thirty-second session continued): direct feedback described the hero showing an empty-schedule message while the Schedule tab visibly had practices on it — code review found no reproducible date-window bug behind this specific framing (the user wasn't sure of the exact repro either), so rather than guess at or remove the unrelated per-team `show_on_home` toggle, a `nextCancelledPractice` fallback was added: when there's no plannable next practice but the soonest upcoming one is cancelled, the hero now shows a "View Practice" card for it instead of the flat "nothing scheduled" empty state, which was the one confirmed case where the old copy could read as misleading.
- **`GettingStartedCard` no longer flashes on every Home load for a coach who already completed it** (thirty-second session continued, direct feedback): `hasCompleted`'s initial state changed from `false` to `null` (loading/unknown, not "not done yet"), and `checklistDone` now treats `null` as "don't render the incomplete-checklist UI yet" — previously the card's own fetch hadn't resolved on first paint, so every load briefly rendered the full checklist (since `false` looked identical to "genuinely incomplete") before flipping to hidden a moment later once the real completed-status came back.
- **Schedule tab**: Agenda (day-grouped, team-color-coded dots) and Month (calendar grid with per-day dots) views, both using the same hollow-when-unplanned/filled-when-planned dot rule. **Agenda's own "Show/Hide Completed / History" section and its `showPast` state are removed** (thirty-second session continued, direct feedback — history now lives solely in Goals & Insights' rebuilt History view, see that section); Month view keeps its existing past-practice access via `DaySheet`/`HistoryViewer` unchanged, since the request was scoped specifically to the Agenda tab.
- **Planned absences**: capture from Practice Detail, Player Profile, or a Home quick action. Feeds Builder's default group assignment and the live session's attendance defaults. **Enforced inside station-block editing too, not just at add-time** (thirty-second session, direct feedback): `StationConfig` (`ActivityConfigs.jsx`) now takes `absentPlayerIds` and disables that player's chip in every station's player picker (grayed out, strikethrough name, "Out" badge, `title` tooltip, `cursor:not-allowed`) rather than only relying on the assignment defaults computed when the activity was first added — a player marked out *after* a station block already had assignments now also gets auto-removed from any station they were sitting in, via an effect keyed on the absent-id set. `genRandom`/`groupByPosition`/`groupByHand` all draw from an `assignablePlayers` list with the absent players already filtered out, so neither random nor attribute-based grouping can assign an out player either.
- Cancel (this-occurrence or this-and-future for a series) / restore; a "missed" state for practices whose time passed with no run.
- All practice times display in the **team's** timezone, never the viewing device's; "today" is always computed via `localDateStr()` (local calendar day), never `.toISOString()`.
- **Planning-depth indicator**: any practice with a `scheduled_duration_minutes` shows "35/60 min" whether planned or not. Three states, purely derived (`planningState()`/`sumMins()`), never stored: red under 90% planned, green at or above it, and — added twenty-seventh session, direct feedback — amber/orange ("exceeds") when the planned drills actually add up to *more* than the scheduled duration, distinct from the under-planned case since running long is a different problem worth its own color. The green state keeps a `✓` glyph; red/amber deliberately have **no** icon (see Session Log for why not `⚠`). Builder's own duration pill (`.pill.over`/now also `.pill.exceeds`) got the same third state.
- **Getting-started checklist** (reworked seventeenth session): was a "?"-icon popup with a green-dot nudge and static, non-clickable rows — direct feedback was that a brand-new coach's first Home screen wasn't intuitive about how to proceed. Now a persistent `GettingStartedCard` on Home itself (gone once every step is done), same 6 steps (team → players → library → schedule → first plan → run live), but each row (except "Run it live," which has no single screen of its own to jump to) is real navigation to the right screen. The green dot on "?" is gone since the card itself is now the visible nudge; "Getting Started" was also removed from the "?" menu (now just FAQs + Send Feedback) since the checklist isn't a popup anymore.
- **New `/faq` page** (seventeenth session, `LegalPages.jsx`, same top-level-sibling-route treatment as Terms/Privacy): natural-voice accordion FAQ, includes "Can I delete my account?" (deactivation vs. contact-us-to-actually-delete) and "How do I set up an organization with multiple teams?" (links to the consultation request above).
- **Missing-location dialogs** (seventeenth session): a coach with zero locations had no inline way to add one from Builder (empty `<select>`, no placeholder) or from the quick-schedule modal (which already had a working "None" option, just no way to add a real one). Both now offer a shared `AddLocationDialog` (`NewLibraryScreen.jsx`, exported) — name + optional sublocations in one step, auto-selects the new location and lets the coach continue what they were doing. Ownership (personal vs. org) is decided by the specific team being scheduled/built for (`team.organizationId`), not the global Coach/Org mode toggle, so it works correctly even through Builder's own known-not-mode-scoped team picker.
- **SeriesWizard consolidated** (seventeenth session): was 5 sequential screens (team → days/time → date range → location → preview); the first 4 (everything that's just picking fields) are now one "details" screen, with only the review/per-date-deselect step kept separate. Team-picker defaulting to the team you opened it from (via `presetTeamId`) already worked correctly before this change — confirmed, not touched.
- **Schedule empty state**: the global Schedule tab now prompts "No teams yet" with a "+ Create a Team" button when `data.teams.length===0`, instead of an empty calendar with no obvious next step.
- **Practice plan PDF export** (2026-07-29, `PracticePlanPrint.jsx`, triggered from a "Print / Export PDF" button on Practice Detail): renders a standalone, print-optimized document — not a screenshot of the app UI — and hands off to the browser's native Print/Save-as-PDF (no client-side PDF library, no server round trip). `#rop-print-root` + a new `@media print` block in App.jsx's `CSS` constant isolate that one subtree regardless of what else is mounted. Every drill shows duration **and** a computed clock start/end range (anchored to the practice's own start time, walking cumulatively through `actMins()` — same formula already used on-screen, so the printed total never diverges from what's displayed in-app), plus description, coaching focus, skill tags, equipment, player-gear notes, grouping, and per-player focus notes (resolved the same category-matching way the live view already does it). Station blocks get the same treatment per station. Intentionally reuses already-loaded client `data`/`team` state rather than a new RPC — no new data-access surface, just a different render of what Practice Detail already has.

### Goals & Insights
- Lives as a sub-toggle inside a team's Plan-equivalent workflow (folded together because "how this team spends its practice time" covered both, and Goals' original placeholder scope had never been built out separately).
- **Internal Overview/Trends/History navigation** (twenty-sixth session, `GoalsSubnav`): same rounded-pill sub-toggle already used for Coach/Org mode and My Drills/Team Libraries, generalized to three options. Overview keeps the existing goal editor, glance view, and (new) Next Practice Guidance; Trends is new (below); History is unchanged except its own detail view now also shows the Practice Execution Scorecard. The selected view is plain component state, so it naturally survives opening/closing a session from History within the same mount.
- **Side margins fixed** (twenty-ninth session): `TeamGoalsRoute` (`App.jsx`) was the only per-team route missing the `16px 16px` horizontal-padding wrapper that Roster/Equipment's routes both use — Goals & Insights only got the outer `.screen` container's 14px, reading noticeably tighter than its sibling tabs across all three subtabs. Added the same wrapper; removed the now-redundant bottom-padding-only div `GoalsScreen.jsx` had instead.
- **Shared attribution math, extracted once** (twenty-sixth session, `supabase/migrations/20260802000000_goal_attribution_shared_helpers.sql`): every new report below reuses the exact category-attribution rules `get_team_goal_report` already established — a multi-tag drill's minutes split evenly across its tags, a station's full `stationDuration` counted per-station (stations run in parallel, never divided), `'break'`-type activities excluded from the denominator entirely. Server-side, four small `security definer` SQL functions (`session_activity_minutes`, `practice_activity_planned_minutes`, `category_minutes_from_rows`, `total_attributable_minutes`, plus a `public.activity_minute_row` composite type) compose into every new RPC; all four have `revoke all ... from public` since they trust the caller's own authorization rather than checking `auth.uid()` themselves — they're internal building blocks, never meant to be called directly, and PostgREST would otherwise expose them as callable RPCs that leak another team's session timing. Client-side, `categoryMinutesForPracticeActivities`/`calculateGoalGapGuidance`/`calculateProjectedGoalImpact`/`classifyDurationVariance`/`summarizeCategoryTrend`/`drillUsageHeatTier` (all in `constants.js`) mirror the same rules in pure JS so Builder can project an *unsaved* draft locally without a round trip per keystroke.
- **Skill-Development Trends** (twenty-sixth session): a new `get_team_goal_trends(p_team_id, p_window_weeks)` RPC returns every category with a currently-saved goal, bucketed into real Monday–Sunday weeks in the *team's own timezone* (never device timezone, DST-safe via the same `AT TIME ZONE` pattern `create_practice_series` already uses), for the team's saved rolling window (1–12 weeks, same cap as the existing report). Each category renders as a `GoalTrendCard`: current target/rolling-planned/rolling-actual percentages (averaged from the same weekly data the chart plots — see the Gotcha below for why this wasn't true in the first draft), a percentage-point variance badge, a compact inline-SVG line chart (`WeeklyTrendChart` — solid Actual line, dashed Planned line, dotted flat Target reference, so the three read apart without relying only on color), and a deterministic plain-language trend summary (`summarizeCategoryTrend`: 3+ usable weeks required, a 3-consecutive-same-direction streak called out first, then latest-vs-earliest movement, falling back to a planned-vs-actual execution-gap message when the movement itself is flat). Empty weeks render as real gaps in the chart, never a misleadingly-connected line. Tapping a card expands a plain weekly table. **Deliberately different from `get_team_goal_report`**: that report's Planned bucket is forward-looking (what's currently scheduled); Trends' Planned bucket is backward-looking instead, computed from each *completed* session's own saved plan via `practice_activity_planned_minutes`, so Planned and Actual always compare the same practice cohort per week — this is a real, intentional divergence between the two RPCs' math, not a bug, and is why Trends never reuses `get_team_goal_report`'s own percentages for its card headlines. Empty states: no goals ("Set team goals to see development trends"), goals but no completed sessions ("Run a practice live to begin building actual-time trends"), both distinguished from the ordinary loading state.
- **Next Practice Guidance** (twenty-sixth session, Overview, `NextPracticeGuidance`): no new RPC — reuses `get_team_goal_report`'s own minute totals (it already returns real minutes, not just rounded percentages, in `planned_minutes`/`actual_minutes`/`denominators`). Uses rolling Actual when usable actual time exists, falls back to rolling Planned (labeled "Based on planned practice time until actual timing is available"), or the bare goal mix when neither exists yet. Duration comes from the next upcoming non-cancelled practice's `scheduledDurationMinutes`; when none exists, falls back to the team's own most recently scheduled practice's duration rather than guessing 60 minutes (this app has no existing default-duration convention, confirmed by grep before writing this) — with no duration at all, shows percentages and priorities only. Shows the three largest below-target categories (goal-mix minutes for the duration, plus minutes needed to fully close the current rolling gap when mathematically possible, capped at "this gap cannot be fully closed in one practice" when it exceeds the practice length) with a "Show all categories" expansion. Categories at/above target never get a negative recommendation. **Each category row expands on tap** (twenty-seventh session, direct feedback, `CategoryGapRow`/`drillsForCategory`): lists the coach's own library drills tagged to that category (name + duration), or "No drills in your library are tagged to {category} yet" when none match — read-only, no add-to-practice action from this screen.
- **Builder library switcher** (twenty-seventh session, direct feedback): the drill section at the bottom of Builder defaults to "My Library" (own drills only) instead of the previous flat merge of every accessible drill (own + org + any peer sharing with the coach, with no way to tell them apart). A dropdown appears only when more than one source exists for the team being built for — the team's own org (if any) and each peer currently sharing with the coach on that team, both derived from what's already in `data.activityLibrary` (already correctly RLS-scoped) via the same per-owner grouping technique `NewLibraryScreen`'s own Explore shelves use. Selecting a drill from another source still only copies it into the practice, never into the coach's own library (unchanged, pre-existing `addAct` behavior).
- **Combined Builder Goal Guidance** (twenty-sixth session, new `src/components/BuilderGoalGuidance.jsx`, `<BuilderGoalGuidance>`): its own sibling section between Practice Details and The Run of Practice (never inside Practice Details' own body), starting collapsed on every entry with a real summary line (e.g. "2 priorities · plan projects closer to goal"). One `get_team_goal_report` fetch per team selection (cached, not refetched per keystroke); every recalculation as the draft changes — add/remove/resize a drill or station, change duration, switch teams — runs locally via `useMemo` over the same pure helpers Next Practice Guidance uses, with **zero additional network calls per edit** (confirmed live: added a drill to an empty draft and watched the projected-impact numbers update instantly, including correctly flagging an *overshoot* — adding too much of one category's time pushed its projected percentage past the target and read as "Farther from goal" even though it moved in the right direction, since the math measures distance-to-target, not direction alone). Three parts when expanded: current priorities (same gap logic as Next Practice Guidance, using this practice's own duration), the current draft mix (minutes per category, untagged minutes surfaced prominently, which target categories have zero planned minutes), and projected rolling impact (current % → projected % per category with a `Closer to goal`/`Farther from goal`/`At goal`/`No change` label). Builder has no Coach/Org-mode awareness anywhere today (a pre-existing gap noted below) — the section doesn't need it to be correct, since `get_team_goal_report`'s own `can_access_team` check already covers a director on an org team with no personal `team_staff` row; the "manage goals" link in the empty-goals state falls back to a personal head-coach check only.
- **Practice Execution Scorecard** (twenty-sixth session, `SessionHistoryDetail`, `PracticeExecutionScorecard`): a new `get_session_execution_scorecard(p_session_id)` RPC, rendered near the top of the session detail, before Skill Minutes/Planned vs. Actual. Deliberately no single overall score — structured measures only (planned/actual duration, planned/logged activity minutes, plan completion count, attendance, other/transition time) plus an activity-execution breakdown classified Extended/Shortened/On Plan/Skipped/Logged-but-not-originally-captured. Execution-unit grain was confirmed against the real logging model before writing this, not assumed: every non-station-block `practice_activity` is one unit keyed by `practice_activity_id`; every individual *station* inside a station block is its own unit keyed by `station_id` — the block container itself is never a loggable unit, so nothing gets marked "skipped" merely because logs live at the station grain. Classification uses a shared 60-second on-plan tolerance (`ON_PLAN_TOLERANCE_SECONDS`/`classifyDurationVariance` in `constants.js`, reused by Drill Insights too, so the two features never disagree about what "on plan" means). "Logged but not originally captured" needed a real signal, not a guess: `adjusted_by`/`adjusted_at` alone can't distinguish a live-captured row that was later corrected from one that never existed during the run at all, since both `adjust_session_activity` and `add_session_activity_row` stamp them the same way — a new `session_activity_log.manually_added` boolean (`20260802020000_session_activity_log_manually_added.sql`), set only by `add_session_activity_row`'s insert, makes this reliably derivable going forward (pre-migration manually-added rows just won't get the special badge, an honest simplification rather than a guessed classification). Refreshes automatically after any manager edit (exclude/restore, adjust a logged row, add a missing one) via the same `refresh()` call site the raw log already used.
- **Drill Performance Insights** (twenty-sixth session, owned-drill shelf only — My Drills in Coach mode, Org Drills in Organization mode; never Team Libraries/peer-shared or Public Library drills): reachable via a "View Drill Insights" ellipsis item, always available on an owned drill regardless of usage, opening an empty state when there's none yet. (The card itself no longer shows a colored heat icon — see the Library section above; direct feedback called it too distracting. `DrillInsightHeatIcon`/the heat-tier math still exist in case a less-prominent surface wants them later.) Two-tier query architecture: `get_drill_insight_summaries(p_library_activity_ids[])` is one batch call per shelf (capped at 300 ids, silently drops any id the caller doesn't own/manage rather than trusting the client, still called even though nothing currently renders its color — the count still drives the Most Frequently Used sort), `get_drill_insights(p_library_activity_id)` fetches full detail only when a coach actually opens one drill — usage counts (4wk/12wk/12mo/all-time), average planned vs. actual duration with a deterministic natural-language read-out, on-plan/extended/shortened percentages, reverse-chron usage history, teams, and recent notes (capped 5, each with a per-note "hide" button added twenty-seventh session — direct feedback wanted to hide one irrelevant note without touching the others, reuses the same `archiveNote` RPC `SessionHistoryDetail` already relies on). Lineage is strictly `library_activity_id`, confirmed through normal activities, stations, templates (a template's own `library_activity_id` copies through into the practice the same way a direct drill add does), and manually-added history rows (which can only target an execution unit already in the plan, so they inherit its lineage automatically) — no fuzzy name matching anywhere, so renaming a drill never breaks its history. A copied drill gets its own new `activity_library.id` via `copyDrillToMyLibrary`, so it starts its own insight history with zero special-casing needed. Every usage/note row is additionally filtered to a team the caller can *currently* access, since a personal drill's history can span teams the coach has since left.
- Coach sets per-skill-tag target percentages (category-grouped picker filtered to the team's sport, sum-over-100 validated). Each category also has a typeable number input alongside its slider now (seventeenth session) — landing on an exact value across several sliders that need to add to 100 was fiddly by drag alone.
- **"Measure over the last N weeks" moved into the glance view's own header** (thirty-first session, direct feedback: it belonged with the report it controls, not the Goals card above it that sets targets): now lives in `GlanceView`'s "Target vs. Planned vs. Actual" title, editable inline for `canManage`, plain text for a read-only viewer. **A real, pre-existing staleness bug found and fixed while making this move**: `updateGoalsWindowWeeks` writes straight to `teams.goals_window_weeks` with no client-side refresh call at all -- the control's own `refreshReport()` call (added this session) fixed the report's percentages, but the top-level `data.teams` cache never got invalidated, so the control's own displayed value (re-derived from `team.goalsWindowWeeks` on every fresh mount) silently reverted to the old number the moment a coach navigated away and back, even though the new value was already saved correctly server-side -- confirmed live by querying the database directly while the on-screen control showed the stale value. `TeamGoalsRoute` → `GoalsScreen` → `GlanceView` now also thread `refreshTeams` down, called alongside `refreshReport` after every save.
- **Save-when-not-100% confusion, root-caused and fixed** (seventeenth session): Save was already disabled unless totals hit exactly 100% (`canSave`), but no `.btn` in the whole app had any `:disabled` CSS at all — a disabled primary button looked pixel-identical to an enabled one, so a coach could tap it, watch nothing happen, and have no idea why. Added a global `.btn:disabled{opacity:.4;cursor:not-allowed;}`. Also added real dirty-tracking (`useBlocker` + `beforeunload`, same pattern Builder already uses) so navigating away with unsaved slider edits now prompts a confirm instead of silently discarding them. Both confirmed live: the confirm dialog fired with the exact right message and blocked navigation; the grayed-out Save button was visibly different at a glance. **Save also grays out again immediately after a successful save** (twenty-seventh session, direct feedback): `canSave` alone (totals=100) stayed true after saving, so the button looked exactly as clickable as before even with nothing left to save — `disabled` now also checks the existing `dirty` flag.
- **`get_team_goal_report`/`set_team_goals` stale-column bug, found and fixed** (seventeenth session, `20260730040000_fix_get_team_goal_report_stale_column.sql`): the live function still referenced `team_goals.skill_tag_id`, a column replaced by `skill_category_id` back on 2026-07-19 (`20260719030000`). A same-day migration (`20260719040000_goals_rpcs_category_level.sql`) already contained the correct `create or replace` definition, but it evidently never took effect live — the exact class of migration-history-vs-live-schema drift this file's Session Log has flagged before (thirteenth session). This broke the "Target vs. Planned vs. Actual" glance view for *every* team, silently, until caught by an actual browser test against production. Fixed by re-applying both functions' correct bodies verbatim; verified via a real (and cleaned-up) throwaway account before calling it done, not just a clean migration push.
- A bullet-chart-style glance view: target tick, planned/actual bars, delta chips (only shown at ≥3 points off), an "untagged" row, an "other/transitions" per-practice average. **"Tag Untagged Drills" button** (twenty-ninth session, `canManage` only, not gated on the untagged-time being "high" — a coach cleaning up a small amount shouldn't have to wait for it to become a problem first): deep-links into the Library's new untagged-only filtered view for the team's own sport, described in the Library section above. **Messaging adapts once every library drill is already tagged** (thirty-first session, direct feedback): the "link drills to the library" nudge stops being actionable advice the moment there's nothing left in the library to tag -- whatever untagged time remains at that point came from activities that were never added from the library at all (typed straight into a practice), which this screen has no way to retroactively fix. `GoalsScreen.jsx` now computes `allDrillsTagged` (same sport/owner-scoped untagged-drill count the Library deep link itself uses) and swaps in a different, accurate message ("Every drill in your library is already tagged...") with the CTA hidden, instead of repeating advice that's already been followed.
- A History section, backed by `get_team_session_history(team_id)`, is the first real frontend read path for `session_activity_log`'s actual (not just planned) timing: reverse-chronological sessions with wall-clock duration, attendance, top-3 skills, and manager-only editing (exclude a session from goals, adjust a logged row's start/end, or log a row that was never captured live).
