- **History consolidated: the Schedule Agenda tab's own "Show/Hide Completed / History" section is gone, and Goals & Insights' History detail was rebuilt to look and act like Schedule's history did** (thirty-second session continued, direct feedback — scoped literally to "remove history from the schedule agenda view," so Month view's own past-practice access via `DaySheet`/`HistoryViewer` was deliberately left alone, not folded into this rework). `SessionHistoryDetail`'s layout now leads with the practice date + `[Edit/Done Editing][Delete]` header, then Run Now (`runNowFrom` — copies the practice's own activities into a brand-new unscheduled practice via `savePracticeTree(null,{...stripIdsForCopy(activities)})`, then jumps straight into Builder for it) and Save as Template (mirrors `HistoryViewer`'s own inline-name-input flow) as full-bleed primary actions, then Print/Export PDF, then a **Planned vs. Actual** section moved to lead the content (visually resembling Schedule's own history view, per direct feedback), then the Practice Execution Scorecard, Skill Minutes, and Notes. **One edit toggle now gates everything editable**, replacing what used to be a separate Edit affordance on every single Planned-vs-Actual line — the per-log "adjust"/"log actual time" buttons and the notes' delete `×` are all now conditioned on the screen's own `editMode` flag, off by default.
- **Unreviewed-notes now surface actively on Home too, and the History tab itself gets a visible indicator** (thirty-second session continued, direct feedback): a new `get_teams_with_unviewed_notes(p_team_ids[])` RPC (plain SQL, `security definer`, joins `practices`→completed `practice_live_sessions`→unarchived/unviewed `notes`, filtered per-row through the existing `can_access_team`) drives a new Home nudge banner — any home team with unviewed notes gets a "review the practice" call to attention. `GoalsSubnav`'s History pill also gained a small red-dot indicator (new this session) reflecting the same `anyUnviewed` signal. The underlying pieces this builds on — per-session mark-viewed-on-open (gated `canManage`, so a read-only viewer glancing at history doesn't clear the head coach's unread state), and the "Mark all as viewed" bulk action inside the `view==="history"` list — already existed correctly from an earlier session and needed no changes, confirmed by reading the code before assuming otherwise.
- **"Delete Practice"** (thirty-first session, direct feedback, top of `SessionHistoryDetail`, `canManage` only, real confirm first): the same soft-delete `archivePractice()` every other "Delete" in the app already uses. `get_team_session_history` needed a matching change to actually make it stick, not just cosmetically -- its `sessions` CTE joined `practice_live_sessions` to `practices` on `team_id`/`status` only, with no `archived_at` check at all, unlike every other practice-listing query in the app, so an archived practice's session would otherwise keep showing up in History forever. Fixed in `20260805010000_session_history_excludes_deleted_practice.sql` (dry-run verified, then a byte-identical-before-edit check via `pg_get_functiondef` per this file's own documented drift-checking convention). Verified live: deleting a seeded completed session immediately dropped it from the list.
- **Logged-row time editing no longer shows a date picker** (thirty-first session, direct feedback -- "I don't think any user will ever need to change the date"): `TimeRangeForm` used two `type="datetime-local"` inputs; now shows time-only Start/End (`type="time"`) plus a Duration (min) field, all three kept mutually consistent (editing Start shifts End by the same amount, holding duration fixed -- a move, not a resize; editing End or Duration recomputes the other, holding Start fixed). The date itself is derived once, invisibly, from the log row's own existing date or (for a brand-new "Log actual time" row that has neither yet) the session's own end date -- never something a coach picks. Same component serves both "adjust an existing row" and "log a row that was never captured live."
- The older, separately-used `HistoryViewer` component (`CommandScreen.jsx`, reused by `ScheduleScreen`/`ManageScreen`) was deliberately left untouched rather than retrofitted with this — it only ever receives a bare `practice` object, no `session_id`, so a new `SessionHistoryDetail` component was built instead to avoid destabilizing three already-working call sites. **It does now show the practice's actual run time, not its scheduled time** (thirty-second session continued, direct feedback: running a practice hours or days before its scheduled slot showed the *scheduled* time in history, which read as simply wrong): a new, narrowly-scoped `fetchPracticeActualStart(practiceId)` queries `practice_live_sessions` directly for the most recent `status='completed'` row's `created_at`, deliberately not touching the shared `fetchPracticeRunStatus` helper (whose `{practice_id:"completed"|"started"}` string-map return contract other call sites depend on) — `HistoryViewer`'s header date/time line prefers this actual timestamp over `fmtDate(practice.date)+startTime` whenever a real session exists.
- **`SessionHistoryDetail`'s Notes card** (added on top of the existing `notes` display, which used to just be plain text) now groups notes by drill vs. end-of-practice, shows each note's author + role (or "Helper" + whatever freeform label an anonymous submitter typed) and any @mentioned players as chips, and lets `canManage` archive a note (`archiveNote` — new; the underlying `notes` table had no update policy at all before this session). **Notes now display under the drill they were captured in specifically (not just grouped alongside it), general/end-of-practice notes stay at the end, and per-note delete is gated behind the same `editMode` toggle the rest of the rebuilt History screen uses** (thirty-second session continued, direct feedback) — the delete `×` only renders while editing, so a coach browsing history read-only can't accidentally remove a note.
- **Development Pulse** (twenty-eighth session, `src/components/DevelopmentPulseCard.jsx`): a single dynamic CTA card on Home, directly beneath the next-practice hero, Coach mode only (never Org mode — a cross-team director-facing version is explicitly a separate, future widget, not this card reused with a random team). Resolves to exactly one of 7 states via a pure, presentation-neutral `resolveDevelopmentPulseState()` (`constants.js`) — no categorical fifth variation of goal math: it's built entirely from `get_team_goal_report` (extended this session with one new field, see below) plus the same `categoryMinutesForPracticeActivities`/`calculateGoalGapGuidance`/`calculateProjectedGoalImpact` helpers Builder Goal Guidance and Next Practice Guidance already use. States in priority order: no categories for the team's sport → goals not configured → insufficient usable history (`DEVELOPMENT_PULSE_MIN_COMPLETED_SESSIONS=2`) → data quality too weak (`DEVELOPMENT_PULSE_MAX_UNTAGGED_PCT=25`, outranks a category recommendation on purpose) → largest gap missing from the next plan → next plan materially improves the largest gap → a meaningful gap not covered by the previous two → everything aligned (`DEVELOPMENT_PULSE_BALANCED_TOLERANCE_PTS=3`, with its own override: a currently-aligned team whose next plan would move a category materially *farther* from goal falls through to the gap state instead of a false all-clear). A bullet-bar SVG graphic (Actual/Projected/Goal on one 0–100 axis, solid fill + hatched-pattern projected extension + a goal tick, never color-only) — no Recharts, no new charting dependency, same inline-SVG convention as the Trends chart. **Focus-team selection** (`resolveDevelopmentPulseFocusTeamId`) is next-practice's team first, else the home team with the most recently completed session (one new batch RPC, `get_teams_recent_completed_session(p_team_ids[])`, so this never becomes an N+1 per-team query), else the first team. **Spec compliance bug found and fixed during live verification**: the "missing from next plan" state's `Plan {N} minutes` CTA didn't check `closable` the way the sibling "meaningful gap" state already did, so it could recommend more minutes than the practice's own scheduled duration (observed live: "Plan 63 minutes" inside a 45-minute practice) — the spec explicitly prohibits this ("Do not use `Plan N minutes` with a number larger than the practice duration"); fixed to fall back to the same "This gap will take more than one practice to close" / "Review Goal Guidance" copy the closable-gap state already used. **Navigation wiring bug found and fixed**: the CTA into Builder (`kind:"builder_goal_guidance"`) initially called `navigate()` directly, which left `BuilderScreen`'s `useState` initializers (which read `editPracticeId` from context) seeing a stale `null` on their first render — `editPracticeId` is only set a tick later via `BuilderRoute`'s own `useEffect`, by which point those `useState` calls had already locked in "new blank practice" defaults and never re-derive. Every pre-existing entry point into Builder avoids this by calling the existing `goToBuilder()` helper (which sets `editPracticeId` synchronously *before* navigating) instead of a bare `navigate()` — `goToBuilder` was extended with an optional 4th `navState` param (forwarded to `navigate()`'s own `location.state`) so Development Pulse's deep link (`openGoalGuidance`, `highlightedSkillCategoryId`) could go through the same safe path instead of bypassing it. `BuilderGoalGuidance` force-opens on arrival (`startOpen`) and gives the highlighted category a one-time CSS pulse (fades on its own via `animation`, plus a 2.6s timer clears the prop so it doesn't replay on an unrelated re-render) — never permanently changes Builder's own open/collapsed state or dirty-tracking. The "Review Untagged Time" CTA has no precise in-page anchor per spec, so it lands on Goals Overview and gets the same one-time pulse treatment on the Untagged row instead (`emphasizeUntagged` via `location.state`, consumed once by `GoalsScreen`). "View Trends" lands directly on the Trends subtab (`location.state.openGoalsView`). All of this verified live end-to-end with a disposable coach account walked through data-quality → missing-from-next-plan → aligned-with-a-moving-away-override → aligned states via direct data mutation (not 7 separate synthetic teams), plus a 19-case pure-function unit pass (`resolveDevelopmentPulseState`/`resolveDevelopmentPulseFocusTeamId`) covering every state, the tie-breaker (stable sort, first-configured-category wins), and focus-team priority order. Also verified: card absent in Org mode (code-inspection only — `!isOrgMode &&` gate, same pattern as every other mode-gated surface in this file, not re-tested live against a real org account), phone-width layout (375px, no clipping/overflow). **A second, unrelated FK gap found and fixed while cleaning up test data**: `session_activity_log.practice_activity_id`/`.station_id` had no `ON DELETE` action at all (unlike every other link in that same cascade chain, which already cascade), blocking deletion of a team/practice's history the moment any activity had been logged against it — fixed to `ON DELETE CASCADE` (`20260805000000_session_activity_log_activity_cascade.sql`, dry-run verified, applied to production).

**Twenty-ninth session addendum — collapsible, persisted, with a new-insight indicator:** the header now has a caret (`Ic_Caret`) that collapses the card down to just its header row; collapsed/expanded state is persisted per-coach (`localStorage`, keyed by `coachId` — this is one physical widget on Home, not a per-team preference, so it stays collapsed across sessions and across whichever team ends up as the focus team on a later visit) rather than per-team or in component state. A small red dot appears next to the header label whenever the card is collapsed *and* the resolved state has changed since the coach last had it open — tracked via a cheap fingerprint (`teamId|state|categoryId`, deliberately excluding percentages/minute counts so ordinary week-to-week wobble in the same underlying situation doesn't re-trigger it) also persisted in `localStorage`; expanding the card (or it simply being expanded when a new state resolves) marks the current fingerprint seen and clears the dot. **A real, pre-existing bug found during this session's live verification, not part of the requested work**: the `insufficient_history` state's goal-mix bar (`GoalMixStrip`) was fed raw `report.skills` rows (`target_pct`, snake_case) while reading `c.targetPct` (camelCase) — every segment computed a `NaN` width, so the bar rendered as nothing at all (invisible, not just wrong), and its `aria-label` read "Passing undefined%, Shooting undefined%". Fixed by mapping to camelCase at the call site, matching every other graphic component's prop convention in this file. Verified live: collapse/expand, persistence across a hard reload, the red dot appearing after a background data change (team goals set via direct SQL while collapsed) and clearing on expand, and the goal-mix bar rendering correctly (both the visual bar and the corrected aria-label) after the fix.

### Live sessions
- **Practice Setup is now a real, shared pre-live stage, not a separate screen (thirty-second session continued, direct feedback from an assistant coach's actual broken experience)**: `practice_live_sessions.setup_confirmed_at` (nullable timestamptz, `20260805040000_start_or_join_live_session.sql`) is null while the session is collaboratively staging — attendance, coach/player station assignment — and gets set once "Start Practice" is tapped, at which point the run actually begins at activity 0. Both the head coach and any assistant now land on the exact same `stage==="attend"` screen (relabeled "Practice Setup," not "Attendance") when either one reaches `/run/:practiceId`, instead of the old flow where a second coach's "Run Practice" tap silently force-started the drill timer out from under the head coach mid-setup, blanked the second coach's own screen, and demoted the head coach to read-only with no visible content. A new atomic RPC, `start_or_join_live_session(p_practice_id)`, replaces the old client-side "check for a session, then insert one" race entirely: it selects for an existing active session first and only inserts inside a nested `begin...exception when unique_violation` block if none exists, backed by a new partial unique index (`practice_live_sessions_one_active_per_practice on (practice_id) where status='active'`) — two coaches racing to open the same never-yet-live practice now deterministically get the same single session row instead of a duplicate, verified live (both via a rolled-back functional RPC test proving `created:true` then `created:false` on repeat calls, and later organically during real repeated "Join Practice" visits, confirmed by a direct DB query showing exactly one session row). Practice Setup itself now handles player *and* coach attendance plus station assignment for both coaches and players in one screen (`StationSetupSection`, new) — tap a station's "Assign Leader," tap a player chip to open a move-picker and reassign them to a different station, all live and shared across every coach viewing setup (backed by the existing `session_groups`/`session_group_members` append-only tables, keyed by session+activity). The assistant's entry-point button is now always labeled "Join Practice" (see the smart-button note below); "Start Practice" only appears once setup is confirmed, and only advances the whole session to the first drill for everyone watching. **Explicitly decided not to add a confirmation prompt before "Take Control"** (direct feedback: "anyone can take control and we now see who has control so I don't think they will be taken off guard... let's leave it for now") — the existing unilateral, no-confirmation Take Control behavior (see below) is unchanged.
- **`PreviewView` (the `/preview/:token` screen) no longer demotes a coach to a read-only anonymous `HelperView`** (thirty-second session continued): its polling/refetch logic now checks `can_manage && has_live_session` first and redirects straight to `/run/:practiceId` (the real coach Setup/live screen) before ever falling through to the old `is_live` → `/live/:token` anonymous-viewer redirect, which is exactly the bug the assistant coach hit (their own screen going blank because it redirected into the helper view instead of the real command screen the moment the head coach's tap made the session "live"). `get_preview_view` (`20260805050000_preview_view_setup_stage.sql`) now also returns `has_live_session` alongside a `is_live` that's true only once `setup_confirmed_at` is actually set, so a coach who reaches Preview while another coach is still in Setup gets routed into Setup too, not treated as "not live yet."
- Real-time state machine (`practice_live_sessions`) with take-control/hand-off between coaches, pause/resume, ±1 min nudge, station rotation, append-only attendance (`session_attendance`), and per-activity dwell-time logging (`session_activity_log`).
- **Strict single controller, no exceptions** (confirmed decision, Assistant Coach handoff §1.3): whoever holds control is the only one who can advance, adjust the timer, mark attendance, or end the session — including the head coach; there is no "head coach always can regardless of controller" carve-out. This was already the shipped model (`isController` already gated every action) except for one place: **mid-run plan editing (`LiveEditBuilder`) is gated on role, not control** — an assistant who takes control still can't open it, unlike every other controller action. Fixed this session (`isController && amHeadCoach`, was `isController` alone).
- **Non-controller's action-button area** (where advance/±1min/timer normally render) now shows who's driving ("Coach X is running this") plus a deliberately low-emphasis (outline, not primary) "Take Control" button, in that exact spot rather than a blank area or a bold button — a bold button there risked an accidental tap seizing control from whoever's mid-drill. A brief top banner still shows a passive "Read-only" status; the actual tap target lives only in the controls area now.
- **Toast on control change**: the coach who just lost control gets a brief, non-blocking "Coach X took control" toast, so a habitual tap on Next/±1min a beat later is explained rather than silently doing nothing.
- Offline resilience: single-pending-write retry with backoff, distinguishes "network down" from "someone else moved the session" so a signal drop doesn't blank the screen.
- A coach can leave a live session without ending it — safe because the whole run state (timer, stage) re-derives from `current_phase_started_at` on mount, not from anything held only in memory.
- **"Turn On Audio?" prompt** (seventeenth session): audio defaults off and was easy to never discover via the small header speaker icon alone. A one-time popup now shows the moment any viewer — coach (`CommandScreen`) or anonymous helper (`HelperView`) — lands in a live session (`stage==="live"` for the coach, session-active for the helper), reminding them to turn their device volume up. Fires once per session id per mount (same guard pattern the station-leader warning modal already used); the two modals don't overlap (station warning waits for the audio prompt to be dismissed first).
- **Audio cues** (coach view only): a silent background `<audio>` loop + Screen Wake Lock keep the tab alive when the phone locks; real audio files play alongside or instead of `speechSynthesis` (`tennis-beep.wav` for the fixed 2-minute warning; time's-up is coach-selectable, see below). Time's-up plays the chosen sound, then a spoken **"Time"** once it ends (`audio.onended` triggers `speak("Time")`, reusing the same `speechSynthesis` call already used for "Two minutes remaining" — not baked into the audio file, since that's real browser TTS, not a recording). `HelperView` (parent-facing) is still speech-only — not yet extended with the sound+"Time" treatment.
- **Live Practice Audio settings** (2026-07-30, Settings screen): the coach picks a time's-up sound (Whistle/Buzzer/Ding/Beep — `public/audio/{whistle,gym-buzzer,ding,beep}.wav`, the latter two newly synthesized) and an announcer voice, each with a tap-to-preview. Stored in `localStorage` (`AUDIO_CUES`/`getAudioCuePref`/`getVoiceURIPref`/`resolveVoiceByURI`/`loadVoices`, `constants.js`), not the database — deliberately per-device, since available `speechSynthesis` voices differ entirely by browser/OS and a voice chosen on one device may not exist on another. Voice selection is a **full picker**, not a Male/Female toggle: an initial version guessed gender via a name-based heuristic (the Web Speech API has no real gender metadata) and just took the first match, which surfaced a dated, robotic-sounding voice ("Daniel") ahead of much better ones the heuristic's hint list didn't know about — there's no way to infer voice *quality* from a name. Replaced with a real list of every voice installed on the device (`loadVoices()` handles `getVoices()`'s async population via `voiceschanged`), each with a preview button, remembered by the specific `voiceURI` chosen. **Currently narrowed to just Samantha/Daniel** (`CURATED_VOICE_NAMES` in `SettingsScreen.jsx`) after Jax tried the full device list and found every option "simply awful" except those two — a stopgap until real recorded voice clips replace `speechSynthesis` entirely for the fixed phrases the app speaks (see Known Gaps).
- **Notes, captured live**: a "Quick note..." bar during the run and an "End of Practice Notes" textarea on the completion screen (both pre-existing, `createNote`/`notes` table) now support typing `@` to tag a player — a picker filtered to the roster inserts the name into the text and records the tag structurally (`note_player_tags`), surfaced later in Goals & Insights and on `PlayerProfile`. See the Notes section below for the full model.
- **Station leader assignment** (2026-07-29): `stations.team_staff_id`/`helper_name` already existed but had no visibility before a practice started and no safety net once it did. `get_preview_view` now also returns `can_manage` (server-computed off `auth.uid()` via `can_coach_team`, since `/preview/:token` is the same route/component for both a signed-in coach and an anonymous shared link) and, only when true, the team's `team_staff` roster, each station's real `id`, `practice_id`, and the caller's own `my_coach_name` — `PreviewView` uses this to show "No leader assigned -- tap to assign" per station plus a "Share Setup Link" button, reusing `updateStationLead`. Separately, in the live `CommandScreen`, the first time a session reaches `stage==="live"` after attendance, a one-time warning modal lists any station_block stations still lacking a leader, letting the coach assign one, share the live link, or explicitly leave it unmanned. Home's hero presents "Practice Setup" and "Review Plan"/"Start Practice" as co-equal side-by-side buttons for a planned practice (sized `blg` with `minWidth:0` — an earlier version used two `bxl` buttons, which overflowed the viewport on phone-width screens since flex items default to `min-width:auto` and can't shrink below nowrap text's own width). `PreviewView` also gained a "Back to Home" link and a persistent "Start Practice" bar (both `can_manage`-only) — previously a coach reaching Practice Setup from Home had no way back except the browser's own Back button, and no way to actually start the run from that screen.
- **"Who has this open" presence** (2026-07-29): a small live badge on `PreviewView`, the staff `CommandScreen`, and `HelperView` showing which coaches currently have a practice's setup/live view open and how many anonymous viewers there are. Pure Supabase Realtime Presence (`subscribeToPracticePresence`, `src/supabase.js`) — no new table, no RLS, just ephemeral client-to-client state over the same websocket infra `subscribeToLiveSession` already uses (a *public* channel, not the newer "private channel" feature, so no extra Realtime authorization config was needed). Keyed by `practice_id` (not `live_session_id`) specifically so a coach still in Practice Setup and a helper who already opened the live link show up on the *same* channel — required adding `practice_id` to `get_live_session_view`'s output too (it never returned one before). Each client tracks `{kind:'coach',label}` or `{kind:'anon'}`; verified end-to-end with two real anonymous browser tabs on the same preview link — the badge correctly showed "2 people watching" and dropped back to "1" the instant the second tab closed.
- **Stations-intro and transition screens now visually distinct from actual coaching** (thirty-second session continued, direct feedback: "this will help a coach know they are transitioning rather than coaching"): the block-intro ("Get everyone to their station") and transition ("Rotate Now") sections both re-skin to a near-black background (`#0d1512`) with `PreviewView`'s existing dark-mode palette (green/gray/white/red text, translucent card borders) instead of the normal light coaching-screen chrome. The intro's redundant "Start Block ▶" button (bottom of the screen, alongside Reshuffle) is gone per direct feedback — the block starts on its own timer; only "Reshuffle" remains, full-width.
- **Live header layout reordered** (thirty-second session continued, direct feedback): the drill/activity title is now bigger (22px→30px) and the live-viewer-count `PresenceBadge` moved to sit directly under the "LIVE" row, above the title, with any subtitle below the (now larger) title — previously the subtitle rendered above the title and the presence badge came after it.
- **Player-attendance pill gets a hand icon** (thirty-second session continued, direct feedback): a small raised-hand SVG (`Ic.Hand`) now precedes the "x/y" count on both the coach (`CommandScreen`) and helper (`HelperView`) attendance pills, so it reads unambiguously as player attendance rather than some other counter.
- **Zero-total practice components (checklist/water break/etc.) show no "0/0 covered" during live view** (thirty-second session continued, direct feedback): the focus-label suffix (" - N/M covered") is simply omitted when a component has zero checklist items, rather than always rendering a count that's meaningless for a water break or a checklist-free intro.
- **"Two minutes remaining" audio skipped for short segments** (thirty-second session continued, direct feedback — it read as odd hearing a 2-minute warning at the very start of a 2-minute water break/transition): the cue's own timer effect now bails (`if(phaseSecs<=120)return;`) before its existing `[118,122]`-second trigger window, so any segment 2 minutes or under never fires it at all.
- **Overview toggle gets a caret** (thirty-second session continued, direct feedback): the Overview/Close button on the live screen now shows a `Ic.Chev` caret (pointing up while open, meaning "tap to collapse") after its label, on both the coach and helper views, so it visually reads as expandable/collapsible rather than a flat action button.
- **Ellipsis menu cleaned up** (thirty-second session continued, direct feedback — "needs a refresh"): Mute Audio/Enable Audio toggle, Share for Attendance, and Restart Practice are all removed from the live-session ellipsis menu (`restartPractice` and its only caller deleted outright); Leave/Edit Practice/Share Live View/End Practice/Abort Practice remain.
- **A rostered coach assigned to a station now defaults straight into that station's own zoomed-in view once stations-intro ends, instead of the shared overview** (thirty-second session continued, direct feedback): a new effect fires exactly once as `inBlockIntro` transitions false, looks up the signed-in coach's own `team_staff` id among `cur.stations`, and `setFocusSt`s to it when found — a signed-in coach with no station assignment, or any anonymous shared-link viewer, still lands on the all-stations overview and can tap into a station manually, unchanged.
- **The stations-overview screen supports live manual reassignment, and its redundant "Start Block" button is gone** (thirty-second session continued, direct feedback): the all-stations grid's player-chip row now renders the same interactive `PlayerChipLive` (tap to move/view profile) that Practice Setup's own station editor uses, for any `isController` viewer, instead of a read-only chip — writes go through the existing `session_groups`/`saveSessionGroups` pipeline, live for every viewer on the session. The bottom "Start Block ▶" button (redundant with the rotation timer already running) is removed.
- **Upcoming-drill "Up Next" items are clickable for every viewer, not just the controller** (thirty-second session continued, direct feedback): tapping a queued item opens a read-only, grayed-out `UpcomingPreview` modal ("Coming Up · Preview Only") showing that activity's equipment, location, and — for a station block — each station's own equipment/location/assigned players, without advancing the session. `get_live_session_view` (`20260805060000_live_session_upcoming_detail.sql`) now returns `description`/`coaching_points`/`sublocation_name`/`equipment`/a per-station `stations[]` array (name, sublocation, equipment, `player_ids` from `stations.assignments`) on every `upcoming_activities` row, not just name/duration/type. For a station block specifically, the preview prefers any live-reassigned groups already saved for that activity (`fetchLatestGroups`) over the block's own static `assignments`, so a coach previewing a not-yet-started rotation sees the *current* player groupings, not stale planned ones.

### Notes (Assistant Coach handoff §2)
- Two grains: per-drill/station (`practice_activity_id`/`station_id` set) and end-of-practice (both null). Backed by the `notes` table (existed since 2026-07-14 for the staff-facing capture UI above) plus a new `note_player_tags` join table for @mentions.
- **Anonymous helper composer reworded** (seventeenth session): the "End-of-practice note" checkbox (checked = general, unchecked = tagged to the current drill) is now a two-option toggle showing the actual choice — the current drill's name vs. "General" — same underlying `endOfPractice` boolean and data model, just a clearer control than a checkbox whose two states didn't read as a real choice.
- **Unreviewed-note red dot** (seventeenth session): `notes.viewed_at` (nullable, `20260730020000_note_review_tracking.sql`) plus a `has_unviewed_notes` flag added to `get_team_session_history`. Goals & Insights' History list shows a red dot per session with any non-archived, unviewed note; opening that session's detail as the head coach marks its notes viewed automatically (gated on `canManage` specifically, not any viewer, so an assistant/helper glancing at read-only history doesn't silently clear the head coach's unread indicator); a "Mark all as viewed" action clears every visible session at once. Marking is a plain client-side update against `viewed_at`, covered by the same `notes_update_archive` RLS policy `archiveNote` already uses — no new RPC needed for either the single-practice or whole-team case.
- **Anonymous helpers can now write notes too** — a first for this app; every other anonymous surface (`/preview/:token`, `/live/:token`) is read-only by design. Token-validated server-side (`submit_practice_note_by_token`, reuses the existing `validate_token()` — never a client-asserted author), with a flat 40-notes-per-token rate limit and a 500-character cap (both confirmed defaults, not assumed). `HelperView` got a new "Notes" panel (there was no note UI there at all before) with the same @mention picker, using the same minimized roster (first name + last initial + jersey) helpers already see everywhere else, plus an optional freeform name field (`author_label` — display only, never trusted as identity).
- Archive-don't-delete: `notes` had no UPDATE policy at all before this session (comment called it "quick-capture-only") — added one, gated the same as read/insert (`can_access_practice`).
- Surfaced in `SessionHistoryDetail` (Goals & Insights, grouped by drill/end-of-practice, author+role shown, tagged players as chips) and, new, on `PlayerProfile` ("Practice Notes" section, reverse-chronological across every practice this player was ever @mentioned in, via `fetchNotesForPlayer`).

### Helper/parent-facing (anonymous, token-based, no login)
- `/preview/:token` — pre-practice setup view (equipment, roster, countdown to start).
- `/live/:token` — live view during a run (current drill/station, roster, optional attendance-marking scope), now also a note-writing surface (see Notes above) — the first anonymous write path in this app. For a group/station drill, each player is shown as a tappable chip; tapping reveals that player's per-category focus note (if the coach has set one) alongside the drill's own skill tags — resolved server-side inside `get_live_session_view` (tag names via `library_activity_id → drill_tags → skill_tags`, notes via that tag's `category_id → player_focus_areas`, team-scoped), since the anonymous helper has no client-side access to `activityLibrary`/`skillTags` the way the staff view does.
- One-tap **Practice Setup View** on Home's next-practice hero card, for any signed-in coach (head coach or assistant): finds/creates a preview token for that practice and opens the same `/preview/:token` page a shared link would, reusing the identical component/query rather than a second one. Always visible once a plan exists, deliberately not time-gated the way the anonymous link is — that gating exists only because the anon link has no auth, which doesn't apply to a signed-in staff member.
- Player data is minimized (first name + last-initial + jersey), everything else (equipment, coaching points, location) is shown in full — a deliberate privacy choice, not an oversight.

### Public landing page, feedback, and legal
- `/` is adaptive on session state: signed-out renders `LandingPage`, signed-in renders the app — deliberately not a hard route split, so an installed PWA icon (`start_url: '/'`) keeps launching straight into the app.
- Landing page is a full 14-section product-walkthrough marketing site with real app CSS-class-built visuals (not screenshots, not stylized mockups) using realistic sample data — pixel-consistent with the actual UI by construction.
- `/terms`, `/privacy` render final, attorney-cleared copy via a single catch-all `vercel.json` rewrite.
- In-app feedback is authenticated only (Home "?" → "Send Feedback"). The landing page's own anonymous feedback form was removed; `submitPublicFeedback`/`feedback` table were kept in place, unused, in case a future non-authenticated contact path wanted them -- **no longer dead code as of the twentieth session**: the Settings/FAQ "Request a Consultation" form (`ConsultationRequestForm`, `LegalPages.jsx`) reuses both `submitFeedback` (authenticated) and `submitPublicFeedback` (anonymous), exactly the future contact path this was speculatively kept around for.

## Database & permissions (architecture, not a live snapshot)

Point-in-time row counts and table counts age out fast on a system under active development — the last full live-DB introspection was 2026-07-21, and several sessions have touched the schema since (the org-model rework and Coach/Org mode work alone add and drop tables/columns). What's stable and worth keeping here is the **architecture**, not a specific count. Run a fresh introspection against the live project before trusting any specific number.

**Ownership patterns**
- **Team-scoped**: practices, stations, session data — access via `can_access_team`/`can_manage_team`, which check `teams.owner_user_id`, org membership (`teams.organization_id` + `is_org_member`/`is_org_admin`), or a non-archived `team_staff` row.
- **Coach-or-org ("owned")**: drills, assets, locations, templates, skill tags — `owner_user_id` XOR `organization_id`, checked via `can_access_owned`/`can_manage_owned` (the latter requires org-admin, not just org-member, for org-owned rows — keeps shared libraries curated).
- **Anonymous/token tier**: `session_access_tokens` (scope, expiry, revocation) gates `/preview/:token` and `/live/:token` entirely through `SECURITY DEFINER` RPCs — `anon` has no direct table grants beyond `REFERENCES/TRIGGER/TRUNCATE` on real tables. As of this session this tier can **write**, not just read, for the first time (`submit_practice_note_by_token`) — same pattern (token validated inside the function, `anon` still has zero direct grants on `notes`/`note_player_tags`).
- **Platform admin**: `admin_users`/`is_admin()` is a flat, separate superadmin gate (founder metrics, public-catalog curation rights) — unrelated to org roles.

**RLS conventions used everywhere**
- Almost no DELETE policy anywhere — archive via `archived_at`, never hard-delete through the app. Exceptions: join tables where removing an association isn't destroying history, and one explicit audit-adjustment delete policy on `session_activity_log`.
- Actor-identity columns (`created_by`, `marked_by`, `noted_by`, etc.) are enforced via `WITH CHECK (col = auth.uid())`, never trusted from the client.
- `service_role` bypasses RLS entirely but **not** base-table GRANTs — every new Edge Function or import script that queries app tables directly (rather than through an RPC, which runs as the function owner) needs its own explicit grant. This has bitten more than one session (first `profiles`, later `org_invites`/`organizations`) — check for it early if a new Edge Function mysteriously returns empty/errors.
- `practice_activities`/`template_activities` are full copies of the source drill's fields at add-time, never live references — editing a library drill or template later never retroactively changes anything already built from it. Same lineage-not-binding pattern applies to `templates.default_team_id` and `practices.template_id`.

**Legacy POC tables — dropped 2026-07-27.** `app_data`, `coaches`, and `live_sessions` (the old table) predated the current migration history entirely (created for the pre-rewrite POC, before Postgres RLS/auth existed in this app) and had carried their original wide-open `anon`-era RLS the whole time. Once `app_data`'s last client-side reference was traced and removed (see Session Log), all three were confirmed zero-reference in `src/`, and dropped via `20260727000000_drop_legacy_poc_tables.sql`. If you're looking for them, they're gone on purpose — see Session Log for the verification trail before dropping.

---


## Known gaps / deliberately deferred (do not build without checking first)

- Hard/GDPR-style account deletion — still not started as an actual feature (the FAQ tells coaches to email support), but several real pieces of the "FK-cascade ordering issue" this entry used to gesture at vaguely are now confirmed and fixed: `practice_series.created_by`, `organizations.created_by`, `feedback.user_id`, `practice_live_sessions`' actor columns (all earlier sessions, all the nullable + `ON DELETE SET NULL` actor-identity pattern), and — twenty-sixth session, found live while tearing down that session's own disposable test account — `activity_library`'s four lineage-pointer FKs (`practice_activities.library_activity_id`, `stations.library_activity_id`, `template_activities.library_activity_id`, `template_stations.library_activity_id`, all previously `no action`; fixed to `on delete set null` in `20260802050000_library_lineage_fk_actor_pattern.sql`, since all four are copy-at-add-time lineage pointers, not live content). **A new instance found the same session, not yet fixed, and deliberately left for a real product decision rather than a unilateral guess**: `session_attendance.player_id references players(id)` also has no `ON DELETE` action, so deleting a player (which cascades from deleting their team, which cascades from deleting their account) blocks on any session they were ever marked present/absent/left-early in. Unlike the lineage-pointer case, `SET NULL` isn't obviously correct here — `session_attendance` is itself the historical record (append-only, "current status = the most recent row"), and `get_team_session_history`'s own attendance count uses `distinct on (sa.player_id)`, which would silently collapse multiple different deleted players' rows into a single NULL group and undercount a session's attendance. Needs a real decision (cascade-delete the attendance rows entirely vs. some anonymized-but-still-counted representation) before fixing, not just an FK migration. Other tables may still have the same gap unaudited; every instance found so far was found by hitting it, not by a systematic sweep — worth an actual audit before this becomes a real self-service feature. **Hit again, thirty-first session, tearing down that session's own test account**: neither `authenticated` nor `service_role` has a direct `DELETE` grant on `session_attendance` at all (`permission denied for table session_attendance`, even via the service-role key) — this table isn't just missing the FK action, it has no delete path through PostgREST for any role. The only way through was `supabase db query --linked --file <path>` (the Management-API SQL path already used for dry-running migrations), which runs with enough privilege to bypass table grants entirely; a plain service-role JS client cannot clean this up. **A third, distinct instance found forty-first session, tearing down that session's own disposable account**: `planned_absences.noted_by references profiles(id)` also has no `ON DELETE` action — marking any player absent (e.g. via `AbsencePicker`) and then trying to delete that coach's account blocks with `update or delete on table "profiles" violates foreign key constraint "planned_absences_noted_by_fkey"`. Unlike `session_attendance`, this one probably *is* just the ordinary actor-identity gap (`noted_by` records who flagged the absence, not the historical fact itself, which is the practice/player pairing) — likely a straightforward `on delete set null` fix, same pattern as `session_attendance.marked_by`/`session_activity_log.logged_by`/`practice_live_sessions.controller_user_id` already got in `20260707190000_live_session_actor_set_null.sql` — but not fixed yet this session, just found and worked around via the same `db query --linked` manual-delete path. Confirms the "other tables may still have the same gap unaudited" line above was right; still worth a real sweep before self-service account deletion ships.
- Week-view / drag-to-reschedule, games/events, series-level default templates, moving a plan between practices, availability polling — explicitly out of scope for now.
- Any other parent/player-facing surface beyond the existing anonymous helper links — explicit product stance: "this is a coach tool, not TeamSnap."
- Push notifications/reminders.
- A second org role beyond `director` — proposed as `admin` in the original design, but `org_staff.role`'s CHECK constraint only allows `'director'` today, so it doesn't exist as a usable value yet. No billing model exists to define what a second tier would even gate. (An earlier, since-replaced org model had `coach`/`viewer` roles too — see Session Log's "Sharing & org model" — but those aren't part of today's `org_staff` table at all, not just unenforced.)
- Automated test suite — every feature is verified empirically in a live browser against production with disposable Supabase test accounts, by design (no local Supabase environment in this setup).
- ~~Dropping the legacy `app_data`/`coaches`/old `live_sessions` POC tables~~ **Done 2026-07-27** — see Session Log.
- `copyDrillToMyLibrary` copying skill tags onto the copy — currently skips tags entirely regardless of scope.
- ~~Equipment visibility for a peer-shared drill~~ **Fixed, same day** — `assets_select_access` extended with the same `is_library_peer`/`is_private` branch already used for drill visibility (`20260801090000_assets_peer_sharing_rls.sql`). `can_link_asset_to_practice_activity`'s own comment about this class of limitation for personal-team co-coaches is now stale for the specific peer-sharing case; it may still apply to a plain (non-Permissions-linked) co-coach scenario, not re-verified.
- ~~Two equipment-mismatch dialogs~~ **Done, twenty-fifth session continued** — see Session Log. Now fully closes the loop for every sharing source (org, Public Library, and peer), following the `assets_select_access` fix above.
- Pro+/plan-based gating on who can be granted "Share Practice Planning" — the delegation mechanism itself (twenty-fifth session) is real and RLS-enforced, but whether granting it requires the assistant to be Pro/org-affiliated (unless the head coach has Pro+) is not wired to anything, consistent with `src/entitlements.js` staying fully inert until real billing exists.
- A real, app-writable "add a skill category" flow — `skill_categories` is service-role/SQL-editor seeded only. 6 of the app's 12 sports have categories today (Baseball, Basketball, Football, Soccer, Lacrosse, Volleyball); Softball, Hockey, Tennis, and Swimming have none — no proposal or seed exists for them, this just hasn't been done yet.
- ~~Baseball/Basketball skill-tag duplication~~ **Fixed 2026-07-27** — see Session Log.
- Anonymous helpers can tag a note to a drill (`practice_activity_id`) but not a specific station within a station block (`station_id` is accepted by `submit_practice_note_by_token` but `HelperView`'s composer never passes one) — deliberately kept simple for v1, not a hard limitation of the schema.
- `PlayerProfile`'s new "Practice Notes" section shows the note text/author/date but not which practice it came from — fine for now with one team's worth of history, would want a practice reference once there's enough volume that "which practice was this" becomes a real question.
- `HelperView`'s notes panel is write-only by design (matches the confirmed scope: staff-only screens are where notes get read back) — a helper gets a "Note added" confirmation flash, never a list of what they've submitted.
- **Replace `speechSynthesis` with recorded human audio for the live-practice voice cues** — every synthetic voice tried (a name-based Male/Female heuristic, then the full device voice list) fell short; Jax is arranging real recordings of the four fixed phrases the app ever speaks (`"Two minutes remaining."`, `"Time"`, `"Next up!"`, `"Audio on"`) to send as `.wav`/`.mp3` files. Once received: add them as static files (same pattern as `whistle.wav` etc.), likely as one or more new selectable "voice" options alongside/replacing the current curated Samantha/Daniel `speechSynthesis` picker (`LivePracticeAudioSection`, `SettingsScreen.jsx`) — probably means `beep()`/`speak()` in `CommandScreen.jsx` branching on whether the chosen "voice" is a recorded-clip set or a `speechSynthesis` voice, since a recorded clip can't take arbitrary text the way TTS can (fine here, since only ever these 4 fixed phrases are spoken).
- **Migrate transactional email off Resend's free tier to Google Workspace's SMTP relay** — not urgent, but worth doing soon. Confirmed (research + a second check against Jax's actual account): included at no extra cost on the paid Workspace plan already in use, official per-user limit 10,000/day vs. Resend free tier's ~100/day. One real catch: **new Workspace tenants are capped below that full limit until $100 of billing spend and 60 days of account tenure have passed** (Google's standard anti-abuse ramp-up for new accounts, not anything specific to this setup) — but even under that ramp-up cap, it's still roughly 5x Resend's current ceiling, so it's a strict improvement even before the account "matures." Two separate integration points, not one: Supabase Auth's SMTP setting (dashboard-only, no CLI/API access from this environment) and the two Edge Functions that call Resend's API directly (`notify-team-staff-added`, `notify-org-invite` — code Claude Code *can* edit once Jax has SMTP-relay credentials to hand off via `supabase secrets set`). Use "Require SMTP Authentication" in the relay setup, not IP allowlisting — Supabase's outbound sending IPs aren't static/publishable.
- ~~`org_create_team` still auto-adds the creating director as `team_staff` head_coach~~ **Fixed (Phase 2 follow-up)** — see Session Log and the Organizations section above.
- ~~A large org/library/equipment restructuring queued~~ **Done, Phase 2 (seventeenth session continued)**: `org_create_team`/`canManageTeamInMode` retrofit, org multi-sport, org equipment-at-location auto-merge, Explore moved under Drills as "Team Libraries" with a per-coach breakdown, Club Library's colored bar, the per-team Equipment tab dropped in Org mode, and `copyDrillToMyLibrary` mode-awareness — see the Organizations and Library sections above and this session's Session Log entries for each. Multi-org library *switching* specifically reuses Home's existing org picker (no new UI needed) rather than being a separate feature, per the decision that scoped this pass.
- ~~Org mode silently reverts to Coach mode on a fresh full page load~~ **Fixed (Phase 2 continued)** — see Organizations section above.
- A director-facing, cross-team version of Development Pulse (Org mode) — explicitly out of scope for this pass; the shipped card is Coach mode only, one team at a time, and should not be reused with an arbitrary/rolled-up team for Org mode.
- `session_attendance.player_id`'s missing `ON DELETE` action (noted above) is still not fixed — a second, unrelated instance of a similar gap on `session_activity_log.practice_activity_id`/`.station_id` *was* found and fixed this session (see Goals & Insights/Development Pulse above), but that fix doesn't touch this one; still needs the same real product decision before a migration is safe.

## Working conventions

- No client-side insert loops for bulk operations — always one atomic, server-capped RPC (`create_practice_series` is the model to copy).
- "Archive, don't delete" (`archived_at`) almost everywhere; see RLS conventions above for the exceptions.
- Migrations are always dry-run (`BEGIN;...;ROLLBACK;`) before applying for real, and applied via `npx supabase db push` (the CLI is linked in this environment). If a migration is ever applied via the raw Management API instead, run `migration repair --status applied` before the next `db push --dry-run`, or the CLI will misjudge it as pending.
- Testing pattern for anything requiring a signed-in user: a disposable Supabase test account via the Admin API's `generate_link` + `verifyEmailOtp`, verify via direct SQL, delete after. Never test destructive flows on the real coach's account. Note: `generate_link` and the app's own "Send Code" share a rate-limit bucket.
- Sign-in email rate limit: `auth.rate_limit.email_sent` in `supabase/config.toml`, currently 30/hour — this is the limit that specifically governs custom-SMTP-sent emails (its own comment says so), not something Resend bypasses. The real ceiling in practice is likely Resend's own account-level plan cap (the project is on Resend's free tier per a code comment in `_shared/emailTemplate.ts`), since that applies across sign-in codes *and* the invite-notification Edge Functions combined — check the Resend dashboard directly for the current number rather than trusting either Supabase setting as authoritative.
- Timezone: always compute/display via the team's own `timezone` column, through `scheduledAtToTeamLocal`/`teamLocalToScheduledAt` — never the viewing device's zone, never bare UTC truncation (`localDateStr()` exists specifically to avoid the UTC-rollover bug described in Session Log).
- Secrets never go into migration files (git-tracked) — Vault or Edge Function secrets only. A `pg_net`-triggered function needs `verify_jwt:false` plus a shared-secret header.
- `.upsert()` still needs an UPDATE grant even for `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` — use `{onConflict, ignoreDuplicates:true}` when a table's RLS is insert/delete-only by design.

## Gotchas (read before you re-debug something already debugged)

This section exists so a root cause, once found, never has to be found twice. Two kinds: real bugs whose *pattern* is likely to recur, and operational/tooling quirks specific to this environment.

### Bugs whose root cause is worth remembering

- **Drill/equipment tags "not persisting"**: `syncDrillTags`/`syncDrillEquipment` never checked `.error` on insert/delete, so a rejected write (e.g. a failed RLS check) looked identical to success — the modal closed and data silently reverted. Not a broken permission model; purely missing error surfacing. Fixed by propagating `{error}` through the whole chain and keeping the modal open with a visible error on failure.
- **"Today" rolling over early for western-hemisphere coaches**: `new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10)` converts to UTC before truncating, which rolls "today" to the next calendar day hours before local midnight west of Greenwich (surfaced by a Phoenix, UTC-7 coach). Fixed with `localDateStr()` (same local-getter construction Month view's grid already used correctly) swapped in everywhere the UTC pattern appeared.
- **Team creation broken for any coach with no last name**: the auto-head-coach trigger copied `profiles.last_name` (optional) straight into `team_staff.last_name` (`NOT NULL`), silently rolling back the whole `teams` insert with no UI error. Fixed with `coalesce(...,'')`.
- **Schedule duration field un-typeable**: `+e.target.value || 60` meant clearing the field to type a new number evaluated to `60` on every keystroke (`+"" || 60`), so a coach could never get past the leading digit. Fixed by allowing a transient empty-string state, defaulting only on blur/submit.
- **iOS auto-zoom on some inputs but not others**: the shared input font-size was 15px, one pixel under the 16px threshold where iOS Safari auto-zooms on focus. Bumped to 16px app-wide.
- **`org_staff` member list silently empty**: the table has two FKs to `profiles` (`user_id`, `invited_by`), so a bare `profiles(...)` PostgREST embed was ambiguous and errored — silently swallowed by the function's own `catch`, returning `[]`. Fixed with an explicit `profiles!org_staff_user_id_fkey(...)` hint.
- **A director seeing a stranger's pending org invite**: `org_invites_select`'s RLS deliberately lets a director see every pending invite for orgs they administer (needed for a sent-invites list) — but the client rendered all of those as if addressed to the viewer. Fixed by filtering client-side to the caller's own verified email, matching the same server-side-truth principle the accept/decline RPCs already use.
- **The under-planned pill's warning glyph**: `⚠` (U+26A0) renders with its own built-in emoji-yellow color in most browsers regardless of the CSS `color` applied to it — looked like a permanent yellow warning triangle no matter the pill's actual state. Removed; the red under-planned state deliberately has no icon now. Don't reintroduce one there without checking it isn't in the emoji-default-presentation set.
- **`useBlocker` + an unconditional per-render effect is a real infinite-loop risk**: PlayerProfile's leave-prompt work added `useBlocker` alongside a *pre-existing* effect that re-registered `subViewBack` on every render (no deps, safe on its own for years) — combined, they fed back into each other and threw React's "Maximum update depth exceeded" the instant the screen mounted. If you add `useBlocker` to a screen that already has any unconditional every-render effect touching shared/context state, register that effect once (a ref for "always see the latest closure" instead of "re-run every render") rather than assuming the existing pattern is still safe.
- **A modal fed a snapshot object at open time goes stale the moment its own action refetches data** (twenty-ninth session, `PermissionsModal`): `App.jsx` held `permissionsCoach` (state) as the *whole coach object*, captured once when a roster row was clicked. Toggling a permission called `refreshTeams()`, which correctly updated `data.teams` with a brand-new coach object reflecting the new value — but `permissionsCoach` itself was never reassigned, so the already-open modal kept rendering the old object's old value. The toggle only looked "stuck" until the coach closed and reopened it (which re-derived a fresh object from the click). Fixed by storing just the id and deriving the live object from `team.coaches` on every render instead. **Any `openX(fullObject)`-style modal/panel state that's meant to stay open across its own refetch should hold an id and re-derive, never the object itself** — check for this pattern (`useState` seeded from a list-item click, no re-derivation afterward) before shipping a new modal that mutates the thing it's displaying.
- **A write to `teams` with no matching `refreshTeams()` call leaves the top-level `data.teams` cache stale, and any control that re-derives its initial value from that cache will silently "forget" a successful save** (thirty-first session, `updateGoalsWindowWeeks`): the RPC/update itself worked every time, confirmed by querying the database directly — the bug was purely that nothing on the client ever told the app's central `data.teams` array to refetch, so a `useState` seeded from `team.goalsWindowWeeks` kept reading the old number on the next fresh mount (switching subtabs and back, or a hard reload), even though the true value had already changed server-side. The report itself looked fine immediately after saving (its own `refreshReport()` call was already correct) — only the control's own displayed value regressed, and only after navigating away, which made it easy to miss in a quick manual check. **Any direct `supabase.from('teams').update(...)` (or similar top-level entity write) needs its caller to also invoke that entity's own refresh function** (`refreshTeams()`, `refreshLibrary()`, etc.) **if any mounted component re-derives state from the cached copy of what it just wrote** — grep for other bare `.update()` calls against `teams`/`practices`/etc. before assuming a save "took" just because the RPC returned no error. **That audit was actually run, forty-first session** (see Session Log): the one real instance found was `GoalsScreen.jsx`'s History "Delete Practice" action, which called `archivePractice()` but only refreshed its own report/history queries (`onChanged`→`refreshAll`), never `refreshPlanning()` — fixed by adding that call, the same one-line fix as here.
- **A second, confirmed instance of the exact same pattern above, found live during the twenty-sixth session's Execution Scorecard verification — no `useBlocker` involved this time, so the bug isn't specific to that hook.** `SessionHistoryDetail`'s own `setSubViewBack({onBack})` effect depended on `onBack` directly, and `onBack` is a fresh closure every time `GoalsScreen` renders (`onBack={() => setOpenSessionId(null)}`) — so the effect re-ran on every render, called `setSubViewBack` with a *new* object every time (that state lives in `Layout.jsx`, an ancestor), which re-rendered Layout and everything it wraps including this route, which recreated `onBack` again, forever. This one had apparently been dormant/borderline for a while (the component predates this session) — the extra state-driven re-renders this session's new scorecard fetch introduced were seemingly what tipped it into an actual runaway (confirmed via a render counter: climbing unbounded within a second of opening any session, not merely "a bit more than before"). Fixed the same way as PlayerProfile's version: a ref holding the latest `onBack`, registering the effect once on `[setSubViewBack]` alone. **The general lesson, now confirmed twice**: any effect in this codebase that takes a freshly-created callback prop (an inline arrow function passed down fresh every render) as a dependency, and writes that callback into shared/ancestor state, is a latent infinite-loop risk regardless of whether `useBlocker` is anywhere nearby — audit for this shape specifically (`useEffect(() => { someAncestorSetter({callbackProp}) }, [..., callbackProp])`) before assuming an existing screen is safe to build on top of. **That audit was actually run, forty-first session** (see Session Log): of the four `setSubViewBack` call sites in the app, two had already gotten the ref fix (this one, and `App.jsx`'s own) but two hadn't — `HistoryViewer` (`CommandScreen.jsx`) and `PracticeDetail.jsx`, both live-risky specifically when reached via `ScheduleScreen.jsx` (which passes `setSubViewBack` alongside a fresh inline `onBack` arrow every render; reached via `HomeScreen.jsx`, which doesn't pass `setSubViewBack` at all, neither was ever at risk). Both fixed with the same ref pattern.
- **A report's "Planned" bucket means different things in different RPCs on purpose here — mixing them silently produces a real, confusing UI bug, not just a documentation nit.** `get_team_goal_report`'s Planned is forward-looking (currently *scheduled* practices); the new `get_team_goal_trends`' Planned is backward-looking (what was actually saved for each *completed* session, so it compares the same cohort as Actual). The first draft of `GoalTrendCard` mixed the two — showing the *report's* forward-looking Planned percentage as the card's headline number, right next to a chart plotting the *trends RPC's* backward-looking Planned line — which live-verified as a real "Planned 0%" summary sitting directly beside a chart visibly showing ~55% planned. Fixed by deriving the card's own rolling summary from an average of the same weekly data the chart already plots, never touching `get_team_goal_report` for anything Trends-specific. If a future report needs *both* concepts on screen at once, label them distinctly (e.g. "Currently Scheduled" vs. "Historical Average") rather than calling both just "Planned."
- **Navigating straight to `/builder/:practiceId` via a bare `navigate()` (instead of the existing `goToBuilder()` helper) silently loads the wrong practice — a real bug, found live while wiring Development Pulse's Builder deep link, not merely a hard-refresh quirk.** `BuilderScreen`'s local state (`existingId`, `teamId`, `acts`, `locId`, etc.) is seeded via `useState(editP ? ... : ...)`, where `editP = data.practices.find(p=>p.id===editPracticeId)` and `editPracticeId` lives in top-level context (`App.jsx`). `BuilderRoute` only sets that context from the URL's `:practiceId` param inside a `useEffect` — one render tick *after* mount — specifically because every existing entry point (`goToBuilder(practiceId)`) already sets it synchronously before calling `navigate()`, so this effect is meant only as a direct-link/refresh fallback. A caller that skips `goToBuilder` and calls `navigate("/builder/"+id)` directly lands on `BuilderScreen`'s first render with `editPracticeId` still `null` — `editP` is `null`, and every one of those `useState` initializers locks in "blank new practice" defaults that React then never re-derives, even once `editPracticeId` correctly updates a moment later (a `useState` initializer only runs once, on mount). The route and URL are both correct; only the rendered content is wrong (today's date, no scheduled duration, empty Run of Practice) — easy to miss unless you specifically check the practice's real saved fields against what's rendered. Fixed by extending `goToBuilder()` with an optional `navState` param (forwarded straight into `navigate()`'s own `{state}`) rather than adding a second, parallel way into Builder. **Any future deep link into Builder must go through `goToBuilder()`, never a bare `navigate("/builder/"+id)`.**
- **A document-level "click outside collapses this" listener that checks `.closest()`/`.contains()` can misfire the instant the clicked element removes itself from the DOM inside its own `onClick` — and this class of bug is easy to reproduce, not a one-off.** Direct feedback: in Builder's station-block editor, choosing "Dominant Hand" from the Group By dropdown, or a drill from a station's "Choose from Library" picker, kicked the coach out of the whole station block back to the collapsed Run of Practice view — they had to tap back in and lost their place. Root cause, confirmed by reading the code rather than guessing: both selections call a state setter (`setGroupByOpen(false)`, `setLibraryPickerIdx(null)`) that unmounts the clicked option element *before* a `document`-level `click` listener (added for "tapping outside the expanded row collapses it") runs later in the same event's bubble phase. `.closest()`/`.contains()` evaluate against the DOM *as it currently exists* — by the time the outside-click handler runs, the clicked element is already gone, so it looks identical to a click that was never inside the row at all, and the handler collapses it. Fixed by switching the outside-click check to `event.composedPath()`, which is captured at dispatch time (before any synchronous handler has a chance to mutate the tree) and lists every element the click actually passed through, immune to later DOM mutations. **Any future "click outside to close/collapse" listener in this codebase should default to `composedPath()` over `.closest()`/`.contains()`** unless there's a specific reason the current-DOM-state check is required — the failure mode (an option that unmounts itself on select) is common enough to hit again. A second fix in the same area, unconfirmed against the original live symptom but low-risk: `collapseAndScroll`'s `scrollIntoView` call, previously scheduled with one `requestAnimationFrame`, now nests a second one — a plausible race with the `ResizeObserver`-driven `runOfPracticeH` state (measuring the green backdrop's height) recalculating layout on the same frame a very tall station block collapses, which could land the scroll position mid-recalculation.
- **`.insert(rows).select()` does not guarantee the returned rows come back in the same order they were sent — zipping a response array against the original request array by index is a latent correctness risk, found (not yet confirmed to have caused a real observed bug, but a genuine one on inspection) while investigating a different, ultimately-false-alarm symptom in station-group saving** (thirty-second session continued, `saveSessionGroups`, `src/supabase.js`): the function inserts one row per player group, then zipped the returned `groupRows` against the original `groups` array positionally (`groupRows.forEach((gr,i) => groups[i]...)`) to attach each group's member-player-ids to the right just-created `group_id`. If PostgREST/Supabase ever returns `.select()` rows in a different order than the insert payload, this pairs a group's row with a *different* group's player list. Fixed to zip by the row's own `group_number` column instead (`groups[gr.group_number-1]`) — a stable key, not response order. **General lesson: any `.insert(rows).select()` call in this codebase whose result is consumed by array-index pairing against the original request array should be audited and switched to a real key-based join instead.**
- **`SortableActivityRow`'s per-row stacking context traps any popover inside it, and the fix generalizes beyond this one component.** Direct feedback: a drill's ellipsis dropdown menu in the Library's drag-sortable "My Drills" list was visually clipped by the *next* row's opaque background — including on the *last* row in the list, ruling out a simple "menu extends past the container" theory. Root cause: `SortableActivityRow` (`ActivityConfigs.jsx`, shared by Builder and the Library drill list) gives every row `position:relative` plus an explicit `zIndex` (at minimum `1`, even when not dragging/sticky) — that combination always creates a new CSS stacking context, and every row gets the *same* zIndex level, so z-index comparisons for something deep inside one row (a `.mini-menu` at `z-index:50`) only resolve *within that row's own stacking context* and can never escape to paint above a sibling row, which is a separate context painted after it in DOM order regardless of any z-index set inside the first one. No amount of raising the menu's own z-index fixes this — the trap is one level up. Fixed with a new `raised` prop on `SortableActivityRow` that bumps that specific row's own zIndex above its siblings while something inside it needs to render over what comes after it (`zIndex:10` when raised, vs. the normal `1`). Any future popover/dropdown living inside a `SortableActivityRow` (or any other component using this same "give every item a zIndex for legitimate reasons" pattern) needs the same treatment — check for exactly this shape before assuming a z-index bump on the popover itself will work.
- **A discarded/uncaptured Supabase `{error}` is this codebase's single most-repeated root cause, and it was still live in the single most central write path in the app.** Already confirmed four separate times before this (drill tags, Add Player, Save-as-Template, the consultation form) — forty-first session swept for more instances and found `savePracticeTree`'s own **update** branch (`src/supabase.js`, the path every Builder Save takes when editing an already-scheduled practice, the common case): the sibling **insert** branch two lines below it correctly checks `{error}` and returns early, but the update branch fired `.update(row)` with no captured return value at all, so a rejected update (RLS edge case, network blip) fell straight through into saving the activities/stations and returned a clean success — the coach would see "Saved" while the practice's own date/time/location/duration silently never changed. Fixed to match the insert branch. A second, related instance: `setPlannedAbsences` didn't even capture `{error}` from either of its two writes, and its caller (`AbsencePicker.save()`) never checked any of its three absence-write functions' return values before unconditionally closing the modal — meaning a rejected "who's out" save looked identical to a successful one, silently, while Builder's own station-assignment code trusts that data to keep an absent player off a station. Fixed both ends: the data-layer function now returns its errors, and the caller now surfaces them with a real error message and keeps the modal open, the same convention `ModalLayer.jsx` already established. **Any `await supabase.from(...).update/insert/upsert/delete(...)` whose result isn't captured into `{error}` and checked by its caller should be treated as a live footgun, not a style nit** — grep for bare `await supabase.from(` with no destructured return before assuming a write path is safe.
- **A reentrancy guard implemented with plain `useState` (`if (busy) return; setBusy(true)`) is unsafe under a fast double-tap, because `setState` doesn't take effect until the next render — a second call can pass the check before the first render commits.** This project already learned this once (`ModalLayer.jsx`'s `save()`, then ported to `SeriesWizard.jsx`'s `confirm()`) but the fix was never swept to other save/confirm actions with the same shape. Forty-first session found and fixed two more: `FuturePracticeGuardModal.jsx`'s `run()` (guards "Run This Plan as a New Practice," which inserts a brand-new practice row — a double-tap there could create two duplicate practices) and `AbsencePicker.jsx`'s `save()`. Both ported the same `useRef(false)` guard already proven in the other two spots. **Any Save/Confirm/Run action gated only by a `useState` boolean (not a paired `useRef`) is a candidate for this exact bug** — the `disabled={busy}` attribute on the button doesn't prevent it, since that disabling also only takes effect on the next render.

### Operational gotchas worth not re-learning
- `supabase config push` (verified against the actual Supabase CLI Go source, not guessed) unconditionally overwrites only `site_url`/`additional_redirect_urls`; everything else (SMTP, email templates, MFA, rate limits) is nil-gated and untouched unless locally set in `config.toml` — check those two values are still correct production values before ever running this, not stale localhost/dev placeholders. Piping `"n"` to its interactive per-service confirmation prompts did **not** reliably decline in past testing — treat any piped-input dry run against this CLI with suspicion. Its per-service `up_to_date` output on a completed push is a **success** status, not a "nothing changed" signal — don't read it as a no-op.
- `service_role` bypasses RLS but not base-table GRANTs (see RLS conventions above) — this has caused silent empty results from more than one new Edge Function.
- No automated test suite exists anywhere in this project, by design — there's no local Supabase environment in this setup, so everything is verified empirically in a live browser against production, using disposable test accounts, before being called done.
- Edge Functions are **not** auto-deployed by pushing to `main` (only the Vercel frontend is) — `npx supabase functions deploy <name>` is required any time an edge function or a shared file it bundles (`_shared/*.ts`) changes. Every function that imports a changed shared file needs its own redeploy; they each bundle their own copy at deploy time, not a live shared reference.
- `read_console_messages` returned an identical stale error after a real fix landed, across a full dev-server restart and several genuinely fresh page loads (confirmed via `script[type=module]` src querystrings) — don't treat it as authoritative when re-verifying a fix in a long-running tab/session. Cross-check with an independently in-page-patched `console.error` counter (or a fresh screenshot/interaction check) before concluding a fix didn't work. **Reconfirmed useful this session** for a different reason too: the counter technique is also how a real infinite-render-loop bug (see Gotchas above) was confirmed as *actually looping* rather than stale/buffered output — a raw error count climbing across two back-to-back reads, immune to any buffering question, is a stronger signal than either a screenshot or the console-messages tool alone. **Reconfirmed a third time, thirty-second session continued, and this occurrence is worth its own note because it nearly triggered a wasted "fix"**: after a live station-player-move, `saveSessionGroups` appeared to be throwing (`read_console_messages` showed two stale `saveSessionGroups: [object Object]` entries) *and* a direct DB query showed zero `session_groups` rows — looking like a genuine, reproducible bug in the new Practice Setup station-reassignment code. The DB symptom turned out to be self-inflicted: manual `curl` inserts made *during the investigation itself* (probing whether RLS/schema allowed the write at all, done via the real REST API with the test account's own JWT — both single-row and batch inserts succeeded, ruling out RLS) had polluted `fetchLatestGroups`' "resolve latest by matching `created_at`" logic for that exact session+activity key, since the curl rows' timestamps didn't line up with the app's own subsequent writes. After deleting the contaminating rows and re-testing clean (fresh seed, fresh page load, an in-page `console.error` patch), the write worked correctly end-to-end and the two console errors never recurred — they were leftover buffered output from a much earlier interaction in the same long-lived browser tab, consistent with the two prior entries above. **Compounding lesson beyond "don't trust `read_console_messages`": manually inserting rows into an app table *while debugging that same table's "latest row" resolution logic* can itself corrupt the very state you're trying to observe** — prefer fully scripted seed/cleanup (a fresh practice, a fresh page load) over ad hoc `curl`/SQL probes against a table whose read path depends on relative row ordering or timestamps, or clean up probe rows immediately and re-test from a known-clean state before drawing any conclusion.
- **`supabase db diff --linked` and `db push --dry-run` don't do what their names suggest in this environment.** `db diff`/`db push --dry-run` both require a local Docker shadow database to actually diff or dry-run SQL against (`Cannot connect to the Docker daemon`) — with no Docker in this setup (same "no local Supabase environment" constraint as everywhere else in this file), `db push --dry-run` silently degrades to only checking the CLI's own applied-migrations bookkeeping table, never actually executing a single line of the pending SQL. The real dry-run mechanism this project's own Working Conventions ask for (`BEGIN;...ROLLBACK;`) needs a different command: `supabase db query --linked --file <path>`, which runs arbitrary SQL directly against the linked production database via the Management API — wrap a migration file's contents in `BEGIN;`/`ROLLBACK;` in a scratch file and pass that. This also validates runtime correctness, not just syntax: adding `set local role authenticated; select set_config('request.jwt.claim.sub','<a real user's uuid>',true);` before calling a `security definer` RPC lets you exercise its real `can_access_team`/`can_manage_team` authorization checks and see real output against real data, still safely rolled back. Twenty-sixth session used this to validate all five new RPCs against the real coach's own production data before ever running `db push` for real.

---



## Persistent design themes (longer-running decisions, not single sessions)

These track *why* something is shaped the way it is, across many sessions — read the numbered sub-entries in order where present, since the design evolved.

### Auth
- Email OTP chosen over magic links specifically because magic links broke for the installed-homescreen-PWA sign-in case. `admin.inviteUserByEmail` is avoided for staff invites for the same reason (it's a magic link) — a custom `pg_net` + Resend Edge Function handles that notification instead.


### Skill tags & taxonomy
- Skill tags are seeded as **personal** `scope='coach'` copies per coach rather than true `scope='global'` rows: global rows are only writable by `service_role`/`is_org_admin` under existing RLS, so one coach archiving a shared global tag would silently remove it for every other coach. Per-coach seeding sidesteps that with zero RLS changes, reusing the machinery that already existed for a coach's own custom tags. `ensureDefaultSkillTags` re-runs (idempotently) on every sign-in so a coach picks up new tags from later migrations, not just what existed at signup.
- `skill_categories` has no app-level INSERT policy anywhere, by design — Jax seeds/curates these directly. This is why 4 of the app's 12 sports (Softball, Hockey, Tennis, Swimming) still have zero taxonomy: nobody has seeded them yet, not a technical blocker.
- A unified taxonomy and public-catalog drill content were drafted for a 6-sport initial pass (JSON-per-sport deliverable, so 150+ drills could be imported mechanically rather than hand-transcribed) with category names deliberately reused across sports on purpose (enables cross-sport reporting). A system publisher profile (e.g. `content@runofpractice.com`) was the chosen design for content attribution over relaxing ownership constraints — real `profiles` row means the existing `activity_has_owner` constraint needs no changes, and gives real "Published by Run of Practice" attribution for free. **This plan is what became the shipped Public Library feature** (see Library section above) for Baseball, Basketball, Football, Soccer, Lacrosse, and Volleyball — the original planning doc undersold this by never being updated to say so. Softball/Hockey/Tennis/Swimming were never part of this pass and still have no categories, tags, or public-library content.
