### Sharing & org model (schema evolved twice — read in order)
1. **Original design (2026-07-06)**: a single `shared_with_organization_id` column on `activity_library`/`templates` — private by default, share with exactly one org, org-owned "Org Library" kept as a separate concept from coach-owned-shared-into-org "From Our Coaches." Public visibility explicitly deferred entirely (became the Public Library work above). The "copy, don't reference" rule was set here specifically to avoid a shared thing referencing something its viewers can't see (this bug class was hit five times during the original schema build) — copying a shared drill resolves equipment by name into the recipient's own pool rather than pointing at the sharer's rows.
2. **First org-model implementation (2026-07-21)**: `organization_members` (owner/admin/coach/viewer, zero real rows ever) was replaced wholesale by `org_staff` (single `director` role, extensible without a migration later). New RPCs (`org_create_team`, `org_assign_team_staff`, `org_assign_player`) were added as explicit auditable entry points, even though the underlying RLS already permitted an org-admin's direct write — deliberately not narrowed, since narrowing wasn't asked for. The single-org `shared_with_organization_id` column was recognized as insufficient the same day (a coach can be director of one org while coaching a team in a different org — a single column can't hold "share with more than one"), so it was replaced by the `activity_library_org_shares` join table before this design ever shipped as originally spec'd. `org_invites` got a real accept/decline consent flow (never the informal `team_staff` auto-link pattern) specifically because joining an org is a bigger commitment than being added to a team roster.
3. **Rework into Coach/Org mode (later session)**: the standalone Org Home page (`/org/:orgId`) was retired and folded directly into `HomeScreen.jsx` behind a persisted Coach/Org toggle — this is why `fetchPendingOrgInvites`/`orgAssignTeamStaff`/`orgAssignPlayer`/`promoteDrillToOrgLibrary` ended up as dead exports (superseded by different call patterns) cleaned up in the thirteenth session above. Team/asset/location/skill-tag creation inside Org mode reuses the same coach-or-org ownership pattern the rest of the app already had (`createOrgLocation`/`createOrgAsset`/`createOrgSkillTag` — no new RPCs needed, existing RLS already permitted a director's direct insert with `organization_id` set).


### Navigation (restructured multiple times — read in order)
1. Home originally had a pill/chip row for picking a team — removed entirely (not reskinned) once direct user feedback said it read as a filter control (tap to toggle in place) rather than navigation (tap to leave the page). Replaced by a dedicated global **Teams** tab, reachable from anywhere including Library.
2. The team workspace went from a 4-tab shape (Schedule/Plan/Goals/Team) to a 3-tab shape (Schedule/Plan/Team, Goals folded into Plan as a sub-toggle) — Plan's original scope had never actually been built past a placeholder, so nothing real was lost merging it in. Team Equipment/Player Gear moved out of global Settings into the Team tab and became sport-aware (auto-stamps the current team's sport, filters lists to `sport === team.sport || 'General'`) once showing all-sport gear while coaching one team's context was flagged as "weird." Skill Tags and Locations stayed in global Settings deliberately — they're coach-wide vocabulary/resources with no team ownership in the schema, so editing them from inside one team's context risked silently rippling to every other team.
3. Later flattened further: the nested Schedule/Plan/Team (with Plan hiding a Build/Goals toggle, Team hiding a Roster/Equipment toggle) became 5 direct top-row routes so nothing was two taps deep — then reduced to the current 4 (Schedule/Roster/Equipment/Goals & Insights) once Build's tab-bar entry was dropped as redundant with Home/Schedule's own "+ Practice" entry points (its route/component lived on as dead code until the thirteenth session).
4. The original `App.jsx` `view`-string state machine with manual `window.location.pathname` regex checks was replaced by real `react-router-dom` v6 routes; the `guardedSetView`/`priorView` unsaved-changes mechanism was replaced by `useBlocker` + `beforeunload` (the old mechanism never protected against a hard refresh or tab close either).
5. The old single "Manage" screen mixed coaching content and one-time setup in one junk drawer. Split: `SettingsScreen.jsx` (Account, Locations, Equipment & Gear, Skill Tags) for setup; Library's My Library/Explore structure for content; `ManageScreen` now purely serves the team workspace's Team tab. A critical catch made during planning, not after: the old Manage "My Teams" list held the app's only reachable "+ Team" button — it was rehomed onto the new Teams tab's header before the old list was deleted, not after.


### Landing page & legal
- No real product screenshots on the landing page, and no stylized mockups either — visuals are built from the real app's own CSS classes with realistic sample data, so they stay pixel-consistent with the actual UI as it changes, with one-line prop slots left for real screenshots later. Copy is deliberately sport/level-agnostic ("coaches"/"players", not "youth"/"kids"/"parents"), per an explicit positioning decision.
- An inline landing-page feedback form was removed after the user felt the copy read as AI-generated (em dashes, listy cadence) — em dashes and choppy list prose were removed project-wide on that page at the same time. The backing `submit_public_feedback` RPC/table were deliberately kept (see Library section) in case a future non-authenticated contact path wants them.


### Live sessions & timing
- The "leave a live session without ending it" feature was judged safe specifically because the live-run timer/stage was already fully re-derived from `session.current_phase_started_at` on mount (built earlier for multi-coach handoff/reconnect) — unmount/remount naturally resumes correctly, no separate persistence needed.
- Real audio-cue files via `<audio>` elements plus a silent background loop were chosen over an earlier, abandoned approach (a raw `AudioContext` oscillator tone) because Safari blocks Web Audio output but has a real background-audio exemption for actual media elements. The new tone was added strictly additively alongside the existing, working `speechSynthesis` cues first, specifically so a tone failing on some device wouldn't break the fallback — the redundant spoken "Next up!" cue was only removed later, after confirming the tone worked. `HelperView` (parent-facing) was deliberately not given the same treatment — the original complaint was specifically about the coach's own phone locking mid-session.
- `session_activity_log` has no stored/computed duration column (duration = `ended_at - started_at`, null if never explicitly ended) and no backfill/cleanup mechanism for a dangling open row from an abandoned session — flagged as needing a product decision (exclude? clamp to session end? clamp to now?) with no existing convention to follow yet.


### Goals & Insights architecture (twenty-sixth session)
- Every category-attribution calculation across Trends, Next Practice Guidance, Builder Goal Guidance, the Execution Scorecard, and Drill Insights is built on the same four small SQL functions and one composite type (`activity_minute_row`), extracted once from `get_team_goal_report`'s own logic rather than copied five more times — this project already paid for that mistake once (the `get_team_goal_report`/`set_team_goals` stale-column incident, seventeenth session) and the whole point of extracting shared functions instead of shared *documentation* is that a fix to the real logic only has to happen in one place. The functions are deliberately `security definer` with `revoke all from public` (not `grant ... to authenticated`) — they take a bare id with no authorization check of their own, trusting the outer RPC to have already verified access, which only works because PostgREST can't reach them directly once PUBLIC execute is revoked. Don't add a new one of these without the same revoke, or it becomes a direct, unauthenticated-relative-to-content data leak.
- Two genuinely different "Planned" concepts coexist on purpose (`get_team_goal_report`'s forward-looking vs. `get_team_goal_trends`'s backward-looking, completed-session-cohort version) — see the Gotcha above for the real bug this caused once already when a component quietly assumed they were interchangeable. Any future report that needs its own notion of "planned" should ask which cohort it actually wants before reusing either RPC's number by convention.
- Builder's projection math (`categoryMinutesForPracticeActivities`/`calculateProjectedGoalImpact`) is intentionally pure client-side JS, not a server round trip per keystroke — the baseline (rolling history) is fetched once per team selection and cached; every recalculation as the draft changes is a local `useMemo`. This is the same "don't send the unsaved draft to a new RPC on every change" instruction the original spec called for, and mirrors how Builder's own local `acts` state has always worked (nothing round-trips to the server until Save).
- The Execution Scorecard and Drill Insights share one on-plan tolerance constant (`ON_PLAN_TOLERANCE_SECONDS = 60`, `classifyDurationVariance`) specifically so "extended"/"shortened"/"on plan" means the same thing regardless of which screen a coach is looking at — a practice-level scorecard and a drill-level insights view giving different verdicts on the same underlying timing data would undermine trust in both.


## Session Log (chronological history — append new sessions here)

Every session's work gets one dated, numbered entry below, **newest first**. When you finish a session:

1. Write a new entry using the same voice as the ones below: what was asked, what was actually built (call out anything that turned out to already exist, or that you deliberately didn't build), any real bug found along the way (not just requested changes), and how it was verified.
2. Insert it at the **top** of this section (immediately below this instructions block), not the bottom — this list reads newest-first.
3. Update "Last updated" and the Status line at the top of this file to reflect the new latest session.
4. If the session changed a known gap, a working convention, added a real gotcha, or changed the feature inventory, edit that section directly — don't rely on someone reading the whole log to find out the app now works differently.

Sessions are grouped by theme rather than strictly re-sorted when a later session revisits an earlier decision — check the inline dates in each entry if exact ordering matters. Numbering is sequential per work session (a same-day follow-up is "Nth session continued," not a new number).

### Forty-first session (2026-08-08): a stability sweep ahead of the planned August test, not a feature session

Prompted by a conversation about whether to build new screens (a desktop/big-screen layout, an iPad layout, a "cast" spectator display) before or after the upcoming major test. Recommendation given: stabilize first, defer the new surfaces — none of them are prerequisites, and the app has no automated test suite to catch regressions from three new, previously-unbuilt screens landing right before a real multi-user test. That led to a more specific question: does this codebase have known-unstable *shapes*, beyond the individual bugs already logged in Gotchas, that are worth sweeping for and hardening before that test? It does. Every Gotchas entry that already says "audit for this shape elsewhere" was actually audited this session, plus one new pattern (silently-discarded Supabase errors) that recurs often enough in this file's own history (four prior confirmed instances) to be worth checking for directly rather than waiting to hit a fifth.

**Found and fixed, real (not hypothetical) instances of already-documented bug shapes:**
- **Stale top-level cache** (same shape as `updateGoalsWindowWeeks`): `GoalsScreen.jsx`'s History "Delete Practice" (`doDeletePractice`) called `archivePractice()` but never called `refreshPlanning()` — only `onChanged`→`refreshAll`, which refreshes the report/history queries, not the top-level `data.practices` cache Schedule's Agenda/Month views read from. `refreshPlanning` was already sitting unused as a prop. One-line fix: call it.
- **Ancestor-state setter fed a fresh closure every render** (the exact shape already found and fixed twice, PlayerProfile and `SessionHistoryDetail`): two of the app's four `setSubViewBack` call sites never got the ref-based fix — `HistoryViewer` (`CommandScreen.jsx`) and `PracticeDetail.jsx`, both live-risky specifically when reached through `ScheduleScreen.jsx` (passes `setSubViewBack` alongside a fresh inline `onBack` every render; `HomeScreen.jsx` doesn't pass `setSubViewBack` at all, so neither was ever at risk from that entry point). Fixed both with the same `useRef`-holds-latest-callback pattern already proven twice in this codebase.
- **`.insert(rows).select()` zipped by array index, the click-outside `.closest()`/`.contains()` pattern, and the `SortableActivityRow` stacking-context trap** — all audited, all clean. Each bug shape turned out to be genuinely contained to the one instance already fixed; nothing else in the app has the same structural shape (confirmed by grepping for the only other multi-row `.insert().select()` calls, the only other `document`-level click listener, and the only other `dnd-kit`/sortable component in the app).

**Two new instances of a bug class this file already knew about generically but had never swept for by name — discarded Supabase write errors:**
- `savePracticeTree`'s **update** branch (`src/supabase.js`) — the path every Builder Save takes when editing an already-scheduled practice — fired `.update(row)` with no captured `{error}` at all, unlike its own sibling insert branch two lines below. A rejected update would silently proceed into saving activities/stations and return a clean success, with the practice's own date/time/location/duration never actually persisted. This is the single most central write path in the app; fixed to match the insert branch's existing correct pattern.
- `setPlannedAbsences` didn't capture `{error}` from either of its two writes, and its caller `AbsencePicker.save()` never checked any of its three absence-write calls before unconditionally closing the modal — a rejected "who's out" save looked identical to success, silently, while Builder's station-assignment logic trusts that data to exclude an absent player. Fixed both ends: `setPlannedAbsences` now returns its errors, `AbsencePicker` now checks all three calls, shows a real error message, and keeps the modal open on failure (`saveError` state, same convention `ModalLayer.jsx` already uses).

**One new instance of the already-known-but-unswept `useState`-only reentrancy race** (`ModalLayer.jsx`'s `save()`, ported once already to `SeriesWizard.jsx`'s `confirm()`, never swept further): `FuturePracticeGuardModal.jsx`'s `run()` (a fast double-tap on "Run This Plan as a New Practice" could `savePracticeTree(null,...)` twice, creating duplicate practices) and `AbsencePicker.jsx`'s `save()` both guarded reentrancy with plain state instead of a ref. Both ported the same `useRef(false)` guard.

Both new bug-shape generalizations (discarded write errors, state-only reentrancy guards) added to Gotchas above with pointers to every fixed instance, so a future sweep has a named pattern to grep for instead of re-discovering it.

**Verified**: `npm run check` (clean build) after each fix, plus a local dev-server load with console-error checking (clean).

### Forty-first session continued (2026-08-08): live verification of all five stability-sweep fixes, using this file's own disposable-account convention

The prior entry's fixes were only build-checked and code-traced, not live-verified, and said so explicitly. Followed through this same session: minted a disposable coach account via the documented `generate_link`+`verifyOtp` pattern, seeded a team/roster/two practices (one scheduled ~5h out, one completed with a real live-session/activity-log/attendance trail) directly through the authenticated user's own client (not service-role — `teams`/`players`/`practices`/etc. all turned out to have no `INSERT`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE` grant for `service_role`, only `SELECT` on some tables and not others; every seed write had to go through the same RLS-respecting path a real coach's session uses), injected the minted session into a local dev server's `localStorage` per the documented `sb-<project-ref>-auth-token` convention, and drove the real UI. All five fixes confirmed working:
- **History delete → Schedule staleness (fix 1)**: deleted the seeded completed session from Goals & Insights' History; Schedule's Month view dropped its dot immediately, no reload, confirming `refreshPlanning()` actually fires now.
- **`setSubViewBack` ref fix (fix 2)**: opened both `PracticeDetail` and `HistoryViewer` via `ScheduleScreen` (the only at-risk entry point), confirmed clean render, no console errors, and correct back-navigation through Layout's colored bar both times.
- **`savePracticeTree` update-branch error (fix 3)**: edited the seeded practice's duration in Builder (reached via `PracticeDetail`'s Edit button → `goToBuilder()`, the supported path — a raw `/builder/:id` URL, tried first, reproduces the *already-documented, already-understood* `editPracticeId`-timing bug this file's Gotchas describes, not a regression from this session's fix), saved, and confirmed via a direct authenticated read that `scheduled_duration_minutes` actually persisted server-side.
- **`setPlannedAbsences`/`AbsencePicker` error surfacing (fix 4)**: marked a player out via "Who's Out?", confirmed the save succeeded and Practice Detail reflected "Out: Casey T" with no regression. (The negative/error path itself wasn't exercised — see below, that's exactly what ended up happening by accident during teardown.)
- **`FuturePracticeGuardModal` reentrancy (fix 5)**: rapid double-clicked "Run This Plan as a New Practice" on a >2h-out practice; a direct authenticated query confirmed exactly one new `practices` row was created, not two.

**Teardown hit two real FK gaps, one already known, one new.** `session_attendance.player_id`'s already-documented missing-`ON DELETE`-action gap blocked deletion (this session's own seeded attendance rows), worked around the documented way (`db query --linked`, which bypasses the same missing-grant problem `session_attendance` already had). A **second, previously undocumented instance** surfaced once that was cleared: `planned_absences.noted_by` (populated by this session's own fix-4 test, marking a player out) has the identical gap, discovered via a raw `delete from auth.users ... rollback` dry run that surfaced the real Postgres constraint error the Admin API's own 500 response was hiding. Added to Known Gaps above. Account and all seeded rows fully torn down afterward (manual dependency-ordered SQL delete via `db query --linked`, then `admin.deleteUser`, confirmed successful); all throwaway scripts removed, nothing committed.

### Fortieth session (2026-08-07): an accepted coach invite kept showing as pending on the inviting coach's own screen

A real, confirmed bug: daabears1213@gmail.com invited Jax as an assistant coach, Jax got the popup and accepted, but daabears1213's own Coaches tab still showed the invite as pending. Traced the whole accept path server-side first since this smelled like it could be the same class of bug as the recent Add Player fix (a write silently not landing): it isn't. `accept_team_invite` is one transaction that correctly flips the `team_invites` row to `'accepted'` and creates the real `team_staff` row, both writes on the exact row the popup was built from, no email-mismatch or orphaned-row path found. The actual bug is client-side: `data.teams` (which is what `team.coaches`/`team.invites` on the Coaches tab actually reads) is fetched once at login and lives in state that persists across in-app navigation, and nothing refetches it in response to someone else's write, there's no realtime subscription on `team_staff`/`team_invites`, no poll, nothing. The one feature built specifically to bridge this gap, the "X accepted your invite, set up their permissions" card on Home, didn't actually close it: `acknowledgeJoinNotice` (its Dismiss handler) only called `refreshLibrary()`, never `refreshTeams()`, and "Set Up Permissions" just navigated straight to the Coaches tab with zero refetch, both in `HomeScreen.jsx`. So even the intended notification path left the inviter looking at the same stale roster. Fixed three ways: `acknowledgeJoinNotice` now also calls `refreshTeams()`; "Set Up Permissions" now awaits `refreshTeams()` before navigating, so the deep-link handler that auto-opens the Permissions modal for the newly-accepted coach (`RostersTab`, `App.jsx`) actually finds them instead of racing stale state; and `RostersTab` itself now refetches on mount, so navigating to Coaches directly (bypassing the Home card entirely, the more common path) always reflects the real state rather than whatever was cached at last login. No schema changes. Verified with `npm run check`; same no-test-credentials limitation as the rest of this session, verified by tracing the exact prop plumbing and the RPC's transaction body rather than a live two-account browser session.

### Thirty-ninth session continued again (2026-08-07): a slow Create Schedule save looked like it had failed, and impatient re-taps could have made it worse

Jax reported Create Schedule appeared to fail; on retry it worked but took a while. Investigated thoroughly (RLS, the `create_practice_series` RPC's own guards, error surfacing) and found nothing that would cause a hard failure in the normal case, the existing error handling already renders any real backend failure on screen rather than swallowing it (unlike the earlier addPlayer bug). The RPC inserts a `practice_series` row plus one `practices` row per occurrence in a single call, so a full season (dozens of rows) over a slow connection can legitimately take several seconds with zero feedback beyond a static "Creating..." label, easy to mistake for a hang. Jax also noticed the coach kept tapping the button while waiting. The button was already `disabled` during a save, but `SeriesWizard.jsx`'s `confirm()` only guarded reentrancy with React state (`if (saving) return;`), the same unsafe-under-rapid-taps pattern already identified and fixed once this session in `ModalLayer.jsx`'s `save()`. Ported that same `useRef` guard here for real defense-in-depth, disabled the Back button during a save too (previously still clickable, which could have let a coach navigate away and re-submit while the original request was still in flight), and added a visible "Creating N practices, this can take a few seconds for a full season, please wait" message under the button while saving. No schema changes. Verified with `npm run check`; same no-test-credentials limitation as the rest of this session, verified by tracing the exact reentrancy-guard pattern against its own working precedent in `ModalLayer.jsx` rather than a live browser session.

### Thirty-ninth session continued (2026-08-07): a coach scheduling a practice had no way to add a new location without losing the form

Jax's ask: when scheduling a practice, a coach should be able to add a location right there, whether they have zero locations saved yet or already have some but need a new one, without losing the date/time/team they'd already filled in. Found the flow already half-handled this: `SchedulePracticeModal.jsx` (the quick one-off scheduler) swaps its Location `<select>` for a "+ Add a Location" button when a coach has zero locations, opening `AddLocationDialog` inline and auto-selecting the result on return, but once a coach has even one location saved, that fallback disappears and there was no way to add another without canceling the whole form. `SeriesWizard.jsx` (the recurring-schedule wizard) was worse: no inline-add at all, in any state, so an empty-locations coach had to abandon the wizard just to create their first location. Builder's own Location field already solved this correctly (task #24, an earlier session) with a `<select>` that has a trailing "+ Add New Location..." option alongside the empty-state button, so both fixes just copy that existing three-way pattern rather than invent a new one. `SeriesWizard` needed a bit more: it never imported `AddLocationDialog` or tracked `showAddLocation` state, and `ScheduleScreen.jsx` wasn't passing it a `refreshPlanning` prop at all, added so a newly-created location actually shows up in `data.locations` afterward. No schema changes, no new components. Verified with `npm run check`; this app has no test credentials available in this environment, so the change was verified by tracing the exact prop plumbing and comparing byte-for-byte against Builder's already-working reference implementation, not through a live browser session.

### Thirty-ninth session (2026-08-07): a new coach's Add Player save silently failed with no error shown

Jax reported a new coach added a player, tapped Save, saw "Saving..." for a while, and it appeared to fail. Traced the whole path: the "+ Add" button on the Players tab is already gated to head coaches only (`canManageTeamInMode`), so this wasn't a permissions gap, it was a real code bug in the save handler shared by every add/edit modal (`ModalLayer.jsx`). `addActivity`/`editActivity` capture their Supabase call's return value into `res` and a shared guard right after (`if(res&&res.error){setSaveError(...);return;}`) surfaces any failure and keeps the modal open. `addPlayer` never captured that return value, so a failed `createPlayer` insert (RLS rejection, network blip, anything) fell straight through to the unconditional `closeModal()` at the end, exactly as if it had succeeded: no error message, no player on the roster. The "Saving..." state itself was never actually stuck (a `finally` block always clears it), it just looked that way because the eventual outcome, silent failure, gave no indication anything had gone wrong. Fixed by capturing `res` the same way the drill-save paths already do and only refreshing the roster on success. No schema changes. Verified with `npm run check`; the actual failure trigger (why the insert itself errored for this specific coach) couldn't be reproduced without live credentials, so if it recurs the new error message will now say so instead of failing silently, which should make the real cause easy to see next time.

### Thirty-eighth session continued (2026-08-07): HelperView never had a background audio session, and the station-block phase label always said "Station" regardless of rotate vs. static

Two more items, same day. The first is a real, confirmed bug with real consequences; the second is a wording fix.

**HelperView's audio dies once the phone locks or the tab backgrounds, and the coach's own view doesn't have this problem.** Jax noticed the coach's PWA shows a "now playing" audio indicator and a helper's plain browser tab doesn't. Confirmed by reading the code: the entire background-audio-session setup (a silent looping audio file, Wake Lock, and Media Session API registration with real metadata) only ever existed on the coach's own `CommandScreen`. HelperView had none of it. Without a registered media session, mobile browsers (especially iOS Safari) are aggressive about suspending a backgrounded tab's audio and timers, since there's nothing telling the OS this tab is doing anything worth keeping alive. This is exactly the situation a helper's link is in: opened as a plain browser tab, not installed as a PWA, so it gets the least benefit-of-the-doubt from the OS of any viewer in the app. Fixed by mirroring the coach's own implementation in HelperView, wired into its existing Turn On Audio prompt and header toggle. Same underlying gap now closed for the last remaining viewer type that had it.

**Station-block phase label wording.** The live header always read "Station X of Y" whether the block rotates or not, which for a rotating block reads as "there are X stations" when what's actually incrementing is the round, not the station count. Now reads "Rotation X of Y" while rotating, and "Station Breakouts" (instead of the generic "Station Block") when the coach set the block to static. Fixed in both the coach's own view and HelperView.

No schema changes. Both fixes verified by `npm run check` and direct code tracing.

### Thirty-eighth session (2026-08-07): two real bugs found (an unusable player-mention picker, an illegible pick-up-and-drop chip), a full live-view ordering audit, and default audio changes

An 8-item batch, most of it a genuine "these two views still don't match, be more thorough" correction from Jax. Two of the items turned out to be real bugs, not just polish, found while actually tracing the exact code paths involved rather than pattern-matching quickly.

**A real, confirmed bug: the @ mention picker in the live quick-note composer was invisible.** The shared `.mini-menu` CSS class always sets `top:calc(100% - 4px)` (it opens downward everywhere else it's used, correctly). The live note composer's own inline style tried to make it open upward instead by setting `bottom:"100%"`, but never cleared the class's own `top` value. With both `top` and `bottom` pinned on an absolutely-positioned element with no explicit height, the browser computed a collapsed, effectively zero-height box. The dropdown existed in the DOM the whole time, just never had any visible area. Fixed by explicitly clearing `top:"auto"` alongside the `bottom:"100%"` override. The end-of-practice note composer's own version already set `top:"100%"` correctly and was never affected.

**A real, confirmed bug: the Introduce Stations pick-up-and-drop chips were illegible.** The unpicked/available player chip used `background:"var(--s2)"` (a light gray, correct for the rest of the app's light theme) paired with `color:"#fff"` (white text) unconditionally, inside a screen whose background is the dark #0d1512 palette. White text on a light gray chip is close to invisible. Fixed to match the exact styling the groupings dialog's own pick-up-and-drop (the pattern this was modeled on) already uses correctly: `rgba(255,255,255,.1)` background, `rgba(255,255,255,.25)` border.

**A genuinely thorough helper/coach parity re-audit**, since the earlier claimed parity work still had real gaps Jax caught in testing. Read both views' station-focused screens and pre-tap grids side by side, field by field, rather than trusting the earlier pass:
- HelperView's "Introduce Stations" screen was still the plain light card treatment (`var(--s1)` background), never actually given the dark #0d1512 palette the coach's own version has had for several sessions now. Fixed, including reordering the header row (station number and coach name on one line, matching the coach's exact layout) and dropping the skill tags and coaching points it was showing that the coach's own version never shows there.
- HelperView's focused single-station view had a real content gap: it never rendered a station's `description` field at all, despite `get_live_session_view` already returning it. Added, in the same position the coach's own view shows it (right after the coach/area line, before coaching focus).
- Coach name and area were shown in a different order and different visual treatment (a separate `CoachPill` badge for helper vs. plain colored text for the coach) across three different views. Standardized on the coach's own plain-text treatment everywhere, since that's what "the same as the coach view" actually means now, after an earlier session's layout reorder already moved the coach away from ever using a pill-badge look. The now-unused `CoachPill` component was removed rather than left as dead code.
- Skill tags removed from live view entirely, both coach and helper, everywhere they were shown (the plain-drill body, the pre-tap grid, the focused station view, Introduce Stations). The untagged-drill reminder button (a different feature, prompts adding a tag rather than displaying existing ones) is untouched and still coach-only.
- HelperView's Player Focus button moved from directly under the timer to below the Coaching Focus section, matching the coach's own field order exactly.

**Up Next redesigned for real visual separation**, in all four places it renders (coach and helper, both the normal case and the mid-rotation spoiler-safe placeholder). It used to be just another card in the same uniform flex-gap stack as the current drill's own content, easy to read as a continuation rather than a preview of something else. Added a proper section divider above it (a small green eyebrow label plus a thin line, the same "label plus line" pattern used for section breaks elsewhere in the app) and gave the card itself a real border instead of just a flat background fill, so it reads as its own distinct block.

**Default audio changed**: voice now defaults to the male option (Daniel, the same one Settings already offers) instead of whatever the browser happens to pick, whenever no explicit per-coach preference is saved. HelperView (which has no Settings screen or saved preference of its own) gets the same default. The default cue sound was already whistle, confirmed rather than assumed by reading `getAudioCuePref`'s own fallback.

**House style, permanent addition**: no em dashes anywhere in this project (code comments, commit messages, this file, in-app copy). Added to the Standing Instructions section above so this doesn't need repeating every session. The Turn On Audio prompt's volume reminder was also reworded to drop its own em-dash usage, now reading "Reminder: Turn up your device's volume."

No schema changes this session. All fixes verified by `npm run check` and direct code tracing; no live-browser verification available here, same standing limitation.

### Thirty-seventh session continued (2026-08-06) — a real live-grouping bug (manual assignments discarded on entry), attendance default reverted, checklist visibility confirmed

Jax asked a hypothetical: would a coach's manually-planned Builder groups survive if they reached Practice Setup before marking anyone present? Tracing the actual code to answer it surfaced a real, more serious bug beyond the hypothetical -- fixed the same session.

**The hypothetical itself: safe.** Practice Setup's own group-seeding effect filters the coach's manual `groupAssignments` by *planned* absences only, never by the live attendance checkboxes -- the underlying `session_groups` row is correct regardless of whether anyone's been tapped present yet in the UI. The one real wrinkle: the View Groupings preview dialog only *displays* a player once they're checked present, so an empty-looking preview before anyone's marked in is expected, not a sign anything broke.

**The bug the investigation actually found**: once a plain (non-station) drill's groups go live, a *separate* effect governs what's shown -- and it never checked for an existing saved seed at all. Every time that drill became current, or attendance changed at all while it was current, it silently discarded whatever was there (the coach's manual Builder assignment, or edits made live in Practice Setup's own groupings dialog) and generated a fresh random shuffle. This directly contradicted the effect's own code comment ("this effect only fetches them"), which was true for station blocks but never actually implemented for plain drills. Fixed with a new shared helper (`reconcileGroups`, `constants.js`) mirroring the minimal-disruption spirit of `rebalanceKeep`/`rebalanceEven`: fetch first, and when something exists, keep every pairing intact, drop only whoever's now actually absent, and place any newly-present unassigned player into whichever group is currently smallest -- a full random reshuffle only ever happens now if nothing was saved at all, or if a coach explicitly taps the existing "Reshuffle" button (untouched, still does exactly what it says).

**Attendance default reverted, on reflection**: an earlier session this week defaulted Practice Setup's attendance to nobody present (reasoning: a coach shouldn't have to tap 15 kids out one by one). Jax revisited this given the app already has a dedicated "who's out?" planned-absence feature specifically for flagging known no-shows in advance -- defaulting to all-absent duplicated that feature's job and added friction for the common case (most of the team actually shows up). Reverted to defaulting present, *except* whoever's actually marked out via that existing feature -- Timmy stays unchecked even though the default is "present," and the coach only has to handle real day-of surprises. Mark All Present was updated to respect the same planned-absence exclusion (so it doesn't undo the one thing this default is supposed to protect); Clear All is untouched. Coaches (as opposed to players) keep last session's separate default (just the logged-in coach) -- not something this specific feedback addressed, flagged as a judgment call.

**Confirmed, no gap found**: assistant coaches already see full practice-component checklist details (Intro/Closer line items) everywhere that matters -- the live view (no role gate on checklist rendering), Practice Setup (added last session, gated only on tap-to-expand, not role), and the static Practice Detail plan-review screen (view access already open to every rostered coach on the team; only *editing* is head-coach-gated, per the assistant-coach handoff work several sessions back). Nothing needed fixing here.

No schema changes this round -- both fixes are pure client-side logic.

### Thirty-seventh session (2026-08-06) — a 14-item live-view batch: real latency/timer-jump bugs root-caused and fixed, a broken tag-picker click, and a full helper/coach parity pass

Jax's largest single-message batch yet, all from testing the live view directly. Several turned out to be real, reproducible bugs rather than polish -- prioritized those first.

**Real bugs, root-caused and fixed:**
- **Pause-then-resume timer jump**: resuming showed the clock jump to a different value before settling back. Root cause -- `total_paused_seconds` only ever updated once `togglePlay`'s write round-tripped to Supabase and back, so whatever real time elapsed *during that round trip* got silently charged to the running timer the instant the response landed, instead of to the pause. Fixed by applying the exact same patch to local state synchronously, before the network call -- the display transitions the instant the button is tapped now; the write still persists it in the background, and if it ever fails, `writeSession`'s existing re-fetch fallback self-heals. Same treatment applied to `nudge` (+/-1min) and `transitionTo` (Next/Back/advance), which had the identical shape of bug.
- **General live-view button latency**: `advance`/`goBack`/`jumpTo`/`togglePlay` all `await`ed `submitOperation` (a pure fire-and-forget audit-log insert with no return value anyone uses) *before* doing anything else -- a full wasted network round trip in front of every single tap. Changed to fire-and-forget across all of them.
- **"First tap only wakes the screen, second tap actually works"**: this is standard OS/browser behavior (a phone's first touch after screen-off is consumed to unlock the display, never delivered to the page as a real click) -- nothing in JS can change that. The actionable fix is not hitting it in the first place: Wake Lock used to only ever get requested when audio cues were on; broadened to hold for the entire live/setup session regardless, since a coach glancing at the timer with audio off needs the screen to stay awake just as much.
- **Untagged-skill tag picker did nothing when tapped**: the reminder button (added last session) opened a modal that was accidentally nested inside the `isBlock`-only "focus station" wrapper -- but the reminder itself only ever renders for a plain (non-station) drill, a condition that can never coexist with `isBlock`. The modal could never actually appear no matter what was tapped. Moved to the correct unconditional level. Reworded the reminder text ("No skill tag yet -- tap to add one") to read less like generated copy.
- **HelperView's "two minutes remaining" fired at the start of a 2-minute phase**: a fix for this exact bug already shipped for the coach's own view a few sessions ago (skip the cue for any phase ≤120s), but HelperView had its own separate copy of the timing logic that never got the same guard. Applied it there too.

**Helper/coach/assistant parity, several real gaps closed** (assistant coaches already share the coach's own `CommandScreen` component, confirmed by re-reading the isController/amHeadCoach gates -- only "Edit Practice" is head-coach-gated, everything else already renders identically, so most of this round's parity work was specifically about HelperView vs. everyone else):
- HelperView's transition ("Rotate Now") screen gained the coach's own dark #0d1512 styling and from/to labels (station + area + coach), instead of a plain light card with no visual cue a rotation was happening.
- HelperView's Overview never showed completed activities at all (not just a rendering gap -- `get_live_session_view` only ever returned `upcoming_activities`, position > current). Added a new `all_activities` field (new migration) alongside the untouched existing one, so a helper's Overview can cross out anything already run the same way the coach's own local-plan computation already does.
- Station pre-focus info unified across coach/assistant/helper: before tapping in, every viewer now sees station #, drill, area, coach, equipment, and player names only -- coaching points and per-player focus notes (previously shown eagerly in both the coach's own pre-tap grid and HelperView's tap-to-reveal chips) now only appear once you've tapped into focus, matching what was actually asked for. Player focus, once tapped in, now shows the same way for every viewer: inline as a second line inside the player's own chip (matching `StationPlayerChip`'s existing pattern), not hidden behind a separate toggle/reveal gesture HelperView used to have.
- The duplicate "tap to focus" hint (shown both under the clock and again at the bottom of every station card) is down to one, reworded "Tap a station to focus" and sized up to actually be noticed.
- Coach/helper names in HelperView are a green pill now (`CoachPill`, matching the header attendance pill's own treatment) instead of plain gray text.
- Notes/Overview toggle buttons read "Notes"/"Overview" always now, with a caret flipping direction on open/close, instead of switching to the word "Close".

**Live view layout reordered** (all user types): title stays put; a new line right below it shows coach + area for a plain drill/checklist (a station block still shows each station's own coach/area on its own card, so there's no single one to hoist here); then the timer; then the control buttons (already sat right after); then description/coaching focus/player focus/equipment/groupings; then Up Next. The old buried Location/Coach blocks deep in the body (both in the coach's view and HelperView) were removed now that the same info reads right under the title.

**Live attendance reworked**: tapping anywhere on a player's row used to toggle their attendance -- a coach trying to glance at someone's card risked accidentally marking them absent. Restructured so only the check circle toggles attendance; every row gained an ellipsis opening a new "View Player Card" (focus areas + every practice note that tagged them, author and date already captured on every note via existing `created_by`/`created_at` columns -- just needed surfacing, not a schema change). Head coaches can delete a note from this new card; the same delete capability was retrofitted onto the existing Roster `PlayerProfile` screen's own notes list too, since that's another screen where player notes already show.

**Smaller items**: shared live links now carry practice-specific detail in the share title ("Help with the [Date] [Team] Run of Practice") instead of identical generic text every time; Practice Setup's expand/collapse chevrons were rendering browser-default blue (no explicit `color` set, inherited unpredictably) -- now explicitly the app's green everywhere they appear, including in the pre-live Preview screen; expanding a non-station practice component (mostly checklists) in Practice Setup now shows its line items (or freeform notes for a component with none), instead of showing nothing at all.

One new migration this session (`20260807080000_live_session_all_activities.sql`, additive only, no data changes). As always, no live-browser verification available here -- every fix verified by build check and direct code reading, several confirmed against production behavior by reasoning through the exact data/timing involved rather than guessing.

### Thirty-sixth session (2026-08-06) — collapse-until-expanded consistency, coach pills in HelperView, Equipment Needed shows who/where, and planned clock times per section

A 6-item direct-feedback batch, same day. All verified via `npm run check`; no DB changes this round.

**Station drills in Practice Setup were showing coaching points without expanding**: `SetupActivityRow` (plain drills) already gated description/coaching points behind its own expand toggle, but `SetupStationBlockRow` never had one at all — every station's coaching points showed unconditionally. Added a per-station tap-to-expand (same chevron pattern), and while in there, added `st.description` rendering too — it turned out `get_preview_view` had always returned a station's description, but neither Practice Setup nor the anonymous pre-live Preview screen ever rendered it, a dormant gap noticed while fixing the actual ask.

**HelperView: coach/helper names became green pills.** Plain gray text before, easy to miss — added a shared `CoachPill` (light-theme green pill, matching the header attendance-pill's existing green treatment) used everywhere a station or drill's coach name renders in the live helper view.

**Equipment Needed redesigned across every screen that has one** (Practice Setup, the anonymous pre-live Preview link, and the PDF export): previously just a deduped list of equipment names with no sense of which drill needed what or who was bringing it. Added a shared `buildEquipmentNeeded(items)` helper (`constants.js`) that pairs every equipment item with every distinct (coach, location) combination that actually needs it, deduped so the same drill/station pair isn't repeated — each of the three screens now shows the equipment pill plus a "Coach @ Location" line underneath, using its own existing coach/location resolution (`leaderLabel`/sublocation lookups in Practice Setup and the PDF, `coach_name`/`sublocation_name` fields already returned by `get_preview_view`).

**Pre-live Preview screen brought closer to parity with Practice Setup**: reminder copy now reads "Use this time to get everything set up before practice starts." (previously "Use this time to set up stations," different wording for what's meant to be the same message coaches see). Run of Practice rows in this screen now default collapsed too, same tap-to-expand-for-description/coaching-points pattern just added to Practice Setup's own station rows — previously this screen showed every drill and station's full text unconditionally, no way to just scan titles.

**Planned start/end clock times added next to duration**, in both Practice Setup and the pre-live Preview screen, mirroring the exact clock-anchoring logic the PDF export (`PracticePlanPrint.jsx`'s `timeRangeFor`) already used — a running clock seeded from the practice's real scheduled start time, advanced by each section's own duration in order. Only shows when the practice actually has a scheduled start time to anchor to, same caveat the PDF already has; unscheduled practices just show duration as before.

### Thirty-fifth session (2026-08-06) — a real, reproduced helper-link sync bug found and fixed, HelperView brought to parity with the coach's live view, Edit Practice now opens a real modern builder, and Practice Setup attendance defaults flipped

Five items from testing the previous session's work live, same day. No account-creation tooling in this environment, so verification was `npm run check` plus, for the sync bug specifically, direct read-only production queries that confirmed the exact failure Jax described had actually happened moments earlier.

**A real bug, confirmed against production, not just theorized**: a helper's shared "Practice Setup" link never followed along once the coach actually started the practice — stuck on the setup screen indefinitely, "eventually" catching up (Jax's words) rather than transitioning at the same time. Root-caused by reading `link_preview_to_live_session`'s own logic: it only ever links a not-yet-live `preview_sessions` row to a live session at the exact moment that session is first created (`startOrJoinLiveSession`'s `created` branch) — but a coach can only reach the "Share Setup Link" button from *inside* Practice Setup, which by definition means a live session (in setup-not-yet-confirmed form) already exists by then. Every real "share from here" click therefore mints or reuses a `preview_sessions` row whose `live_session_id` never gets set, so `get_preview_view`'s `is_live`/`live_token` computation can never resolve for it — that helper's link is permanently stuck, not just delayed. Confirmed directly against production: the most recent `preview_sessions` row (created today) had `live_session_id: null` against a practice with two already-abandoned live sessions, exactly the reported shape. Fixed by calling `linkPreviewToLiveSession` again, unconditionally, every time Share Setup Link is tapped (the underlying RPC only ever touches an already-unlinked row, so this is a safe no-op once truly linked) — `PracticeSetupScreen` needed a new `session` prop threaded through to make the live session's id available at share-time. Also tightened `PreviewView`'s poll interval from 5s to 2s so the remaining delay (network + poll cadence, no realtime channel exists for an anonymous pre-live viewer since the live session's real id is deliberately never exposed pre-link) reads as closer to "at the same time." The audio-prompt-sync half of the same ask needed no separate fix — `HelperView` already defaults `showAudioPrompt` to true on mount, so once the link-follow bug above stopped stranding helpers on the wrong screen, they get prompted the moment they actually land on the live view, same as everyone else.

**HelperView rebuilt toward coach-view parity, read-only**: audited what the coach's own live view shows that HelperView didn't. Added a real "Player Focus" button (gated on the drill actually having a skill tag, same as the coach's own `categoryIdsForLibraryId` gate) opening a read-only modal listing every present player's note for that tag, sourced from the same `player_focus` map `get_live_session_view` already returns. Added a persistent "Up Next" queue row in the body (the coach's view has always had this in addition to its separate "Overview" toggle panel — HelperView only ever had the toggle), including the same spoiler-avoidance the coach's queue already has (a mid-block rotation shows a generic "coming up" placeholder instead of revealing the real next rotation early); tapping an item opens the existing `UpcomingPreview` modal, already shared between both views. Title/timer/description/coaching focus/coach/location/equipment/grouping were all already present in HelperView from earlier sessions' work — this closed the two real gaps (Player Focus, persistent Up Next) rather than a full rewrite.

**Assistant-coach parity, audited rather than rebuilt**: confirmed via a full `isController`/`amHeadCoach` grep across `CommandScreen.jsx` that an assistant coach already gets the exact same view as the head coach today — every gate found was either `isController` (rotates to whoever currently holds control, not fixed to any one role) or the two `amHeadCoach` checks, both specifically on "Edit Practice" (an intentional permission boundary already documented elsewhere in this file, not an oversight). No code change was needed here; this is what "identical view, permission-gated actions" already looks like.

**"Edit Practice" from Practice Setup's ellipsis was a real, separate, bare-bones builder** (`LiveEditBuilder`, its own component living in `CommandScreen.jsx`) that had never been kept in sync with the real Builder screen's own recent work — no drag reordering (manual up/down arrow buttons instead), no library source picker, no equipment-mismatch check, old row header/subtext. Rebuilding it as a real navigation to the actual `/builder/:practiceId` route was considered and rejected: that would unmount `CommandScreen` (and the live-session refs/closures its `onSaveResume` callback relies on to safely reset the session's current-activity pointer after a mid-live edit) before those closures could run. Instead, rebuilt `LiveEditBuilder` in place using the exact same reusable pieces the real Builder screen already uses — `useActivityDnd`/`ActivityDndContext`/`SortableActivityRow` for drag reordering and last-added sticky-pin, the same library-source-picker logic (`librarySources`/`libSource`/`sourceFilteredLib`, `coachDisplayName`), the same equipment-mismatch dialog flow (`findMissingEquipment`/`resolveDrillEquipmentForCoach`/`EquipmentMismatchDialog`), and the same row header/subtext redesign (coach name in the title, grouping/area/equipment in the subtext) — so editing mid-live now looks and behaves like editing anywhere else in the app, without the real risk of navigating away from a live session's own component instance.

**Practice Setup attendance defaults flipped, plus Mark All/Clear All**: attendance used to default to everyone present, which meant tapping *out* every kid who hadn't arrived yet rather than tapping *in* the ones who had. Now defaults to nobody present except the logged-in coach's own coach record (a real, known exception) — players and other coaches get tapped in as they actually show up. Added "Mark All Present"/"Clear All" buttons above both the Players and Coaches grids for the case where everyone genuinely is here and one-by-one tapping is pure friction.

### Thirty-fourth session (2026-08-06) — 13-item direct-feedback batch from testing the groupings-dialog redesign live: manual pre-assignment, Introduce Stations tap-to-reassign, live header/audio/skill-tag polish

Continuing straight off the prior session's pick-up-and-drop groupings redesign, Jax sent another 13-item batch from testing it live. No account-creation tooling in this environment either, so every change was verified by `npm run check` plus reading the actual code paths involved; two of the items needed real schema changes, applied via `supabase db push` after review (no mutating dry-run needed — both are pure additive columns/fields, nothing to roll back against).

**Groupings dialog got its Reshuffle/Group By controls back**: the pick-up-and-drop redesign (previous session) dropped the old Generate-Random/Group-By-Position-or-Hand shortcuts it replaced, and Jax asked for them back alongside the new manual gesture, not instead of it. Added `regenerateSetupGroups` (`CommandScreen.jsx`) — reshuffle does a fresh random split via the existing `assignGroups`; Group By reuses `groupByAttribute` (already imported for Builder's own station config) against *today's* actual present players, not the full roster — and a small "Reshuffle"/"Group By ▾" control pair inside `GroupingsDialog` itself, gated the same way the pick-up-and-drop gesture already is (`isController`, real `session_groups` writes via `saveSessionGroups`).

**Manual pre-assignment for Partners/Groups in Builder** (a real ask, not just a UI tweak): previously a drill's Partners/Groups split was *always* silently randomized fresh once attendance was taken, with no way to plan who'd be paired with whom ahead of time. Added a `group_assignments` jsonb column to both `practice_activities` and `template_activities` (`20260806080000_activity_group_assignments.sql`) — an array of player-id arrays against the full roster (attendance isn't known yet at plan time), mirroring how a station block's own `assignments` column already works. `ActConfig`'s Player Grouping section (`ActivityConfigs.jsx`) grew a new `ManualGroupAssign` sub-component: Generate Random / Clear / Group By Position-or-Hand shortcuts plus a per-group tap-to-toggle roster (green=here, yellow=in another group, gray=unassigned) — deliberately the same visual language Station's own player-assignment pills already use, not a new pattern. Left untouched (empty `group_assignments`), behavior is unchanged. Practice Setup's own group-seeding effect (`CommandScreen.jsx`) now checks for a non-empty manual assignment first, filtered down to whoever's actually present, before falling back to a fresh random split.

**Builder polish, four smaller items**: (1) the station drill-picker popup's bottom option was hidden behind the tab bar — added `calc(20px + var(--tab) + env(safe-area-inset-bottom,0px))` bottom padding, matching how the rest of the app already accounts for the tab bar. (2) The library source picker showed a vague "A coach's Library" instead of naming anyone — `librarySources` (`App.jsx`) now resolves real names via a new `coachDisplayName`/`coachNameByUserId` lookup (mirroring `NewLibraryScreen.jsx`'s own cross-team name resolution) and adds a genuine "Public Library" source option (which `StationConfig`'s own `filteredLibrary` in `ActivityConfigs.jsx` was separately, redundantly re-excluding via a leftover `!a.sourceCatalogId` filter — removed, or the new source would have always shown empty results). (3) A real regression-of-a-regression: an earlier session's fix for "the last-added drill should stay pinned at the top" had grown a `scrollToRow`/`scrollIntoView` call, which Jax reported *also* felt wrong ("tapping a drill slides the screen up... I shouldn't change locations in my library") — removed `scrollToRow` and its three call sites entirely, relying solely on the pre-existing `position:sticky` mechanism (`SortableActivityRow`'s `sticky` prop), which pins the new row without moving the coach's own scroll position in the library below it. (4) Practice Setup's Run of Practice rows now show a "Coach" label ahead of the leader pill/name, so an unassigned drill reads as "Coach: Unassigned" rather than just a bare, ambiguous pill.

**Builder's Run of Practice row header/subtext redesigned**: per feedback that duration was already visible in the green badge and didn't need repeating, a plain (non-station) row's subtext now shows grouping type (Whole Team/Partners/N Groups) + area + equipment instead — exactly what a coach needs to spot something forgotten at a glance — and the row's title line grew an inline `· {coach name}` so who's leading is visible without expanding. Station blocks were left as-is (their own drill-list-plus-duration-math subtext already serves a different purpose and wasn't reported broken).

**Turn On Audio prompt**: Jax asked whether device volume level is readable from the web, to only show a volume reminder below some threshold. It isn't — no Web Audio/media API exposes a page to the OS or hardware output volume (only in-page gain, which is a different thing entirely) — so there's no way to conditionally suppress the reminder. Rather than leave it folded into the existing paragraph, split it into its own bolded, amber-callout line so it can't be skimmed past, with a comment explaining why it's unconditional.

**Coach name added to the live "Up Next" station preview**: tapping a station block in the queue showed drill name and area but not who was leading it. The controller's own local-plan-based preview builder (`CommandScreen.jsx`) now resolves it via the existing `leaderLabel` helper; the anonymous/shared-link path needed a real schema fix, since `get_live_session_view`'s `upcoming_activities[].stations[]` never selected `coach_name` at all (unlike the *current*-activity version of the same function, which already did) — added via `20260806090000_live_session_upcoming_station_coach.sql`, same `team_staff` join and helper-name fallback as the existing current-activity query.

**"Introduce Stations" (renamed from "Introducing Stations") gained the same tap-to-reassign as the groupings dialog, and player movement moved out of the live per-station view entirely**: these two items turned out to be the same code path from opposite ends, so they were done together. Previously, tapping a player *during* an actual live station opened a list-based "move to which station?" picker (`onClick`), and a 500ms long-press opened their player card (focus areas/notes) — the exact awkward-list-picker UX already replaced in the groupings dialog, still living here untouched, plus a long-press gesture Jax explicitly asked to retire now that movement has a better home. Simplified `PlayerChipLive` to a single tap → player card (no more long-press timers, no more `onMove`); removed the old list-based move modal and its "Move to Another Station" button from the live per-station view entirely. That freed up `movePlayer`/`setMovePlayer`/`movePlayerToStation` (previously that modal's own backend) to be reused, unchanged, inside the "Introduce Stations" block-intro screen instead — tapping a player there now highlights them in place and every other station grows a dashed "+ Drop here" landing zone, identical gesture and visual language to the groupings dialog's own pick-up-and-drop, reusing `movePlayerToStation`'s existing rotation-offset math (still 0 at this point in a block, before any rotation has happened).

**Live header restructured to fix a real layout bug, not just crowding**: the pulsing "Live" dot (`.live`, a 7×7px circle) had gone oval — root cause was a missing `flex-shrink:0`, so a cramped flex row (dot + label + schedule badge sharing space against the entire button cluster via `justify-content:space-between`) could shrink the dot's width below its fixed height under real screen widths. Fixed at the source (`.live` in `App.jsx`) rather than just in one call site. Separately restructured both the controller's and the helper's own header (`CommandScreen.jsx`) so the dot/Live label/schedule badge/presence-badge now sit on their own row below the button cluster, instead of competing with it — giving the ellipsis (and every other header button) the full row width Jax asked for, and directly answering the "move the dot to its own line, followed by who's watching" ask in the same change.

**Untagged-skill reminder in live view**: a drill with no skill tag silently lost its "Player Focus" callout with no indication why or how to fix it mid-practice. Added a reminder note (only for a drill the coach actually owns in their own library — checked via `ownerUserId`/`sourceCatalogId`, since editing someone else's org/peer/public-catalog drill from live view isn't safe) that opens a tag picker on tap; saving calls the existing `updateDrill` (passing through the drill's own unchanged fields alongside the new `skillTagIds`, since `updateDrill` always writes every field it's given) and `refreshLibrary()`, so the change is real and persists for next time, not just a session-local override.



Jax reported testing the app live and hitting a real bug: an August 5th practice, actually run a day early (August 4th) while testing, silently read as "completed" against its own real scheduled slot instead of staying scheduled — plus 12 more items spanning Library, Home, Practice Setup, live-session UX, and Goals & Insights. This session did **not** have access to the account-creation/auth-session tooling this project's own Working Conventions call for (minting a disposable test account is out of bounds for this environment specifically, not a project-convention gap) — every change below was verified by build check (`npm run check`) plus direct **read-only** SQL against the linked production database (`supabase db query --linked --file`, never a mutating query outside the one real migration below) rather than live browser click-through. Flagging this plainly rather than claiming a live-verified "done": these changes should get a real in-app pass before being trusted the way this file's other entries are.

**The early-start bug, root-caused and fixed**: nothing in the app gated *when* a practice could be started — Home's hero, Practice Detail's "Run Now," and Builder's Run Now (1-hour gate only) all just started whichever practice `practice.id` pointed at, however far off its own scheduled time was. Fixed with a shared 2-hour rule (`isMoreThanTwoHoursAway`/`practiceScheduledMs`, `constants.js`, built on `supabase.js`'s existing `teamLocalToScheduledAt` rather than a second date-math implementation) and a new shared 3-choice `FuturePracticeGuardModal` (its own leaf component, `src/components/FuturePracticeGuardModal.jsx` — kept out of both Home/PracticeDetail to avoid a circular import between the two, which already import each other). Home's hero and Practice Detail's own Run Now both now run directly when the practice is live/soon/already-past (missed), and otherwise open the guard: **Cancel**, **Run This Plan as a New Practice** (the existing `runAgainFrom`/`runNowFrom` copy-into-a-fresh-unscheduled-practice pattern — `stripIdsForCopy` + `savePracticeTree(null,...)` — so the original scheduled slot is never touched), or **Run This Practice Now** (the direct, pre-existing path, for a coach genuinely on-site early). Practice Detail's button intentionally was **not** deleted outright despite the literal ask ("remove run now option from the review plan screen") — a missed (already-past, never-run) practice has no other UI path to re-run today (Home's hero only ever surfaces `p.date>=todayStr`, and History only lists practices that actually got a session), so removing it outright would have silently broken re-running a missed plan; gating it the same way Home's hero now is preserves that path while closing the actual reported loophole. Builder's own Run Now keeps its prior, separate 1-hour disable+tooltip (not migrated to this same guard/popup this session — a deliberate, previously-shipped decision, not something reported broken).

**Home's hero button/copy simplified**: "Practice Setup" as a distinct label is gone (direct feedback: it showed for a practice over a week away, where jumping into pre-live setup makes no sense) — always "Review Plan" + either "Join Practice" (a session already exists) or "Start Practice →" (direct if soon/live, the guard above otherwise). No change was needed for "upcoming practices shouldn't show a practice already completed from its own scheduled slot" — `agendaWindow`'s existing `!ran(p)` filter already did this correctly; the reported symptom was entirely the early-start bug above (the Aug-5 slot really did get completed, a day early), not a separate filtering gap.

**A real, separate "glitchy Home" bug found and fixed, not previously reported as this specific mechanism**: the Home hero and Upcoming Practices list could visibly show a practice for a moment and then have it disappear (or the hero could jump to a different practice) a beat after mount. Root cause: `runStatus` starts `{}`, so `ran(p)` reads false for *everything* until `fetchPracticeRunStatus`'s own async round trip resolves — the very first render treated an already-completed practice as still upcoming, then recomputed and dropped it once the real statuses landed. Fixed with a `runStatusLoaded` gate (same shape as this file's existing `hasCompleted`/`checklistDone` fix) — the hero/Upcoming Practices section shows a plain "Loading..." card instead of a guessed-then-corrected one until the first fetch resolves, and is never re-hidden on a later background refresh. A second instance of the same class of bug: Development Pulse's focus-team resolution has a documented "guess `homeTeams[0]`, correct once `recentSessionByTeamId` arrives" fallback for a coach with no next practice and more than one team — which meant the card could render one team's data for a moment and then visibly swap to a different team's. Fixed by withholding the card (`focusTeamDataReady`) until that fetch resolves, but only when it's actually going to run (a coach with a next practice or only one team gets the right team on the first render already, no wait needed).

**Save as Template losing a station block's contents, root-caused (not template-specific)**: read-only SQL against production confirmed a real practice with a `station_blocks` row saved correctly (duration/rotate intact) but *zero* `stations` rows underneath it — the template built from it faithfully copied that already-broken practice, it didn't lose data during templating. The actual defect: `saveActivityTree` (`supabase.js`, shared by `savePracticeTree`/`saveTemplateTree`) logged-and-continued past any insert/update failure without ever surfacing it to the caller — a station-row insert that silently failed (RLS/FK/whatever the original trigger was) still left the surrounding UI reporting "Saved"/"Saved as Template" with data quietly missing, the same root-cause shape this file's very first Gotcha already documents for drill/equipment tag sync. Fixed by collecting every failure and returning `{errors}`, which `savePracticeTree`/`saveTemplateTree` now surface as `{error}` to their existing callers (`handleSaveAsTpl` in both `GoalsScreen.jsx`/`CommandScreen.jsx` already had `if(error){setTplError(...)}` in place, previously unreachable dead code). Historical data for the one broken practice found isn't recoverable (its source stations were already gone, and both affected rows were disposable test/QA data, not the real coach's) — this fix prevents recurrence and, for the first time, tells a coach when a save partially failed instead of a silent false "Saved."

**Practice Setup rebuilt as a black, information-rich "Run of Practice" rundown** (`PracticeSetupScreen`, new, `CommandScreen.jsx`): the thirty-second session's rework made this a real shared collaborative pre-live stage but dropped the informational richness the old public `/preview/:token` view (`PreviewView`) already had. This session merges the two: dark palette (`#0d1512`/`#52b788`, matching `PreviewView`'s own), a live countdown to the real scheduled start (`teamLocalToScheduledAt`, exported from `supabase.js` for this), and every activity — not just stations — listed under a **"Run of Practice"** header (renamed from "Run Order" here and in `PreviewView` itself) with location/equipment/coaching-points per activity and per-station, equipment flagged "not acquired" the same way `PreviewView` already does. `StationSetupSection` (station leader assignment, live player-move) gained the same equipment/location display rather than being replaced — its collaborative editing is unchanged, just richer. The old plain-attendance `AttendanceScreen` is now used **only** for the mid-practice "Attendance" quick-update modal (`isUpdate`); the fresh pre-live case always renders the new dark screen.

**Live-session header layout fixed** (`CommandScreen.jsx`, both the coach view and `HelperView`): the drill title used to be squeezed into a narrow left column alongside a separate button column (`.cc-header`'s own `justify-content:space-between` on two side-by-side children), which is why it wrapped awkwardly instead of spanning the screen. `.cc-header` is now a column: a top row (LIVE/on-time badge on the left, attendance pill + Overview + audio + ellipsis all on the right, all on one line) followed by the presence badge and then the title, now full-width and bigger (30px→34px). The attendance-pill icon changed from a raised hand (`Ic.Hand`) to a plain person silhouette (`Ic.Person`) per direct feedback that a hand-raise read oddly for a bare headcount. The audio toggle's emoji and "On"/"Off" text are now explicit sibling spans in a flex row with a controlled font size, instead of one string — the emoji glyph's own larger rendered size was visually overlapping the word next to it.

**Insights' false "untagged drills" complaint, root-caused and fixed**: non-station Practice Components (Intro/Closer/Checklist/Water Break/Stretch/Other — always saved as `practice_activities.type='checklist'`, never linked to a real library drill) were counted in every category-attribution denominator, both inflating "untagged" and understating every real category's percentage. `practice_activities.type='break'` was already excluded everywhere for exactly this reason but never actually reachable from the Builder UI (every component type writes `'checklist'`, not `'break'`) — extended the same exclusion to `'checklist'` too, both in the shared attribution helpers (`category_minutes_from_rows`/`total_attributable_minutes`, `20260805080000_exclude_admin_components_from_attribution.sql` — Trends/Scorecard/Drill Insights all compose on these and needed no changes of their own) and in `get_team_goal_report`'s own inline CTEs (copied verbatim from its current live definition per this file's own migration-drift-checking convention, then edited). Verified the fix directly against the linked database (`select total_attributable_minutes(...)` with a synthetic `checklist`/`break`/`activity` row triplet correctly returning only the `activity` row's minutes) before and after `db push`. Client-side mirror `categoryMinutesForPracticeActivities` (`constants.js`, used by Builder's own goal-guidance projection) updated to match. Development Pulse's "data quality" state (`resolveDevelopmentPulseState`) additionally gained the same `allDrillsTagged` carve-out `GlanceView` already had — even after the denominator fix, some genuine untagged time can remain from activities typed straight into a practice rather than added from the library, and the card now falls through to a real category recommendation instead of a false "go tag your drills" complaint once there's nothing left in the library to tag.

**Builder's station "Choose from Library" picker now shows and defaults to "My Library"**: it silently merged every accessible drill (own + org + any peer sharing with the coach) with no indication of source — the exact gap the main "My Drill Library" section's own `librarySources`/`libSource` switcher (twenty-seventh session) already closed for the bottom-of-screen add-drill flow, just never threaded into `StationConfig`. `BuilderScreen` now passes its already-scoped `sourceFilteredLib` (not the raw `data.activityLibrary`) into `StationConfig`, plus the switcher's own `librarySources`/`libSource`/`setLibSource`, so the station picker shares one library-source selection with the rest of Builder instead of a second, independent (or missing) one. `ActConfig` (a plain activity's own inline picker, if it ever gains one) has the identical gap and was deliberately left alone — out of the scope Jax actually asked for ("when building a station").

**Library's blue sport-header text, root-caused**: three of the four collapsible per-sport section headers (Equipment, Player Gear, Skill Tags tabs) already set an explicit `color:"var(--black)"`; the fourth (My Drills/Team Libraries) had no `color` set at all on that span, falling back to whatever default the browser/OS supplied (a blue tint on at least one real device). Fixed by setting all four explicitly to `var(--green)` — matching the app's actual "consistent with the rest of the app" convention (most section labels/actives already use the green, not black) rather than just patching the one outlier to match its three siblings' black.

Known, deliberate scope decisions from this session, not oversights: Builder's Run Now keeps its separate 1-hour gate (see above); `ActConfig`'s library picker wasn't touched (see above); the assistant-coach/head-coach "Join" semantics from the spec were confirmed already correct in the existing code (`isSessionLive` is role-agnostic — any signed-in coach sees "Join Practice" the instant a session exists, regardless of who started it or of `canManage`) and needed no change.

### Thirty-third session continued (2026-08-05) — 13 more direct-feedback items from testing the above live, headlined by two real RLS gaps found and fixed

Jax reported the previous batch's own Save-as-Template fix now surfacing a real, reproducible red error, plus a delegated assistant coach unable to schedule or save-as-template at all with no error shown either way. Both were root-caused via direct, rolled-back SQL against the linked production database (never a live account, still off-limits this session) rather than guessed at.

**Two real RLS gaps found and fixed, both about equipment/location a coach didn't personally create**: (1) `practices_insert_manage`/`practices_update_manage` required `can_access_location` for a scheduled practice's location — a delegated planner (`team_staff.can_build_practices`) could pass the team check but still get silently rejected scheduling at the team's own regular location, since that's routinely owned personally by the head coach, not the delegate. Confirmed live (`can_access_location` false, `can_manage_team` false, `can_build_practice_for_team` true, for a real delegated assistant and a real team location) and fixed with a new `can_use_location_for_team` function, extending both policies. (2) `can_link_asset_to_template_activity`/`_station` (and the practice/station equivalents) never accounted for equipment owned by a *teammate* rather than the caller or an org — reproduced live end-to-end (an assistant saving a real practice as a template hit an actual `new row violates row-level security policy` error trying to link the head coach's own gear) and fixed by extending all four functions with a "owned by any non-archived team_staff member of this team" fallback, mirroring the exact reasoning already used for `ast.team_id = tm.id` team-owned assets. A third, separate bug in the same area: `saveActivityTree`'s two join-table helpers (`replaceEquipment`/`replaceChecklistItems`) never checked `.error` on their own inserts at all — even after the RLS fixes, a genuinely failed equipment link would still vanish silently; both now return `{error}` and `saveActivityTree` surfaces it. A fourth, unrelated bug found via the same investigation: Builder's own `lastLocForTeam` fallback (no prior practice for this team yet) defaulted to `data.locations[0]` — the first location in the coach's entire cross-team list by pure array order, not anything to do with the team being built for — silently breaking Run Now/Save for any team whose own location wasn't coincidentally first; now prefers a location the coach actually owns.

**Practice Setup screen taken further, informed by feedback on the redesign from the earlier session today**: attendance is now collapsible (header reads "x/y players present," defaults open) with shorter/more-compact tiles so more of a roster fits on screen; the base `.att-circle` unchecked state's background referenced `--b2`, a CSS variable never actually defined in `:root` — a real, separate bug, not a removed element — so it rendered fully transparent/invisible rather than a visible blank circle, now fixed to a real color with dark-mode and compact variants.
