Confirmed directly against the database with each account's own authenticated session (not service-role, since the point was testing what a real signed-in user can and cannot do): default sharing visible both directions immediately on accept; assistant-to-assistant visibility correctly absent (peers are only ever visible relative to the shared head coach, never symmetric between two assistants on the same team); `is_private` correctly hides a drill from peers while the owner still sees it; the manager toggle correctly scopes per-relationship (turning it off for one assistant left a second assistant's visibility untouched); the self-service toggle works and immediately affects the head coach's visibility; cross-actor authorization holds (an assistant calling the manager-only RPC gets a hard "not authorized" error; a manager calling the self-only RPC on someone else's row silently affects zero rows, confirmed by re-reading the row unchanged afterward); a delegated assistant can create a practice and add practice_activities to it while a non-delegated assistant is flatly rejected by RLS on the same action; the one-delegate-per-team cap produces the friendly error, confirmed both via direct RPC call and by reproducing it live in the Permissions modal itself (toggle visibly stayed off, red error text rendered). Copying a peer-shared drill into a viewer's own library was confirmed to work with zero new authorization code, relying entirely on the RLS extension already being sufficient. All three test accounts and the test team were deleted afterward, with cascade confirmed to leave zero orphaned rows.

**Deliberately not done this session:** the Pro+/plan-based gating on who can be granted "Share Practice Planning" (Jax's fuller description mentioned needing the delegated assistant to be Pro or org, unless the head coach is on Pro+) -- the delegation mechanism itself is real and enforced, but which plan is required to use it is not wired to anything, consistent with this session's earlier, deliberate choice to keep all entitlement checks inert until real billing exists. The two equipment-mismatch dialogs (add-to-library and add-to-practice) remain unbuilt, unrelated to this piece.


### Twenty-fifth session continued (2026-08-01) — Pro+ tier, pricing polish, and a real assistant-invite consent flow

Jax came back the same day with pricing-page fixes, a new Pro+ tier to add, and a much bigger item: a real production gap hit live -- an assistant coach added to a team got no notification and was just silently, immediately active, with no accept/decline anywhere. Handled the small, unambiguous pieces directly, then investigated the invite/roster/library-sharing code before scoping the larger bundled feature request (Pro+ delegation permissions, drill-library default-peer-sharing, equipment-mismatch dialogs) rather than guessing at all of it in one pass.

**Pricing-page fixes, all straightforward:** the header was replaced with a reimplementation of `LandingPage`'s own `lp-header` pattern (sticky white bar, brand left, nav right) instead of the plain centered-logo bar it started with -- self-contained per this file's own convention, not an import from LandingPage. The "PRICING" eyebrow was bumped from 13px to 18px (matching `lp-eyebrow`'s own size) since it read as too small. The Annual/Monthly toggle now defaults to Monthly (overriding the original brief's "Annual is default" call, per direct instruction).

**Pro+ re-added** ($15/month or $124/year, no annual discount stated separately -- the toggle's shared helper text now reads "Save $45/year on Pro or $56/year on Pro+" since a single dollar figure stopped being accurate for two differently-priced tiers). Grants everything Pro does, plus delegating practice planning to one assistant coach per team, 2 concurrent live practices, and (per Jax's fuller description, not yet built -- see below) sharing those same permissions with assistants who aren't on Pro themselves. `src/entitlements.js`'s `PLAN_LIMITS` gained a `pro_plus` entry (`concurrentLivePractices`/`delegatedPlanningPerTeam` fields added to all three plans for a complete config) and `user_entitlements.plan_type`'s CHECK constraint was widened to allow `'pro_plus'` (`20260801000000_user_entitlements_pro_plus.sql`, written to find-and-drop the existing constraint by inspecting `pg_constraint` rather than assuming Postgres's default auto-generated name, since there's no local/Docker environment here to verify it against first) -- verified live by setting a real row to `pro_plus`, confirming a bogus value still gets rejected by the same constraint, then reverting. Same inert-scaffolding treatment as free/pro: nothing enforces the delegation/concurrency limits yet.

**The bigger piece: a real accept/decline consent flow for team invites**, since `add_team_staff` (shipped testing-round-1) auto-linked and granted access immediately with zero consent step -- fine when a solo coach was the only real user, not fine once a second real person is actually being added. Investigated first (an Explore pass over the invite/roster/library-sharing code) rather than guessing: found `org_invites` already had the exact pattern needed (real `pending`/`accepted`/`declined`/`cancelled` states, email matched against `auth.jwt()`, a Home accept/decline card), so a new `team_invites` table mirrors it closely (`20260801020000_team_invites.sql`) instead of retrofitting a status column onto `team_staff` itself -- `team_staff` is read by `can_access_team`/`can_manage_team` and referenced across most of the app, so gating it with a new pending state would have meant auditing every one of those call sites for a miss; a separate table keeps `team_staff` meaning exactly what it always has ("a real, already-consenting member") and needed zero changes to any existing access check.

Confirmed with Jax before building: existing active `team_staff` rows keep working exactly as before (nobody currently active loses access or sees a surprise invite prompt) -- the new consent flow only applies to invites sent going forward. The one class of existing row that _did_ need migrating: unlinked (`user_id is null`) `team_staff` rows never represented real access in the first place (nobody could act as that person yet), so those were copied into `team_invites` as pending and deleted from `team_staff` in the same migration -- zero of these existed for the one real account by the time this ran, confirmed before and after. `claim_pending_team_staff` (the old signup-time auto-link trigger) and `add_team_staff` itself were dropped outright, not left as dead code -- `team_invites` needs no claim trigger at all, for the same reason `org_invites` never did: a plain-text email row is already visible to its recipient the moment they sign in, matched against their own verified email, nothing needs pre-linking.

New RPCs: `invite_team_staff` (upserts by team+email regardless of the existing row's status, so it doubles as the resend/re-invite-after-decline path with no separate RPC needed), `accept_team_invite`/`decline_team_invite` (email-verified against `auth.jwt()`, mirroring `accept_org_invite`/`decline_org_invite` exactly, including reviving an archived `team_staff` row for someone being re-invited after leaving), `cancel_team_invite` (head-coach-side "Clear," pending or declined), and `edit_team_invite` (fixes a typo'd email/name/role, puts a pending-or-declined invite back to pending). The notify email (`notify-team-staff-added`, kept at the same deployed URL rather than standing up a new function name) now reads `team_invites` instead of `team_staff` and actually asks for a response instead of saying "no need to log in until you're ready" -- and branches its copy on whether the invited email already has a Run of Practice account ("Sign In to Respond" vs. "Create Your Account"; both point at the same OTP sign-in link since Email OTP creates an account on first use either way, only the wording differs). The "Invite Sent" confirmation modal and the roster's Coaches tab both got matching copy/UI updates: a "Pending Invites" section below the real roster shows each invite's status with Resend/Request Again, Edit, and Clear actions.

**A real RLS bug found and fixed during live verification, not just code review.** The new Home accept/decline card initially rendered "You've been invited to join **as an Assistant Coach**" with the team name missing entirely. `team_invites_select`'s RLS correctly let the invitee see their own invite row, but the client's `teams(id, name)` embed on that same query hits `teams`' own SELECT policy separately -- and someone with a still-pending invite has no `team_staff` row, no org membership, and isn't the owner, so `teams_select_access` blocked it. Same class of gap as the seventeenth session's `organizations_select_member` relaxation ("org name shown on team cards"), same fix shape: one narrow additional `OR` clause on `teams_select_access` (`20260801040000_teams_visible_to_pending_invitee.sql`) exposing only the team row (not its roster/practices/anything else) to someone with a genuine pending invite addressed to their own verified email. Confirmed fixed live via the real invitee's own session.

**Verified fully live, not just built.** Three disposable test accounts (a head coach, an assistant, a decliner) via the established `generate_link`+`verifyOtp`+localStorage-token-injection pattern: created a real team, sent a real invite through the actual Add Coach modal, confirmed the "Invite Sent" copy and the roster's Pending Invites section, switched to the assistant's session and confirmed the Home card (including the team-name fix above), accepted it and confirmed a real `team_staff` row was created with the correct role while `team_invites.status` flipped to `accepted`. Repeated with a second invite through Decline instead, confirming the roster showed "Declined" with no access granted, then exercised Request Again (declined → pending again), Edit (changed role, correctly did **not** appear to persist on the first attempt -- turned out to be a test-script artifact, two synchronous DOM clicks fired before React re-rendered between them, not a real bug; confirmed by re-running the same two actions as separate steps), and Clear (invite disappeared from the roster, `status` set to `cancelled`). All three test accounts deleted afterward via the Admin API, with cascade confirmed to have cleaned up the test team, its `team_staff` rows, and its `team_invites` rows down to zero.

**Also done, trivial and zero-risk:** the "What do I do if my sport isn't listed?" FAQ item, added both to the app's main `/faq` page (right after the existing "different sports" question, `LegalPages.jsx`) and to the pricing page's own FAQ (added earlier the same day during the pricing-scaffolding work, before this message's fuller list arrived).

**Deliberately not done this session** (the rest of the same original message, explicitly scoped as later phases): the two-way drill-library sharing permission system (head-coach/assistant "Share Drill Library"/"Share Practice Planning" toggles), the default-shared-with-peers library model with a per-drill private flag (today's sharing is entirely org-scoped -- a plain team_staff pair with no org has zero visibility into each other's libraries, confirmed via the same investigation pass), the Pro+-gated delegation-permission logic itself, and the two equipment-mismatch dialogs (add-to-library and add-to-practice).


### Twenty-fifth session (2026-07-31) — pricing/entitlements brief: new 3-tier structure, inert plan scaffolding

Jax shared a full pricing/entitlements/upgrade-UX implementation brief (Free/Pro/Organizations, replacing the prior 4-tier Free/Pro/Pro+/Org concept from last session) and asked to update the pricing page and start moving toward feature gating and upgrade opportunities, explicitly inviting scoping questions before work started.

**Phase 1 repo review (per the brief's own instructions) found this is a fully greenfield build.** No `plan_type`/`subscription`/`billing_status` column existed anywhere in the schema, no feature-flag system, no analytics event pipeline -- every limit in the prior pricing page's copy (2 assistants, 15 drills, etc.) was pure marketing text, never enforced anywhere. The app is live with exactly one real user (a solo coach) currently on completely unrestricted access. Given the brief itself is realistically weeks of work (a central entitlement service, server enforcement across 9 gates, trial/downgrade flows, an upgrade-prompt catalog, analytics), asked two scoping questions rather than guessing: how far to go this session, and how the one real early-access user should be treated once entitlement checks exist in the code at all. Confirmed: pricing page + inert entitlement scaffolding only (no live gates, no upgrade-prompt UI, no trial/downgrade flows this session), and every current/future account gets an implicit early-access bypass so nothing observable changes for the real coach until `BILLING_ENABLED` is deliberately flipped on in a future session.

**A real security gap identified and designed around before writing any schema.** The obvious approach -- add `plan_type`/`billing_status`/trial columns straight onto `profiles` -- would have been silently exploitable: `profiles` already has `grant select, update on public.profiles to authenticated` with no column-level restriction, and `profiles_update_own`'s RLS only checks `id = auth.uid()` (row-level, not column-level). Any signed-in coach could have set their own `plan_type` to `'pro'` via a plain `.update()` call. Solved by keeping billing state in a brand-new `user_entitlements` table instead (`20260731110000_user_entitlements.sql`) with a `select`-only grant to `authenticated` and no insert/update grant at all -- server-authoritative only, same "flat, separate gate" pattern `admin_users`/`is_admin()` already uses elsewhere in this schema, not a retrofit of profiles. One row per user, auto-created via a new `on_profile_created_entitlements` trigger (mirrors `handle_new_user`'s own pattern) and backfilled for all 22 existing profiles, defaulted to `plan_type='free'`, `early_access_eligible` left `null` ("not yet evaluated") rather than guessing the brief's not-yet-decided eligibility rule. Verified live: row count matches `profiles` exactly (22/22), `service_role` explicitly granted per this project's own documented "bypasses RLS but not GRANTs" gotcha.

**`src/entitlements.js`, new file: `FEATURE_FLAGS`, `PLAN_LIMITS`, and `can(user, action, resourceContext)`**, implemented close to verbatim from the brief's own recommended shapes. All six billing-state flags reflect the real current state (`EARLY_ACCESS_ACTIVE: true`, `BILLING_ENABLED: false`, `PRICING_PAGE_PUBLIC: false` since still unlinked, `PRO_PREVIEW_ENABLED`/`EARLY_ACCESS_OFFER_ENABLED`/`ORGANIZATION_LEAD_FORM_ENABLED: false`). `can()` is fully real logic, not a stub -- it correctly resolves plan limits and upgrade keys for every action in the brief's structured-denial catalog -- but every call resolves to `allowed: true` today because of the `EARLY_ACCESS_ACTIVE`/`BILLING_ENABLED` short-circuit at the top. **Not called from anywhere in the app yet**, on purpose, per this session's confirmed scope; the next session that actually wires up a gate should use this rather than reimplementing the same limit logic per-screen.

**`/pricing` fully rewritten to the brief's exact copy**, replacing the prior 4-tier version entirely (Pro+ removed, confirmed zero other references to it anywhere in `src/`/`supabase/` afterward). New: an early-access notice banner (shown/hidden by `FEATURE_FLAGS.EARLY_ACCESS_ACTIVE`), an accessible Annual/Monthly billing toggle (real buttons, `aria-pressed`, changes Pro's displayed price only -- Free/Organizations never show it, per the brief), a 2-card Free/Pro grid (was 4 cards) with Free stacking above Pro on mobile, a full-width Organizations section, a grouped feature-comparison table, and an 11-item FAQ accordion (reusing the same open/closed button pattern `LegalPages.jsx`'s `FAQItem` already established, not a new pattern). Added real per-route SEO for the first time on this page -- this app has no head-management library anywhere, so a small `useSeo()` effect sets `document.title`/`meta[name=description]` and restores them on unmount, plus a `noindex` robots tag gated on `PRICING_PAGE_PUBLIC` (currently on, since the page still isn't linked from anywhere, matching the brief's own "during internal review, use noindex" instruction). Both CTAs that would otherwise go to a real checkout instead point at `/` (start using the app now) or a plain `mailto:` for Organizations, matching `BILLING_ENABLED: false` and `ORGANIZATION_LEAD_FORM_ENABLED: false`.

**One real bug caught and fixed during live verification, not just code review.** The comparison table's mobile treatment (a horizontally-scrollable table with a sticky first "feature name" column, per the brief) initially used a single `<th colSpan={4}>` for each category's group-header row (e.g. "Teams", "Plan and run"). `position: sticky; left: 0` on that cell computed correctly (confirmed via `getComputedStyle`) but visibly did nothing when scrolled -- a real, if narrow, browser quirk where sticky positioning on a column-spanning table cell doesn't reliably pin the way it does on a single-column cell, even though the exact same CSS worked perfectly for the (single-column) row-header cells one row below it. Fixed by restructuring each group-header row into a single non-spanning `<th>` (using the same sticky mechanism already proven to work for feature-name cells) plus a separate `colSpan={3}` `<td>` just for the gray background band. Verified live at 375px width via `scrollLeft`-based JS scrolling (the Browser pane's synthetic horizontal-scroll gesture was unreliable in this sandbox -- consistent `computer timed out, pane hidden` errors on `scroll_direction: "right"` specifically, while vertical scrolls with the identical error still visibly worked; cross-checked with direct DOM inspection rather than trusting the gesture tool alone, similar in spirit to the twenty-third session's `read_console_messages` staleness lesson) -- group labels now stay pinned alongside the feature column as the table scrolls right to reveal Pro/Organizations values.

**Deliberately not done this session, all per the confirmed scope:** no upgrade-prompt modals/banners from the brief's catalog, no actual blocking anywhere (team/drill/template/assistant creation all remain fully unrestricted for the real coach), no trial countdown or 14-day-preview flow, no downgrade-selection UI, no analytics events, and `/pricing` is still not linked from navigation or the landing page. A server-side mirror of `can()` (a Postgres function, for when gates need real enforcement rather than a client-trusted check) also wasn't built -- there's no caller for it yet, and the brief's own Phase 2/3 sequencing puts real gate wiring after this scaffolding, not alongside it.


### Twenty-fourth session (2026-07-31) — a forward-looking pricing page, not yet linked

Jax shared a 4-tier pricing spreadsheet (Free/Pro/Pro+/Org) and asked for a `/pricing` page built from it, deliberately not linked from the landing page or anywhere else yet, plus expert pricing feedback and clarifying questions before building.

**Feedback given before building anything:** the Free tier's "view 2 previous practices" was flagged as a data-*retention* cap rather than a usage cap -- coaches remember losing access to something they already built, which tends to erode trust more than it drives upgrades; recommended keeping full history visible on Free and gating deeper analytics instead. Also flagged the Pro→Pro+ jump ($96→$150/yr, +56%) as resting on a fairly narrow value prop (one more concurrent live session, assistant write access) that's worth naming a persona for on the page rather than just listing deltas, and that Org's flat per-team rate combined with "unlimited coaches & helpers" sends mixed signals unless volume pricing is real (Jax confirmed it is -- see below).

**Clarified before building:** URL is `/pricing` (not `/plans`, the more standard SaaS convention); the page is explicitly forward-looking ("here's what pricing looks like at general availability") since the rest of the site still says "free during early access" everywhere and no billing system exists at all yet; every tier shows a plain "Coming Soon" badge instead of a real button, since wiring up fake checkout would be worse than no CTA; Pro+'s blank spreadsheet cells (templates, history, player focus, home program) inherit Pro's values and are stated outright rather than left blank, Pro+ has no monthly option, and "tryout mode" (empty in every tier) is left off the page entirely until it's defined.

**Org pricing is deliberately not public.** Jax confirmed per-team pricing actually does decrease as an organization adds more teams, so the page shows no number for Org at all, just "Custom pricing -- talk to us for a quote, pricing per team goes down as your organization grows." (matches how org creation already works in this app: white-glove, admin-gated, sales-assisted, not self-serve.)

**Every feature rewritten as a natural-language benefit sentence**, not a spec label, e.g. "concurrent live practices: 2" became "Run two practices live at the same time, ideal when your teams practice at overlapping hours." Each tier also got a one-line "who this is for" pitch (Free: a coach trying it out with one team; Pro: a coach running more than one team; Pro+: a coaching staff that plans together; Org: a club coaching multiple teams under one roof).

**Follow-up:** Jax reviewed and asked to drop the retention cap after all -- Free's practice-history line now matches Pro/Pro+/Org exactly ("Keep your entire practice history forever, and look back on any practice you've ever run"), consistent with the feedback given up front.

New file `src/components/PricingPage.jsx`, self-contained (its own scoped `<style>` block reusing the app's existing global CSS variables and Barlow Condensed type, not LandingPage's LP_CSS, which is only injected when LandingPage itself mounts). Routed at `/pricing` as a top-level sibling of `/terms`/`/privacy`/`/faq` (renders regardless of auth state). Verified live at desktop and mobile widths, and confirmed via grep that nothing else in the app references `/pricing` yet -- reachable only by typing the URL directly, as asked.


### Twenty-third session (2026-07-31) — a real regression caught and fixed, a 4th instance of the FK bug class, and an app-wide em-dash sweep

Jax asked three things: review the Upcoming-vs-History logic for practices run early/ahead of schedule and present options (not a silent fix); fix an em-dash in the "added to a team" email; and sweep the whole app for em-dashes generally. Two unrelated real bugs were found and fixed along the way.

**Reviewed: why a practice run ahead of schedule can still show in Upcoming Practices.** Both Home and `ScheduleScreen` already exclude a practice from their "upcoming" list the moment its live session's `practice_live_sessions.status` reads `'completed'` -- date-agnostic (a practice run this morning counts immediately, not just after midnight), confirmed via source and an explicit prior-handoff comment ("Completed practices leave the list entirely"). Live-tested directly: scheduled a practice 3 days out, opened it in Builder, hit Run Now (ahead of schedule) -- it behaves exactly as designed once the session actually completes. The real gap is the *other* status this schema tracks: `'abandoned'` (a session started but not finished through the app's own end-of-practice flow) is deliberately **not** treated as completed -- CommandScreen's own abort screen says so directly: "it won't count as completed. Start a fresh run any time." That's almost certainly what's actually being observed: a practice that got started but wasn't walked all the way through Finish stays in Upcoming/Schedule by design, not by bug. Presented options rather than picking one: (a) leave as-is (abandoned = still plannable, matches "want to try again"); (b) always move a *started* session (completed or abandoned) out of Upcoming into History, with its status badge distinguishing them, so "I ran this" always reads as history even if it didn't finish cleanly; (c) something in between (e.g. auto-resolve an abandoned session after some idle time). No code changed for this item pending Jax's direction.

**Real bug found and fixed while live-testing the above: a 4th instance of the missing-`ON DELETE`-action bug class.** Hard-deleting a disposable test team failed with `practice_live_sessions_practice_id_fkey` violated -- that column (and `preview_sessions.practice_id`) had no `ON DELETE` action at all, unlike `practice_activities.practice_id` (already `cascade`) and every other practice-scoped child table. Both are pure content rows scoped to a practice, so cascade is correct here (not the nullable + `SET NULL` "historical record" pattern used for actor columns). Also found `practice_live_sessions.controller_user_id` -- `NOT NULL`, no `ON DELETE` action -- the same actor-identity gap already fixed three times this history (`practice_series.created_by`, `organizations.created_by`, `feedback.user_id`), except this one was additionally `NOT NULL` and had to be relaxed first. `20260731100000_practice_live_sessions_actor_fk.sql` fixes all three; verified by retrying the same team's hard-delete afterward, which then succeeded, and again with a second, unrelated test account.

**Real bug found and fixed: an infinite render loop in PlayerProfile, already live in production from last session's work.** Reproduced fresh (clean tab, cleared console) the moment a player's profile was opened -- React's "Maximum update depth exceeded" warning, immediately, every time. Root cause: last session's `useBlocker` addition (for the three-way Save & Leave prompt) combined with a *pre-existing* effect that re-registers `subViewBack` (the screen's Back-button hookup) unconditionally on every render -- a pattern that had been safe on its own but fed back into itself once `useBlocker`'s own re-render was in the mix. Fixed by registering once via a ref (`handleBackRef`) instead of re-running the effect every render, preserving "Back always sees the latest isDirty" without the per-render `setSubViewBack` call. Caught what looked like a second confirmation failure mid-fix: `read_console_messages` kept reporting the identical stale error after the fix, across a full dev-server restart -- turned out to be the *tool* returning buffered/stale output, not a real persisting bug; confirmed the fix was real via an independent in-page `console.error` counter (patched fresh, zero fires across opening the profile and editing a field) rather than trusting that tool alone. Worth remembering: don't take `read_console_messages` as authoritative after a fix in a long-running tab/session without cross-checking another way.

**Em-dashes removed app-wide.** The team-added email notification's line was rewritten to "This is just an FYI, no need to log in until you're ready." (both `notify-team-staff-added` and `notify-org-invite` edge functions redeployed, since both bundle the shared `_shared/emailTemplate.ts` at deploy time). Full sweep of `src/` and `supabase/functions/` for the literal em-dash character found and fixed real user-facing instances in `PracticePlanPrint.jsx`, `HomeScreen.jsx`, `LegalPages.jsx` (Privacy Policy), `CommandScreen.jsx` (several live-session status strings), and `FounderMetricsScreen.jsx` (subtitle prose) -- replaced with the app's existing " · " separator convention for short label-style fragments, and with plain sentence splits for full prose. Deliberately left the `"—"` used in `FounderMetricsScreen.jsx` as a null-value placeholder (an "N/A" convention, not punctuation). Also cleaned up a handful of code-comment section dividers that used real em-dashes purely stylistically, for full compliance, even though comments aren't user-facing.


### Twenty-second session (2026-07-31) — 8-item batch: two real bugs found and fixed, one new coach<->coach notification feature

Jax sent an 8-item batch touching Builder, Roster, the assistant-coach-add flow, My Team Assignments copy, the org-invite popup, the Teams list, per-team permissions, and a brand-new "someone left" notification.

**Builder: dashed outline removed from the green Run of Practice zone.** Purely cosmetic -- the `border:"2px dashed rgba(255,255,255,.4)"` on the absolute backdrop is gone; the solid green fill (and the real gap below it from last session) stays.

**Roster: PlayerProfile's Save button is now always present, and leaving with unsaved changes prompts to save.** Previously Save/Discard only rendered once the form was dirty; Save now always shows (disabled until dirty), matching the request that it "always be there." The old leave-guard was a plain `window.confirm("...Leave without saving?")` -- offering only leave-or-cancel, no way to actually save from the prompt. Replaced with a real three-way `.confirm-box` (Save & Leave / Leave Without Saving / Cancel), and widened its coverage: the screen's own Back button already went through a local handler, but bottom-tab and workspace-tab taps navigate via the router directly and previously bypassed it entirely. Added `useBlocker` (same guard `BuilderScreen`/`GoalsScreen` already use for their own unsaved-changes cases, both already importing it) plus a `beforeunload` listener for a hard reload/tab-close. Verified live: editing a jersey number, then tapping the bottom Home tab (a pure router nav, not the screen's own Back), correctly surfaced the three-way prompt; Save & Leave saved (confirmed the number persisted after re-opening the player) and navigated away in one step.

**Assistant-coach-add confirmation copy rewritten.** Was "{name} will get an email at {email}, and you can also just tell them: sign in at runofpractice.com with {email}." Now "{name} will get an email at {email} and will see {team name} when they log in." -- shorter, and tells the head coach what the invitee will actually see, not just how to reach them. `ModalLayer.jsx`'s `addedCoachInfo` now carries `teamName` (looked up from `modal.payload.teamId`) alongside name/email.

**My Team Assignments: em-dash removed from the "Show on Home" explainer.** The paragraph used a spaced `--` mid-sentence ("...Home agenda -- turning it off doesn't remove you..."), the same em-dash-substitute convention this file's own comments use, but which had leaked into user-facing copy. Split into two plain sentences instead.

**Real bug found and fixed: the "you were added to a team" welcome card could keep reappearing after being accepted.** Root cause: the mount-time effect that marks a `team_staff` row welcomed (`markTeamStaffWelcomed`) never refreshed the local `teams` state afterward -- the "Accept" button only ever set a component-local `dismissedWelcomeIds` Set, which resets to empty on remount. A full page reload always fetches fresh data (so `welcomedAt` reads correctly there), but *navigating away and back within the app* (tapping Teams then Home, no reload) remounts `HomeScreen` with a fresh empty dismissal set while `data.teams` still held the stale, never-refreshed `welcomedAt: null` -- so the card came back. Fixed by chaining `.then(() => refreshTeams())` onto the mark call, so the real data catches up within about a second of mount, independent of the local-only Accept dismissal. Confirmed live: `team_staff.welcomed_at` was set correctly (`2026-07-31T15:17:44Z`, moments after the assistant's first login), well before any subsequent navigation could have raced it.

**Teams page now shows the same coach-type/org shortcut Home's own team cards already do.** `TeamsListScreen.jsx`'s per-team row only showed sport/player-count/org name; added the same `myTeamRole(team, coachId)` label (e.g. "HEAD COACH" / "ASSISTANT COACH") Home's cards use, gated the same way (Coach mode only -- Org mode's own header already establishes context). Verified from both a head coach's and an assistant's view of the same team.

**Assistant coaches no longer see the team-options ellipsis.** `Layout.jsx`'s colored team-identity bar rendered the ellipsis (which opens "Edit Team") unconditionally for anyone viewing a team workspace -- an assistant coach, who can't actually manage the team, saw an affordance implying they could. Gated behind `canManageTeamInMode(team, coachId, mode)`, the same check already used elsewhere for Add Coach/Player, +Practice, etc. Required threading `coachId` down through `LayoutRoute` → `Layout` (wasn't there before). Verified live: the head coach's view of a team keeps the ellipsis; the assistant coach's view of the identical team has none.

**New: head coach gets notified on Home when an assistant coach or helper leaves a team.** `leave_team` was explicitly "silent to the head coach beyond the roster reflecting it" by original design (see the tenth-session migration's own comment) -- direct feedback asked for the same treatment the existing "you were added" card gets, in the other direction. Since the departing `team_staff` row gets archived and excluded from every existing fetch (`archived_at is null`), there was nowhere for the head coach's client to see it, so this needed a new table rather than another column: `team_departures` (migration `20260731090000_team_departure_notices.sql`), one row per self-initiated departure (populated inside `leave_team` itself, not `archiveStaff` -- a head-coach-initiated removal is something the head coach already knows about firsthand), visible via a `can_manage_team`-gated select policy, dismissed via a narrow `acknowledge_team_departure` RPC. Home renders it exactly like the requested pattern: "{name} ({role}) left {team}." with **Acknowledge** (a real server-side write, not a local-only dismissal like the welcome card's) and **View Coaches** (navigates straight to `/team/:id/roster`). Verified live end-to-end with two real accounts: added an existing coach as assistant, had them leave via My Team Assignments, confirmed the notice appeared for the head coach with correct name/role/team, confirmed View Coaches landed on the roster (now showing Coaches (1)), confirmed Acknowledge dismissed it, and confirmed it stayed dismissed after a full reload (server-side `acknowledged_at`, not session-local).

**Live verification.** Two disposable test accounts (a head coach and an assistant), a real team, a real player, a real assistant-coach add/accept/leave cycle, and a full Save & Leave round-trip on the roster -- all driven through the actual UI in a local dev server against production Supabase, not just reviewed as a diff. Both accounts and all their data deleted via the Admin API afterward.


### Twenty-first session (2026-07-30) — fifth same-day round: stripe side, real Run of Practice gap, a genuinely broken caret

Jax tested the twentieth session's changes live and came back with 3 more small items, same day.

**Practice Details stripe moved from right to left.** Purely a follow-up preference after last round's right-side placement -- `position:absolute` stripe now `left:0` instead of `right:0`, and the compensating 6px spacing moved from the chevron's `marginRight` to the "Practice Details" label's `marginLeft` (the stripe is now adjacent to the label, not the chevron).

**Real gap added between the green Run of Practice section and the black Practice Components bar below it.** This was a genuine bug, not a style preference: `runOfPracticeEndRef`'s `height:14` div was doing double duty as both the green backdrop's measurement endpoint (`eRect.bottom` in the `ResizeObserver` effect) and the intended bottom padding, so the green area painted through the entire 14px with zero actual gap before Practice Components started immediately after. Fixed by leaving that sentinel alone (it's still the green's correct bottom inset) and adding `marginTop:14` to the Practice Components bar itself instead -- that space is outside what gets measured into `runOfPracticeH`, so it now renders as real visible space rather than more green. Verified with an activity added (backdrop grows correctly, gap holds).

**Library Drills per-sport caret bug, fixed.** In `NewLibraryScreen.jsx`'s "My Drills" tab, the per-sport collapsible section header used a literal `"v"` character as its own hand-rolled caret, shown only when *expanded* (`collapsed[sport]?"":"v"`) -- meaning it disappeared entirely on collapse, backwards from what a disclosure indicator should do. Also matches Jax's separate complaint that it "looks too much like a v" -- it literally was the letter v. Replaced with the same `▶`/`▼` treatment already used by the three other per-sport/per-category collapsible sections in this same file (Equipment tab, Player Gear, Skill Tags), so it's now both correct and visually consistent with the rest of the screen.

**Live verification.** Local dev server, fresh disposable test account, a real team with an orange color (deliberately different from the app's own green, to make the stripe unambiguous), a scheduled practice opened in Builder, and a real drill added to the library. Confirmed live: the stripe renders on the left with clean label spacing, a real visible gap now separates green from black both empty and with a row added, and the Football drills section's caret flips between `▼` (expanded) and `▶` (collapsed) instead of vanishing. Test account and its data deleted via the Admin API afterward; temporary scripts removed from the repo.


### Twentieth session (2026-07-30) — fourth same-day round: margin regression, Practice Details walked back, real contact form, a third FK-deletion bug
Jax tested the nineteenth session's changes live and came back with 4 more items same day.

**Run of Practice margin regression, fixed.** The green backdrop (introduced last round to fix the sticky-containment bug) was rendering 14px wider on each side than Practice Components/My Drill Library. Root cause: it was `position:absolute; left:0; right:0` inside the padded `padding:"0 14px"` outer wrapper -- but an absolutely positioned element is placed relative to its containing block's *padding* edge, which by definition ignores that same padding, so `left:0` meant "flush with the wrapper's outer edge," not "inset by the wrapper's own padding" the way a normal-flow sibling (Practice Components' bar) automatically is. Fixed by anchoring the backdrop to `runOfPracticeStartRef` itself instead of the padded outer wrapper -- that sentinel is a zero-padding, normal-flow child of the padded wrapper, so it's already correctly inset, and `left:0`/`right:0` relative to *it* means what it looks like it means. Also simplified away the now-unneeded separate `top` offset tracking (`runOfPracticeBox` → plain `runOfPracticeH`), since anchoring to the sentinel makes `top:0` automatically correct.

**Practice Details walked back from a full team-color background to solid black with a stripe.** Direct follow-up: the full-bar team-color tint (added last round) read as too loud. Now always `var(--black)`, with `team.colorPrimary` (when set) rendered as a plain 6px `position:absolute` stripe down the right edge instead -- same "the team still shows up here" intent, more subtle execution.

**Real contact form replaces both "request a consultation" mailto links.** Previously a bare `mailto:` link on Settings' main list (non-admin coaches) and in the FAQ answer for "How do I set up an organization with multiple teams?" -- gave Jax nothing to prep for the actual sales call with, no phone number, no sense of club size. New `ConsultationRequestForm` (`LegalPages.jsx`, exported for `SettingsScreen.jsx` to reuse) collects name/email/phone/number-of-teams (required) plus an encouraged free-text field ("sport, location, timeline, anything that'll help us prep for the call"), and reuses the *existing* `feedback` table/`submitFeedback`+`submitPublicFeedback` RPCs rather than new backend surface -- exactly the "future non-authenticated contact form" this file's own Known Gaps entry already flagged `submitPublicFeedback` as being kept around for. `coachId` present (Settings, always authenticated) → the authenticated insert path with `user_id` attached; absent (FAQ, reachable signed out) → the anonymous RPC. The structured fields get formatted into the table's single `message` column (name/phone/team-count as labeled lines, then the free text) since a new dedicated table felt like overkill for what's still fundamentally a triaged-by-Jax-directly lead list. Real gap caught before shipping: the form's `send()` originally set "success" unconditionally without checking either helper's return value for an error -- for a sales-lead form, silently claiming success on a failed submit would be actively harmful, not just a minor bug. Fixed to check `result.error` and show a real error state with a fallback "email us directly" message instead.

**Copy reworded in both places** (no em dashes): "We set these up with you rather than offering instant self-service" → "Our team personally sets up every organization, so sports, teams, and coaches all start out configured correctly" -- same underlying process (org creation is still admin-gated, not self-serve), reframed from a limitation to a value-add, since a prospective club reading the old wording could reasonably read it as "this product isn't ready for real organizations."

**Settings card relocated.** Removed entirely from the main Settings list (the first thing anyone poking around Settings sees, arguably the worst place for anything that reads as "this needs us to configure it for you"); added to `AccountSection` under a new "Membership" header, between "Your Info" and "Legal," gated to `!isAdmin` (founder-admins keep the real self-serve create-org flow, unchanged, still on the main list).

**A third instance of the missing-ON-DELETE-action bug, found and fixed.** Hard-deleting the test account used for this session's verification failed the same way the `practice_series.created_by` and `organizations.created_by` bugs did in earlier sessions: an opaque `AuthRetryableFetchError` from the JS admin client, with the real Postgres error only visible via the raw REST endpoint -- `feedback_user_id_fkey` had no `ON DELETE` action, blocking deletion of any account that had ever submitted feedback (including, now, a consultation request). `feedback.user_id` was already nullable; `20260731000000_feedback_user_id_actor_fk.sql` drops and re-adds the FK with `on delete set null`, the same established pattern. Verified by retrying the hard-delete on the same account after applying the migration, which then succeeded. Two harmless test `feedback` rows (from verifying both the anonymous and authenticated submit paths) are still sitting in the table, clearly identifiable by their `claude-verify`/`claude-consult-test` emails -- that table deliberately has no delete policy for any role, including no `service_role` base-table grant for `DELETE`/`SELECT` (confirmed hitting `permission denied for table feedback` when trying), consistent with its own migration comment that triage is manual, by Jax, not through the app. Left for Jax to clear manually rather than granting broader table access just for this cleanup.

**Live verification.** Local dev server against the real Supabase backend, a fresh disposable test account, a real team (with a picked color) built through the actual UI. Confirmed live: the margin fix (screenshotted, all four sections' edges now align exactly), the black-bar-with-stripe Practice Details styling, both the anonymous (FAQ) and authenticated (Settings) consultation-form submission paths end-to-end (including a direct RPC call from a script to confirm `submit_public_feedback` returns `{success:true}`), the Settings card's new location under Account > Membership, and its removal from the main list. Test account and all its data (including the now-successfully-cascaded feedback row) deleted via the Admin API afterward; temporary scripts removed from the repo.


### Nineteenth session (2026-07-30) — third same-day Builder round: the sticky fix, and a real duration data-loss bug found along the way
Jax came back a third time after actually using the redesign, with 13 more Builder-specific items, again asking for clarifying questions or pushback where warranted. Asked one question up front (which section should take on the selected team's color -- Run of Practice, Practice Details, or both; answer: Practice Details only, Run of Practice keeps the app's own brand green) and flagged one real tension in the requests themselves before writing code: "auto-expand a newly-added drill" plus "keep it sticky-pinned to the top while scrolling" together recreate almost exactly the "very tall sticky element blocks scrolling" problem a code comment from two sessions ago was written specifically to avoid. Proceeded anyway since this session's *other* request (per-station collapse in Station Block) meaningfully shrinks the worst case, but said so rather than silently picking one behavior.

**What shipped, all in `BuilderScreen`/`ActivityConfigs.jsx` unless noted:**
- **Auto-expand + click-away collapse**: `addAct`/`addBlock`/`addComponentType` now all call `setExpandedId` (previously only the component/station paths did). A new `useEffect` attaches a single `document` `click` listener whenever a row is expanded, collapsing it (and clearing `lastAddedId`) on any click outside that row and outside any open overlay (`.closest(".movly")`) -- deliberately *not* trying to enumerate every button that has its own expandedId-setting logic (a Practice Component tile, another row's own header); instead both `setExpandedId`/`setLastAddedId` calls use functional updaters that only null out the id if it's *still* the same one the effect was attached for, so a same-tick update from some other handler (switching to a different row, adding a new activity) naturally wins regardless of which handler happens to run first in the click's bubble order, rather than being clobbered. Verified live via a scripted press-and-release sequence and by clicking Practice Details' own bar mid-edit.
- **The sticky fix (the real one).** Investigating "we had this before but something broke it" turned up the actual mechanism: `position:sticky` can never move an element past its own DOM parent's bottom edge, and the green box wrapping the activity rows (added when this section's redesign first landed, two sessions ago) is sized to fit almost exactly the rows themselves -- live-measured at just ~21px of "room" below the last row before the box's own bottom passed the stick point, nowhere near enough to visibly hold during a real scroll through the drill library below. Not something this session's own changes caused, but the real root cause, confirmed live via `getBoundingClientRect()` scroll traces both before the fix (row position tracked scroll 1:1, no hold at all past the ~21px window) and after. Fixed by a genuine restructure: the header/empty-state/rows now render as normal children of the same tall `padding:"0 14px"` wrapper Practice Components/My Drill Library already live in (so sticky has the *entire* rest of the page as room to work with), and the green background is a separate `position:absolute` backdrop sized via a `ResizeObserver` measuring two sentinel refs (`runOfPracticeStartRef`/`runOfPracticeEndRef`) rather than by actually containing the rows. `SortableActivityRow`'s `zIndex` is now always ≥1 (was `undefined` when not sticky/dragging) so every row reliably paints above the `zIndex:0` backdrop regardless of state. Re-verified live after the restructure: scroll-traced the same row and confirmed it now holds exactly at `top:55` across a real scroll range instead of sliding past it, and confirmed click-away-collapse still works correctly on a row that's simultaneously sticky.
- **Per-station collapse** (`StationConfig`): each station's header row gained a caret; collapsed shows just "Station N" + the drill/activity name, everything else (description, grouping, coach, equipment, players, ...) hides. Manual only, no auto-collapse -- new stations from "+ Add Station" still open expanded, matching how a freshly-added activity already behaves.
- **My Drill Library collapse caret**, matching Practice Details/Practice Components, defaulting open (`myDrillsOpen`, initial `true`).
- **Long-press preview removed entirely** (added just last round) -- a real gap found in actual use: a scrolling touch gesture was being misread as a long-press hold. Deleted `longPressHandlers` from `ActivityConfigs.jsx`, the preview modal, `previewItem` state, and the now-unused `assetsById`/`equipNames` helpers; drills and component tiles are plain tap-to-add again, unchanged from before that feature existed.
- **Practice Details**: moved inside the same `padding:"0 14px"` wrapper as its sibling sections -- it had been rendering *outside* that wrapper (relying on its own now-removed 14px `.card` padding to match), so once that padding became a black bar instead, its outer box was 28px wider than everything below it. Gained a Date field and a Duration (min) field for an already-scheduled practice (`editP`), reordered Date+Start Time paired above Location+Duration, both now backed by real `schedDate`/new `schedDuration` state. The bar itself now takes on `team.colorPrimary` when a team is selected (falls back to black).
- **Real bug found wiring up Duration, unrelated to anything asked for**: `handleSave`/`handleRun`/`doSchedule` never included `scheduledDurationMinutes` in their `savePracticeTree` calls, and that function does a full column `.update()` -- meaning saving an already-scheduled practice from Builder was silently resetting its target duration to `null` on *every single save*, the whole time this feature has existed. Threading `schedDuration` through all three calls (and reading it live for the progress pill, instead of the stale `editP` prop) fixes this as a side effect of the new UI, not a separate patch.
- **Unscheduled-build entry path**: `detailsOpen` now defaults to `!editP` (open for a fresh build, collapsed for an existing scheduled practice, was unconditionally `false`). The Schedule/Template button row is gone; Save's `onClick` is conditional (`editP` still saves directly, unchanged) -- for a fresh build it opens a small chooser (`bottomMode==="savechoice"`) offering "Template" (routes into the existing template-name flow) or "Add to Schedule" (opens the existing Schedule modal, which also gained the same Duration field).
- **Select-all on focus**: `ChecklistConfig`'s Name field (shared by all 6 checklist-type Practice Components, including "Other") now selects its existing text on focus, so typing immediately replaces a default name like "Other" instead of requiring a manual select-all first.
- **Practice Components persistence, investigated and confirmed already correct.** Re-read `getVisibleComponentTypes`/`setVisibleComponentTypes` carefully first (both looked right), then verified the actual reported scenario live rather than trusting the code review alone: toggled Station Block off, navigated all the way back to Home (not just within the same Builder mount), opened a brand-new unscheduled practice, and confirmed Station Block was still correctly absent from the tile row. No bug found or fixed here -- it was already working; flagged back rather than "fixing" something that wasn't broken.

**Live verification.** Same pattern as the prior two rounds: local dev server (`npm run dev`) against the real Supabase backend, a fresh disposable test session via the `.env` service-role key, a real team/player/location/drill built through the actual UI. Coordinate-based `computer` clicks continued to be unreliable in this environment (silently no-op'd more than once); every interaction in this round went through `javascript_tool` DOM dispatch instead (`.click()`, native-setter + `input`/`change` events), which was reliable throughout -- worth defaulting to that here going forward rather than `computer` clicks. Test account and all its data deleted via the Admin API afterward; temporary mint/cleanup scripts removed from the repo.


### Eighteenth session continued (2026-07-30) — Builder rebuilt again same day, this time verified live
Jax came back with 5 more Builder-specific requests immediately after the batch above, explicitly asking for clarifying questions first given the size (a new 7-type customizable "Practice Components" system, an animated brand mark, long-press previews). Asked 3 questions before starting -- long-press preview scope (drills only vs. every component type, including the empty generic ones), what the Practice Components "..." menu actually does (a persisted customize-the-toolbar picker vs. a one-shot add-anything menu), and whether the new component types (Water Break/Stretch/custom Checklist/Other) needed genuinely different live-run behavior or could just be more checklist activities with different defaults. Answers: preview everything (including natural-language "why you'd use this" blurbs for the 7 component types, since they have no real content otherwise; "Other" is explicitly a nameable one-off, never saved to the library), customize-the-toolbar with per-coach persistence, and reuse the checklist shape as-is (avoids touching the live-session runner, PDF export, or Goals bucketing at all, all of which already key off `act.type==="checklist"` generically rather than by name -- confirmed by grep before committing to this approach).

**What shipped, all in `BuilderScreen` (`App.jsx`) unless noted:**
- **Scroll-to-top after Done**: tapping Done inside an expanded drill/station/checklist config used to leave the scroll position wherever it was, which -- since the body collapsing back down shifts everything below it upward -- often dropped the coach somewhere in the middle of the drill library with no obvious context. Each `.ablk` row now keeps a plain ref in a `rowRefs` map; a new `collapseAndScroll(id)` collapses (`setExpandedId(null)`) and then `scrollIntoView({behavior:"smooth",block:"start"})`s that same row, via `requestAnimationFrame` so it runs after the collapse has actually shrunk the layout. All three configs' `onDone` now call this instead of the bare `setExpandedId(null)`.
- **Long-press preview**: a new non-hook `longPressHandlers(store,id,onLongPress,onTap,ms)` (`ActivityConfigs.jsx`) -- deliberately not a hook, since a whole list of these needs building one per row inside a `.map()`, which the Rules of Hooks don't allow; the mutable per-row timer state lives in one `Map` the caller keeps via a single `useRef`, so a re-render mid-press can't orphan a still-pending timeout the way a fresh-per-render hook closure would. A plain tap still adds directly (unchanged); holding ~500ms opens a preview modal (drill: name, sport/duration, description, coaching points, skill tags, equipment via new `assetsById`/`equipNames` helpers; component type: label, duration, and a short description) with "Back"/"Add to Practice" buttons, the latter running the exact same add path a tap would. Verified live that a real long-press-then-release does *not* also fire a normal add (the click-suppression `fired` flag), including via a scripted `pointerdown` → wait 700ms → `pointerup` + `click()` sequence that confirmed the row count stayed at 1.
- **Practice Components, reworked into 7 selectable types**: `PRACTICE_COMPONENT_TYPES` (`constants.js`) -- Intro, Closer, Checklist, Water Break, Stretch, Station Block, Other, each with a default name/duration and a natural-voice description (no em dashes) -- replaces the old hardcoded Intro/Closer/Station-Block-only tile row. `getVisibleComponentTypes()`/`setVisibleComponentTypes()` persist which types show as one-tap tiles in `localStorage` (`rop_practice_component_types`), defaulting to today's exact set (Intro/Closer/Station Block) so nobody's Builder changes shape unless they open the new "..." button and pick something different. The section header is now a black bar (matching Practice Details/My Drill Library) with a collapse caret and the "..." (a literal horizontal ellipsis, not the app's existing vertical 3-dot `.ell-btn` kebab, since Jax asked for "an ellipsis" specifically and this opens a whole picker, not a small dropdown) opening "Add/Remove Practice Components" -- a checklist-style picker with a green checkmark circle per type. `addChecklist(isClose)` was replaced entirely by a generic `addComponentType(key)` that looks up defaults from `PRACTICE_COMPONENT_TYPES` and either delegates to `addBlock()` (station_block) or creates a checklist activity -- confirmed via grep that no other screen (`NewLibraryScreen.jsx`'s TemplateWorkspace, `CommandScreen.jsx`'s mid-run LiveEditBuilder) references the old function; both have their own independent Intro/Closer logic and were deliberately left untouched, out of scope.
- **The Run of Practice, now solid green**: header and content (whether the empty message or the real list) share one `var(--green)` background with a dashed white-ish border framing the whole section, replacing the previous black canvas + separate light empty-state box. "The Run of Practice" is now bigger (20px, was 14px) and white. The duration pill is forced white-on-green for the normal case (the default `.pill` styling is light-green-on-white and would nearly vanish against solid green) but deliberately *not* forced when the under-planned `.pill.over` (red) state is active, so that warning still reads correctly rather than being silently hidden by the override -- caught and fixed before shipping, not found live.
- **Animated brand mark**: `RunOfPracticeMark` reuses `LoadingScreen`'s existing stopwatch SVG (the app's own loading-screen logo) but swaps its infinite CSS `tick` keyframe for a controlled `rotation` prop with a `transition:transform .6s` -- a `handRotation` state increments by 360 on every add (`addAct`/`addBlock`/`addComponentType`) and decrements by 360 on every remove (`remAct`), so the hand does one full spin forward or backward per action rather than spinning continuously. Verified live via the rendered `<g>` element's actual `style` attribute across an add/add/remove sequence: `rotate(360deg)` → `rotate(720deg)` → back to `rotate(360deg)`.
- **My Drill Library / Practice Details, black bars**: My Drill Library's bar recolored from green to `var(--black)` with an explicit `minHeight:40` (shared with Practice Components' bar) so the two read as the same thickness regardless of which buttons/icons they happen to contain -- previously they were both green but only incidentally similar heights. Practice Details got the identical black-bar treatment plus its own collapse caret, defaulting to **collapsed** (a new `detailsOpen` state, `false` initially) with a one-line summary (team + location) visible in the collapsed bar so collapsing it doesn't leave the coach with zero context.
- **Chevron moved**: the per-row expand/collapse chevron (added last round) now renders before the duration badge instead of between the duration and the red delete X, so a coach tapping near "expand" can no longer land on "delete" by mistake.

**Live verification, this time actually completed.** Started a local dev server (`npm run dev` via the `rop-dev` launch config, port 5173) against the real Supabase backend, minted a fresh disposable test session the same way as the seventeenth session, and this time the injection/interaction worked once auto mode was confirmed off (the Browser pane blocked the same category of action three times across this and the prior round before finally going through -- root cause still unclear, but retrying after explicit confirmation eventually succeeded both times). Coordinate-based `computer` clicks were unreliable in this environment (several silently no-op'd, e.g. the first two attempts at the name-capture Continue button and the Add Player Save button); dispatching real DOM events via `javascript_tool` (`.click()`, native-setter + `input`/`change` events for controlled inputs) was reliable throughout and is the better default here going forward. Built a real throwaway team ("Claude Testers," Basketball), player, location ("Test Gym"), and drill ("Layup Lines," with "Cones" equipment) entirely through the UI, then exercised every item above directly in Builder -- all confirmed working exactly as designed, including two bugs caught and fixed *before* this pass (the pill-override-hides-the-red-warning issue above, and an earlier over-engineered `SortableActivityRow` `outerRef`/hook-in-a-`.map()` design for long-press that was simplified before ever being tested). Also incidentally re-confirmed four items from the batch above live for the first time (Explore rename, sport-default/ordering, the skill-tags helper line, the equipment-not-added save guard) and sanity-checked that Station Block's existing Group By button still renders after `addComponentType` started delegating to `addBlock`. Test account and all its data (team, player, location, drill, practice) were deleted via the Admin API afterward; the temporary mint/cleanup scripts were removed from the repo, same as every prior round.


### Eighteenth session (2026-07-30) — 13-item direct-feedback batch, live verification attempted but blocked by tooling
Jax gave a single message with 13 distinct items spanning Builder, Home, the player-out picker, the skill-tag picker, the drill-equipment form, sign-in copy, and Library's Drills tab. Implemented all 13 and confirmed `vite build` clean, then tried to re-run the seventeenth session's real live-browser verification (disposable test account via the `.env` `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, `generate_link`+`verifyOtp`, tokens injected into the Browser pane's `localStorage`) -- initially missed doing this at all until Jax pointed at the `.env` file directly. The mechanism itself still works end-to-end (minted a session, injected it, reloaded, landed correctly on the first-sign-in name-capture screen), but the sandboxed Browser pane's own permission layer then blocked further form-filling on `runofpractice.com` repeatedly and inconsistently -- it wasn't a clean auto-mode-on/off story: it blocked with auto mode on, blocked again immediately after Jax turned it off, then let one `javascript_tool` call through, then blocked `form_input` three more times in a row including right after being told auto mode was off. Given that inconsistency, stopped rather than keep guessing at a workaround. Both throwaway accounts (neither had gotten far enough to attach any real data) were deleted via the Admin API before stopping, and the temporary `scripts/tmp-verify-mint.mjs`/`tmp-cleanup.mjs` helper scripts were removed afterward -- not left in the repo. **Nothing in this session's changes has actually been seen live** -- flag anything that doesn't look right once it's actually seen, same caveat this file has raised before, but the credential/pattern are confirmed working for whoever picks this up next; the blocker was the Browser pane's permission handling, not the testing setup.

**Builder (`App.jsx`'s `BuilderScreen`), the largest single piece:**
- Station Block's "Group By..." button (`StationConfig`, `ActivityConfigs.jsx`) now shows which criterion was last applied ("Group By...Position") via a new local `groupByLabel` state, cleared by Generate Random/Clear Groups, set by picking Position or a handedness option. Purely a UI label, not persisted -- a coach can tap the button again to change the selection, same as before.
- A coach can now add a brand-new location straight from Builder's own Location `<select>`, not just when the team has zero locations configured: a `"+ Add New Location..."` option is always present in the dropdown (sentinel value `__add_new__`), routes to the existing `AddLocationDialog` (already wired for the zero-locations case, unchanged), which saves via `createLocation`/`createOrgLocation` (so it shows up in Library's Locations tab afterward) and auto-selects the new location on return.
- A margin gap below the Practice Details card, found by re-deriving the box model rather than guessing: `.screen` (`Layout.jsx`'s route wrapper) already gives every route 14px of horizontal padding, and the top nav row/`.card` each add their own 14px on top of that (28px total to their content) -- but everything below the card (the activity-count row, the activity list, Add Drills, the sport header, the drill list) relied on `.screen`'s 14px alone, so it read as flush against the edge by comparison. Fixed by wrapping that whole remainder in one `padding:"0 14px"` container to match.
- Two new dark-green (`var(--green)`) title bars, styled like the rest of the app's Barlow Condensed uppercase headers rather than a new pattern: "Practice Components" above the Intro/Closer/Station Block builders, and "My Drill Library" (with the existing "+ New Activity" button folded into it) above the actual drill list -- previously both lived under one flat "Add Drills" header with no visual distinction between "structural pieces" and "your saved drills."
- "Practice Setup" → "Practice Details" (the card's own `.clbl` label, wiring unchanged).
- The activity-count/duration row ("3 Activities" + the `35/60 min` pill) is now headed "The Run of Practice" and shows in both the empty and populated states (previously the whole row, including any header, was suppressed entirely until at least one activity existed or a scheduled duration was set).
- Save and Run Now moved up into the same row as Back (previously Back stood alone above a separate 4-button bar); the "Editing: Team · Date · Time" banner now sits directly under that row. Schedule/Template (only shown for a not-yet-scheduled practice) moved to their own row below the banner, same underlying `bottomMode` state machine, unchanged.
- Empty-state copy: "Nothing added yet. Select activities below to begin building your practice." → "...Add activities below to begin building your Run of Practice."
- Each drill/station row (`.abhdr`) now shows a chevron (`Ic.Chev`, already used elsewhere in the app) that flips between up/down with the expanded state, next to the existing duration badge -- direct feedback was that tapping the header to expand wasn't obviously interactive before.
- Once the practice has at least one activity, the whole list renders inside a `var(--black)` container so it reads as a distinct "this is what's actually being built" canvas, separate from the light Practice Details card above it and the light drill library below. The empty-state placeholder box stays its existing light/dashed style -- the dark treatment is specifically for the populated list, per Jax's own "being built" framing.

**Everywhere else:**
- Two text-wrapping fixes, same root cause in two files: `PlanPill` (`HomeScreen.jsx` and its independent copy in `ScheduleScreen.jsx` -- this codebase's existing per-file-duplication convention, not shared) rendered `{total}/{scheduledDurationMinutes} min` as plain adjacent text nodes with no `white-space:nowrap`, so a tight card could wrap between "0/60" and "min"; `HomeScreen.jsx`'s "N out" absence count had the same issue. Both now wrap in a `whiteSpace:"nowrap"` span so the phrase can't split mid-word, while the `" · "` separators around them can still wrap normally.
- Home's next-practice hero card gained a "Up Next" label (`clbl`) above it, distinguishing it from the "Upcoming Practices" list further down. The unplanned-state "Plan Practice"/"Not planned yet" button was still sized `.bxl` (a leftover from before the planned-state buttons below it were deliberately downsized to `.blg` in the sixteenth session to fix a phone-width overflow) -- now `.blg` too, matching every other button on the card.
- `AbsencePicker.jsx`'s "Mark out for..." step capped candidate practices to a 14-day window (`p.date <= in14Str`); removed entirely, so any scheduled, non-cancelled practice for the player's team is selectable, however far out. Covers both entry paths (`PracticeDetail`'s "Who's out?" and `PlayerProfile`'s "Mark Out For...") since both already route through this same component.
- Skill-tag picker (`SkillTagPicker`, `ModalLayer.jsx`): its search overlay is a `position:fixed` bottom sheet, and on mobile `100dvh` doesn't reliably shrink for fixed content when the on-screen keyboard opens (browser-dependent), so search results could end up partly or fully hidden behind the keyboard. Added a `useVisualViewportHeight()` hook (tracks `window.visualViewport`'s `resize` event) and sized the sheet's `height`/`max-height` to it instead of the default full-inset `.movly`. Not device-tested in this environment -- the mechanism is the standard fix for this exact class of bug, but flag it if it's not quite right on a real phone. Also added a gray explainer line under the "Skill Tags" field label ("Tagging skills helps power Goals & Insights and reporting on what your team actually practices"), no em dash.
- Drill add/edit form (`ModalLayer.jsx`): the equipment/gear "Add" inputs are plain uncontrolled DOM inputs (`new-equip-inp`/`new-gear-inp`), read only by their own Add button -- typing a name and tapping Save without ever clicking Add silently discarded the text with no linked equipment and no warning. `save()` now checks both inputs' live DOM values right before the `addActivity`/`editActivity` branches and blocks the save with an inline error if either has unsubmitted text.
- Sign-in screen ("Welcome, Coach"): removed the em dash from "Enter your email — we'll send you a sign-in code." → "Enter your email. We'll send you a sign-in code."
- Library's Drills tab: the My/Org Drills vs. "Team Libraries" pill (introduced the previous session's restructuring) relabeled to "Explore" per direct feedback.
- New-drill Sport default and ordering (`ModalLayer.jsx`): `defaultSport()` previously always preferred the most-recently-added library drill's sport whenever the library was non-empty, only falling back to the coach's own team sport if the library was completely empty -- so a single-sport coach whose library happened to contain a stray other-sport or General drill wouldn't reliably get their own sport as the default. Now a coach with exactly one team sport always gets that sport, full stop; a multi-sport (or team-less) coach still gets their last-added drill's sport, same as before. The Sport `<select>`'s option order (shared by the add and edit drill forms) is now the coach's own team sports first, then the rest of the full sport list in its existing order, via a new `orderedSports` array -- previously a flat, unordered `SPORTS` list regardless of what the coach actually coaches.


### Seventeenth session continued (2026-07-30) — Phase 2 org rework begins: org_create_team head-coach removal + canManageTeamInMode retrofit
Direct continuation of the seventeenth session's deferred org/library/equipment restructuring, scoped via 4 clarifying questions before starting: (1) include the `org_create_team` auto-head-coach removal, retrofitting all six affected screens rather than leaving it deferred again; (2) multi-org library switching reuses Home's existing org picker rather than a new UI; (3) org-owned equipment at a matching location auto-merges into a coach's available equipment when building/running a practice, rather than requiring a manual pull; (4) adding a team inside a multi-sport org restricts/defaults its sport to the org's own sports.

First piece landed: `org_create_team`'s auto-insert of the creating director as `team_staff` head_coach is removed (`20260730060000_org_create_team_no_auto_head_coach.sql`), and `canManageTeamInMode(team, coachId, mode)` — which already existed and already did the right mode-aware thing, but was only wired into `HomeScreen.jsx` — replaced bare `isHeadCoach`/`myTeamRole` checks in the six screens that had been blocking this removal: `RostersTab`, `GoalsScreen`, `PracticeDetail`, `ScheduleScreen`, `SeriesWizard`, `SchedulePracticeModal`. One naming collision surfaced during the retrofit: `ScheduleScreen` already had an unrelated local `mode` state (agenda/month view toggle); resolved by destructuring the incoming Coach/Org mode prop as `mode: appMode` and using `appMode` for all permission checks, leaving the view-toggle `mode`/`setMode` untouched.

Verified live in two passes. First, a scripted RPC-level check (fresh throwaway director → new org → `org_create_team` → confirmed zero `team_staff` rows for the new team → confirmed the director could still `insert into players` as an org admin with no personal staff row, i.e. RLS's `can_manage_team` org-admin branch was already correct and only the client-side gate needed fixing). Second, a full browser click-test with a real minted session injected into `localStorage`: switched to Organization Mode, opened the new org team, and confirmed Schedule showed "+ Practice"/"+ Series" (not the empty/no-permission state), Roster showed "+ Add" and "No players yet — tap + Add" (not the bare "No players yet." with no add affordance), and Goals & Insights rendered as a fully editable, unlocked goal-setting form — all despite the director having no `team_staff` row on that team.

**Incidental discovery while doing that live click-test**: reloading the page while in Organization Mode silently reverts to Coach Mode. Root cause: `App.jsx`'s guard effect that resets a stale persisted org selection (`if(mode.type==="org"&&library.myOrgs&&!library.myOrgs.some(...))setMode({type:"coach"})`) can fire on a fresh page load before `library.myOrgs` has actually finished its first fetch — an empty-but-already-truthy array satisfies `library.myOrgs&&...` and wrongly looks like "this org doesn't exist," resetting mode to Coach and then persisting that wrong value immediately via the adjacent `rop_mode`-writing effect. Not fixed yet (out of scope for this retrofit) — see Known Gaps.

**Second piece, same continued session: org multi-sport.** `organizations.sport` (single nullable text, 2026-07-22) replaced with `organizations.sports text[] not null default '{}'` (`20260730070000_org_multi_sport.sql`) rather than adding a parallel column — existing single values backfilled into one-element arrays first, then the old column dropped, so there's exactly one source of truth for an org's sports going forward (this project already paid once for a stale-parallel-column bug, on `team_goals`/`get_team_goal_report`, and it wasn't worth risking twice). Org Details' Sport dropdown became a multi-select chip row; adding a team inside an org with sports configured now restricts and defaults the new team's Sport field to just those sports (falls back to the old unrestricted full list for an org with none configured yet). Verified live end-to-end: created a throwaway two-sport org (Basketball, Soccer) via a real authenticated session, confirmed the Teams list showed "Basketball, Soccer," confirmed +Team's Sport dropdown contained exactly those two options with Basketball defaulted, then opened Org Details' edit form, added Baseball as a third sport via the chip toggle, saved, and confirmed the persisted read-back showed "Basketball, Soccer, Baseball."

**Third piece, same continued session: org equipment-at-location auto-merge, and a real leak found along the way.** Investigating how to merge org equipment into a coach's practice-building equipment picker turned up an existing, unrelated-to-this-feature bug: `ActConfig`/`StationConfig` (`ActivityConfigs.jsx`) filtered the equipment picker by sport and location only, with no owner/org check at all — under RLS that meant *every* asset for any org a coach belonged to showed up in every practice's equipment picker regardless of team or location, the exact same class of leak already fixed once this session in `EquipmentTab`'s Team-tab view (`visible Equipment`'s owner/org scoping, "9 baseball items already there, cones twice"). Fixed with a shared `ownedOrOrgAtLoc` helper: a coach's own equipment always shows; an org's equipment only merges in when this specific team belongs to that org *and* the asset is tagged to the activity's own location (an untagged org asset doesn't merge in the way a coach's own untagged equipment does — merging on "no location info" felt too permissive for equipment a coach doesn't own). Verified live: scheduled a practice for an org team at a "Club Gym" location, added a station, and confirmed an org-owned "Club Cones" asset tagged to that location appeared in the picker while a second org asset tagged to a different location did not.

**Fourth and final piece, same continued session: the Library restructuring** (`org_create_team`/`canManageTeamInMode`, org multi-sport, and the equipment auto-merge above were the three foundational, more schema-heavy pieces; this closed out the rest of the queued Phase 2 list in one pass, since all four remaining items — Explore-under-Drills, the per-org shelf becoming per-coach, Club Library's color, and dropping the per-team Equipment tab — touch the same one screen). The 5-tab content-type row (`NewLibraryScreen.jsx`) is unconditionally visible now instead of hiding behind the old page-wide My-Library-vs-Explore pill; that pill moved down into just the Drills tab as "My/Org Drills" vs. "Team Libraries." The old flat "From {org}" shelf became one shelf per coach who's shared a drill into that org, with a real gap found and fixed along the way: the first attempt resolved names via `data.profilesById`, which turned out to only ever resolve an org_staff co-member's name (`profiles_select_org_co_member`'s actual RLS condition) — a coach who's merely `team_staff` on one of the org's teams (verified live to be exactly what `can_share_drill_to_org` itself requires as the minimum bar to share at all) resolved to nothing but "A coach." Fixed by falling back to `team_staff`'s own stored first/last name (already fetched into every team's `coaches` array, no extra query needed) ahead of giving up. Club Library also picked up a colored accent bar under its title using the org's own color (same "so it looks like their org" treatment Layout.jsx's bottom tab bar already had), and the team workspace's Equipment tab is gone entirely in Org mode (`Layout.jsx`'s `teamWorkspaceTabs`, plus a redirect-away guard on `TeamEquipmentRoute` for a stale direct link) since equipment is now managed centrally from Club Library instead. `copyDrillToMyLibrary` also became mode-aware: copying while in Org mode now writes to the org's library (organization-owned, resolving equipment against the org's own asset pool) instead of always landing in the copier's personal library regardless of which mode they were actually working in. Verified live end-to-end with a real two-coach org: the per-coach shelf showed "CoachJane Smith (1)," opening it showed her shared drill with a "Copy to Org Library" button (mode-aware label), and clicking it produced a row with `organization_id` set and `owner_user_id` null, visible back on the Org Drills shelf with "From A coach" attribution. Test data (2 throwaway accounts, 1 org) fully torn down afterward.


### Seventeenth session (2026-07-30) — landing-page batch, live audio/notes UX, then a ~40-item backlog
First half of the session: a batch of landing-page and live-session requests, handled before the much larger backlog below.
