Landing page FAQ answers updated (schedule-adjustment wording, "different sports" now opens with "Yes"), the Practice History section's copy tightened and its mockup replaced (real drills with planned-vs-actual minutes and two notes tied to specific drills, one tagging a player by first name, instead of a made-up "88 min wall time"), the Practice Builder mockup updated to show the real app's `x/y min` under-planned pill instead of a flat total, and a stray parenthetical removed from the "smooth practice starts" copy. In the app itself: a one-time "Turn On Audio?" popup now shows for both the coach's `CommandScreen` and the anonymous `HelperView` the moment either lands in a live session (audio defaults off and was easy to never discover otherwise); the anonymous helper's note composer replaced its "End-of-practice" checkbox with a two-option "General"/current-drill toggle; Library's "+ Build Practice" now offers a real choice between an unscheduled build and picking an upcoming scheduled practice (a new `SchedulePracticePicker`, agenda/month view) instead of always jumping straight into an empty unscheduled plan; and Goals & Insights' History list gained a red dot for any session with a note nobody's reviewed yet plus a "Mark all as viewed" action, backed by a new `notes.viewed_at` column and an extended `get_team_session_history` (`20260730020000_note_review_tracking.sql`) — reviewing is marked automatically the moment the head coach opens that session's detail.

Second half: Jax dropped a large, unstructured list covering landing-page copy/visuals, live-practice audio/notes UX, and a wide batch of bug fixes and UX polish across scheduling, equipment, goals, onboarding, and auth, plus a genuinely large org/library/equipment restructuring mixed into the same message. Given the size, ran a parallel investigation pass first (4 Explore agents covering org/team model, equipment/library architecture, builder/scheduling UX, and settings/auth/onboarding) before writing any code, then asked 4 clarifying questions rather than guessing on the consequential/irreversible ones. Confirmed answers: (1) org creation becomes a consultation request for regular coaches, with a real admin-only create-org-and-hand-to-a-director flow; (2) the org/library/equipment restructuring gets its own dedicated future pass — everything else (this session) goes first; (3) the getting-started checklist becomes a persistent, clickable Home card, not just a popup, and the green dot goes away; (4) Goals gets both a disabled Save at non-100% *and* an unsaved-changes leave warning.

**Real bugs found and fixed, not just requested UX changes:**
- **Leave-team leak**: the global `/schedule` route (`ScheduleLegacyRoute`, `App.jsx`) passed raw unscoped `data.teams`/`data.practices` straight through, unlike `HomeRoute`/`TeamsRoute` which already scope via `teamsForMode`/`homeTeamsForMode`. A team a coach had genuinely left (their own `team_staff` row archived) stayed visible as a filterable schedule option and kept showing its practices — not an RLS bug (`can_access_team`'s org-membership branch correctly keeps a team visible to org staff independent of personal `team_staff` rows, by design, for org oversight), just a missing client-side scope. Fixed by scoping `ScheduleLegacyRoute` the same way; confirmed a plain owned-team deletion already worked correctly via a completely different path (`archived_at`), which is why only the org-team leave case was ever reported.
- **Equipment duplication/leak**: `EquipmentTab`'s `sportFilter` path (the Team tab's Equipment view) filtered `data.assets` by sport only, no owner/org check — leaking in Public Library catalog equipment (RLS deliberately makes `source_catalog_id` rows visible to every coach) plus, in principle, any other coach's owned assets sharing a sport. This is exactly what produced "9 baseball items already there, cones twice" for a coach/org that added none. Fixed by reusing the Library tab's own `visibleEquipment()` scoping (own/org, catalog excluded), narrowed to the one team's sport; `TeamEquipmentRoute` needed `mode` threaded through, which it wasn't receiving.
- **New-signup email was Supabase's stock unbranded template, confirmed live via `supabase config push`'s diff output**: `enable_confirmations=true` routes a brand-new email's *first* `signInWithOtp()` call through the `confirmation` mail type, not `magic_link` — only `magic_link` had ever been customized (branded, code-based via `{{ .Token }}`), so new signups got Supabase's default "Confirm your email address" link-based email instead of the code the app's own UI tells them to expect. Added a matching branded `confirmation.html` template and pushed config; the diff Supabase printed before applying (`content = "<h2>Confirm your email address</h2>..."`) is direct confirmation this was the actual live bug, not a guess.
- **Disabled buttons had no visual disabled state anywhere in the app**: `.btn` and every color variant (`.primary`, `.outline`, ...) set a solid background/color with no `:disabled` override. This directly explains the reported Goals confusion — Save was *already* correctly gated on totals summing to exactly 100% (`canSave`), but looked pixel-identical whether enabled or not, so a coach could tap it, watch nothing happen, and have no idea why. Added a single global `.btn:disabled{opacity:.4;cursor:not-allowed;}` rather than a Goals-specific fix, since the same invisible-disabled-state gap existed on every other disabled button in the app.
- **Builder margin gap**: the sticky action-bar header (border-bottom, no margin of its own) sat completely flush against the "Practice Setup" card's top edge with zero space between them, on every entry path (Home hero, PracticeDetail Edit, Library +Build Practice) — not a difference *between* paths as first suspected, but one consistent gap present regardless of how Builder is reached, matching Jax's own "any screen" framing once re-read carefully. Fixed with a small `marginTop` on that first card. Not visually confirmed against the real thing (no login in this environment) — flag if it's not quite the issue seen live.

**Also done:** `organizations_select_member` RLS relaxed so any team member (not just `org_staff`) can read their own org's name (org name now shown on Home/Teams team cards); added-to-team banner redesigned to "Accept"/"Manage Team Assignments" instead of a raw "Leave" link; Builder's drill-add list now excludes Public Library/catalog drills (was showing them mixed into "your own" library); inline add-location dialogs (with optional sublocations) added to both Builder and the quick-schedule modal for a coach with zero locations; SeriesWizard collapsed from 5 sequential screens to 2; Schedule's empty state now prompts to create a team; bottom-nav active-tab underline plus a persistent team-workspace back-to-Teams button; Coach/Org toggle relabeled "Coach Mode"/"Organization Mode"; Settings' redundant per-org "switch to Org mode" rows removed (Home's own toggle already covers it); NameScreen reworded (asterisk convention, dropped subtext); Deactivate Account section un-scared (was "Danger Zone," new confirm copy verbatim per Jax's wording) and reactivation is no longer silent (a real prompt: reactivate or exit); Terms/Privacy's back button now returns to Settings > Account (was hardcoded to Home) via `location.state.openSection`, the same mechanism the added-to-team banner's "Manage Team Assignments" link uses to deep-link into a specific Settings section; new `/faq` page (natural voice, no em dashes, covers account deletion and multi-team org setup) replacing "Getting Started" in the "?" menu now that the checklist lives on Home itself.

**Deliberately not done:** `org_create_team`'s auto-head-coach insert (see Known Gaps) — asked for, investigated, found to be load-bearing across six other screens' permission gating, and left alone rather than risk locking directors out of their own teams under time pressure with no way to test live. The full org/library/equipment restructuring bundled into the same original message was scoped as its own future pass per direct confirmation, not attempted here.

Every migration (`20260730020000_note_review_tracking.sql` from earlier in the session, `20260730030000_org_name_visible_to_team_members.sql`) and the `confirmation.html` config push were applied directly to production via `supabase db push`/`config push` — no local Supabase environment in this setup, same as always. At the time this batch shipped, nothing had been click-tested (no login credentials available yet in this environment) — flagged explicitly as a gap, with a follow-up recommended before relying on any of it. See the next entry for what happened once that gap was actually closed, same session.

**Real live-browser verification, same session, immediately after.** Jax asked for a login credential rather than leaving everything code-review-only. Talked through why a credential shouldn't live in this file (or anywhere git-tracked — permanent in history, readable by anyone with repo access) or even in the chat transcript itself, and landed on the safer version: a `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` in a local `.env` (already gitignored), used only to mint disposable test sessions via the Admin API, never to touch real user data. Verified the whole mechanism end-to-end before relying on it (create a throwaway user, mint a session, confirm it authenticates as that user, delete it) — then actually used it: real director + coach + org + org-team + personal-team test accounts, injected into the Browser pane by writing the minted session tokens directly into `localStorage` (`sb-<project-ref>-auth-token`), clicking through the real signed-in app against production via a local dev server (same Supabase backend either way).

Confirmed working, live, not just in code: the leave-team fix (left an org team, watched it vanish from both the Schedule filter and Teams tab), the equipment dedup fix (confirmed real catalog items like "Basketballs"/"Cones"/"Pinnies" exist for Basketball but correctly don't leak into Team Equipment), the Builder drill-scope fix (confirmed real catalog drills exist but don't appear in Add Drills), both location dialogs, the Builder margin fix on both entry paths, the Library Build-Practice picker (including creating a real 17-practice series via the now-2-step SeriesWizard and picking one from the scheduled-practice picker), Goals' numeric input + visibly-grayed disabled Save + the leave-confirm dialog (fired with the exact right message), the persistent Getting Started card, the trimmed "?" menu, the FAQ page, the added-to-team banner, org name display, the bottom-nav underline, the team-workspace back button, Settings' copy changes, and the full deactivate → sign back in → reactivation-prompt → reactivate flow. One thing from the prior session's own list didn't reproduce (Library's title reads "Library," not "My Library," in Coach mode, contrary to an earlier report) — noted as possibly stale rather than "fixed."

This pass also caught a real bug incidentally: `get_team_goal_report` erroring on every call (see the Goals & Insights entry above for the fix) — something no amount of code review would have surfaced, only an actual RPC call against production did.

**Cleanup, a real gotcha, and a real bug it led to (fixed same session, in a follow-up turn).** All disposable test data was torn down — teams/orgs archived (`archived_at`; `organizations` has no delete grant for any role and no app-level "delete an org" feature at all, so archiving is the only option even for cleanup), then the underlying auth users deleted via the Admin API, cascading through real `on delete cascade` chains (`profiles` → `teams.owner_user_id` → practices/team_staff/etc.) once the non-cascading `organizations.created_by` reference is cleared first. One of five test accounts — the one with a real recurring series attached — repeatedly failed hard-deletion via `admin.deleteUser()` with an opaque `AuthRetryableFetchError` / empty `{}` message. The JS SDK was swallowing the real error; calling the raw REST endpoint directly (`DELETE /auth/v1/admin/users/{id}`) surfaced it: `practice_series_created_by_fkey` — **`practice_series.created_by` was `not null references profiles(id)` with no `ON DELETE` action at all**, unlike every other actor-identity column in this schema (see `notes.created_by`'s established nullable + `ON DELETE SET NULL` pattern, "historical practice truth that should outlive the author's account"). This is very likely a real piece of the already-documented "hard account deletion blocked by an FK-cascade ordering issue" gap below — *any* real coach who ever created a recurring schedule would hit this same block if they asked for account deletion. Fixed properly (`20260730050000_practice_series_created_by_actor_fk.sql`: dropped the `NOT NULL`, re-added the FK with `ON DELETE SET NULL`) rather than just working around it for cleanup purposes — verified by retrying the hard delete, which then fully succeeded (profile, team, and all associated data physically gone, confirmed by direct query). A `shouldSoftDelete: true` retry earlier in the process had reported success while actually just anonymizing/banning the account with zero cascade — worth remembering that `deleteUser()`'s soft-delete path can look identical to a real success in its response shape.


### Sixteenth session continued (2026-07-30) — PDF branding/access, presence, Setup nav, audio settings
- **Home hero button overflow, fixed**: the Practice Setup / Start Practice buttons (added the prior session) overflowed off the viewport on phone-width screens. Root cause: both were `.bxl` (nowrap text, large padding) inside `flex:1` with no `min-width:0` — flex items default to `min-width:auto`, so neither could actually shrink below its own text width and the row overflowed instead of the two buttons splitting the space evenly. Fixed by sizing down to `.blg` and adding `minWidth:0` to both. Verified at 375px.
- **Practice Setup screen gained navigation**: a "Back to Home" link and a persistent "Start Practice" bar, both `can_manage`-only — previously a coach reaching Practice Setup from Home had no way back except the browser's own Back button, and no way to start the run from that screen at all. Needed `practice_id` added to `get_preview_view`'s output.
- **"Who has this open" presence**: a small badge on `PreviewView`, the staff `CommandScreen`, and `HelperView` showing which coaches currently have a practice's setup/live view open and how many anonymous viewers there are. Pure Supabase Realtime Presence (`subscribeToPracticePresence`, `src/supabase.js`) — no new table, no RLS, ephemeral client-to-client state over the same public-channel websocket infra `subscribeToLiveSession` already uses. Keyed by `practice_id` (not `live_session_id`) so a coach in setup and a helper already on the live link show up together — needed `practice_id` added to `get_live_session_view` too. Verified end-to-end with two real anonymous browser tabs on the same link: the badge correctly showed "2 people watching" and dropped back to "1" the instant the second tab closed.
- **PDF export, branded and made permanent**: `PracticePlanPrint.jsx` gained a small letterhead (app icon + "Run of Practice" wordmark) and the eyebrow line now reads "THE RUN OF PRACTICE FOR {team} ON {date}" instead of a bare "PRACTICE PLAN" — so a printed sheet handed to a parent or another coach is recognizably from the app. More importantly, **the Print/Export PDF button only ever existed on `PracticeDetail`, which a past/already-run practice never routes to** — `ScheduleScreen.jsx`'s own routing comment says so explicitly ("Any past/planned practice... goes to HistoryViewer"). Added the same button (reusing the same component, same data each screen already has) to `HistoryViewer` (`CommandScreen.jsx`, reached via Schedule/Calendar → a past date) and `SessionHistoryDetail` (`GoalsScreen.jsx`, Goals & Insights → History tab) — the two places Jax's own question ("calendar day and history from that page?") correctly guessed at. `SessionHistoryDetail` needed a new `data` prop threaded through from `GoalsScreen` (it never received the app's full `data` before, only `session`/`practice`/`team`).
- **Live Practice Audio settings** (Settings screen, new "Live Practice Audio" nav item): after several rounds of chasing one "right" whistle sound, made both the time's-up sound (Whistle/Buzzer/Ding/Beep) and the announcer voice coach-selectable instead, each with tap-to-preview. Two new synthesized files (`ding.wav` — a bell-like chime, `beep.wav` — a clean two-tone alert) reuse assets from the earlier "3 alternatives" detour that got rejected as the *default* but work fine as *options*; `gym-buzzer.wav` (unused since the original whistle swap) becomes the Buzzer option. Stored in `localStorage`, not synced server-side, since voice availability is inherently per-device. Voice picking started as a Male/Female heuristic, then got replaced same-day with a full list of every installed voice (see Session Log, "Whistle re-synthesis" v6) after the heuristic's first pick sounded bad.


### Sixteenth session (2026-07-29) — batch of 10 feature/UI requests
- Jax gave 10 requests in one message; working through them in order of complexity, verifying each against production before moving on rather than batching all 10 into one commit at the end.
- **Helper-view skill tags + player focus (`/live/:token`)**: the anonymous helper view never surfaced a drill's skill tags or a player's per-category focus notes, even though the staff-side `CommandScreen` already had both (`tagNamesForLibraryId`/`noteForPlayerAtDrill`). Since the anonymous view has no client-side `activityLibrary`/`skillTags`/`players.focusAreas` to work with, this had to be resolved server-side: extended `get_live_session_view` (new migration `20260729000000_helper_view_skill_tags_focus.sql`) to add `skill_tags` (name array) and `player_focus` (`{player_id: note}` map) to both `current_activity` (via `practice_activities.library_activity_id`) and each `stations[]` entry (via that station's *own* `library_activity_id` — different stations in the same rotation can run different drills with different focus areas). Matching is by `category_id` only, not `skill_tag_id`, mirroring the staff-side logic exactly and the 20260720000000 category-level migration. Verified against a real production session (a basketball "5-on-4 to 5-on-5" drill) before applying — real skill tags and real per-player notes resolved correctly. `HelperPlayerChip` became a real interactive button (was a static, non-functional "tap to focus" hint) that toggles the player's note inline; added a shared `HelperSkillTags` tag-row component reused across the plain-drill, station-intro, station-overview, and station-detail render paths.
- Landing page copy: hero reverted to "Plan the practice. Run it live. Keep everyone aligned.", the Live Adjustments & Timers paragraph rewritten per Jax's exact wording, the Transition Support mockup (`ROTATION_MOVES`) reordered to Location → Coach → Drill (matching the real app change below), and "Ava's Dad (helper)" renamed to a normal coach name in the Pre-Practice Setup mockup — all just mock-data/copy edits, no schema changes.
- Live rotation/transition screen (`CommandScreen.jsx`, both the real `CommandScreen` and `HelperView` transition-card labels): reordered from "Drill Name - Coach - Location" to "Location - Coach - Drill Name" to match what a coach actually needs first when hustling players between stations.
- Buzzer → whistle: `gym-buzzer.wav` swapped for a synthesized `whistle.wav` (no real whistle sample existed in the repo; downloading one would need explicit permission per the file-download policy, so generated one via a throwaway Node script — two frequency-modulated ~3kHz blasts, 16-bit PCM mono). **Re-synthesized once already** (still same session, see below) after Jax asked for something more authentic.
- The remaining 3 items from the same 10-item batch were completed in follow-up turns of this same session:
  - **Library publisher filter**: `activity_library` gained three nullable "copied from" columns (`copied_from_owner_user_id`/`organization_id`/`catalog_id`), set once by `copyDrillToMyLibrary` at copy time — previously a copied drill was indistinguishable from a self-authored one the moment it landed in "My Library." `NewLibraryScreen.jsx`'s "mine" shelf now shows a "From {publisher}" line per drill and a Publisher section in the existing Filter modal.
  - **Station leader assignment**: `get_preview_view` now returns `can_manage` (computed server-side off `auth.uid()` via `can_coach_team`, since the anonymous share link and the signed-in coach's own "Practice Setup View" hit the identical `/preview/:token` route/component) and, only for a manager, the team's coach roster and each station's real id — `PreviewView` shows "No leader assigned -- tap to assign" per station plus a "Share Setup Link" button. Separately, the live `CommandScreen` now warns once, right after attendance, if any station_block station still lacks a leader (assign/share/leave-unmanned). Home's hero presents "Practice Setup" and "Review Plan"/"Start Practice" as co-equal side-by-side buttons instead of Setup being a small link underneath.
  - **Practice plan PDF export**: a "Print / Export PDF" button on Practice Detail renders `PracticePlanPrint.jsx`, a standalone print-optimized document (not a screenshot of the app UI), handed off to the browser's native Print/Save-as-PDF via a new `#rop-print-root` + `@media print` block in App.jsx's `CSS` constant — no client-side PDF library, no server round trip. Shows duration *and* a computed clock start/end range per drill (anchored to the practice's start time, reusing the same `actMins()` formula already shown on-screen), plus description/coaching focus/skill tags/equipment/grouping/per-player focus notes, same treatment per station in a station block.
- **Whistle re-synthesis, four rounds** (still 2026-07-29, a chain of same-day follow-ups): Jax asked for the whistle to sound like an authentic basketball-referee whistle in a loud arena, not just "a whistle sound." Every round was sent directly to Jax to listen to first, since audio can only be verified here technically (valid WAV, no clipping, correct duration) — never by ear.
  - v2: modeled on a Fox 40-style *pealess* whistle — three close, non-harmonic carriers (2850/3020/3160 Hz) whose interference/beating is what makes a real pealess whistle harsh and able to cut through crowd noise (a single clean tone reads as a toy). Added filtered breath-turbulence noise, breath-pressure sag, and a slight tremolo; one long (~1s) hard blast.
  - v3: "too much breath" — pulled the noise/turbulence way down and removed the sag/tremolo (closer to a modern *electronic* whistle, which is a battery-driven tone generator, not blown, so it doesn't carry breath noise or fade). Changed pattern to a quarter-second blast, brief pause, half-second blast (a double-blow), per spec.
  - A detour: Jax asked for 3 non-whistle alternatives in case something less "sports" would be less obnoxious if several coaches' phones played it in sync — a bell-like chime (inharmonic decaying partials, a falling major third), a clean two-tone digital alert (single frequencies, no beating), and a rising chirp (urgency via pitch contour, short/percussive). All three were built and sent, but rejected — the whistle direction was preferred after all.
  - v4: single ~0.5s blast (dropped the double-blow) followed by a spoken **"Time"** once the blast ends, wired via `whistleAudioRef.current.onended` calling the existing `speak()` (`speechSynthesis`) function — reuses the exact mechanism already used for "Two minutes remaining," not a second audio system. The spoken word carries the "second blast"'s job now, so one clean whistle hit is enough.
  - v5 (2026-07-30, next session): rather than keep guessing at one "right" sound/voice, made both coach-selectable in Settings instead. See "Live Practice Audio settings" above.
  - v6 (2026-07-30, same day, a follow-up): the Male/Female gender-heuristic picker's first pick ("Daniel") sounded bad — replaced entirely with a full voice-list picker (every installed voice, previewable) rather than trying to fix the heuristic, since there's no reliable way to infer voice quality from a name. See "Live Practice Audio settings" above.
  - v7 (2026-07-30, same day, session-closing follow-up): even the full device voice list was "simply awful" per Jax, aside from Samantha and Daniel. Rather than keep hunting for a better synthetic voice, agreed the real fix is recorded human audio for the fixed phrases (`"Two minutes remaining."`, `"Time"`, `"Next up!"`, `"Audio on"` — no dynamic text is ever spoken, so this is fully feasible as static clips) — Jax is arranging recordings (own voice and/or others) to send as `.wav`/`.mp3` files. In the meantime, narrowed the voice picker's list down to just Samantha/Daniel (`CURATED_VOICE_NAMES`) so the coach isn't wading through two dozen novelty voices for no reason.


### Fifteenth session (2026-07-28) — a real regression from the thirteenth session's dead-code sweep
- **`fetchLibraryData()` has been throwing on every single call since the thirteenth session, silently emptying `assets`/`skillTags`/`activityLibrary`/`catalogs`/`myOrgs`/`pendingOrgInvites`.** Reported by the user as "I lost the Coach/Org toggle" — the toggle is genuinely the most visible symptom (`HomeScreen.jsx` only renders it when `data.myOrgs.length > 0`), but the actual blast radius was the entire `library` state, since one failed fetch inside `fetchLibraryData()` fails the whole function.
- Root cause: the thirteenth session's dead-code sweep deleted `fetchPendingOrgInvites` from `supabase.js`, having concluded it was unreferenced. The verification grep at the time was `grep -rn "\b$fn\b" src --include="*.jsx" --include="*.js" | grep -v "src/supabase.js:"` — deliberately excluding matches *within* `supabase.js` itself, to avoid a function's own `console.error('functionName:', ...)` line counting as a false-positive "usage." That same exclusion also hid the one real call site: `fetchLibraryData()` (same file) calls `fetchPendingOrgInvites()` directly. The other 8 functions removed in that same pass were re-checked this session and are confirmed to have zero references anywhere, including within `supabase.js` — this was a one-function miss, not a systemic pattern, but it's exactly the kind of miss that same-file exclusion in a grep will reliably produce, worth remembering next time a "same-file usage" filter feels like the right way to reduce grep noise.
- This also retroactively explains part of the earlier "production hang" incident (fourteenth-session-adjacent, actually thirteenth-into-fourteenth): `fetchLibraryData()` was *already* broken by the time the `Promise.all([refreshTeams(),refreshLibrary(),refreshPlanning()])` loading gate was introduced, so `refreshLibrary()` was rejecting on every load from the start. Switching to `Promise.allSettled` correctly fixed the *hang* (nothing should block the whole app on one fetch failing), but it also means the broken fetch's failure became silent instead of loud — the app loaded fine, just with empty library data, and nothing surfaced that until a coach who actually uses Org mode noticed the toggle was gone.
- Fixed by restoring `fetchPendingOrgInvites` verbatim from git history (`git show acfc8cf~1:src/supabase.js`), same implementation, same location, same comments. Verified the restored query directly against production (`SET LOCAL ROLE authenticated` + the real coach's JWT claims, rolled back) before considering this done, not just a clean `npm run build`.
- Lesson: a "verified via whole-repo grep" claim is only as good as what the grep pattern excludes. A function's *own* file is not a safe exclusion zone for "is this called anywhere" — its own other functions are exactly where an internal helper is most likely to be called from.

- **Auth email rate limit raised, and a real gap in what "Resend" actually buys you clarified.** Jax hit "email rate limit exceeded" on the sign-in screen and asked why, assuming Resend (custom SMTP) meant no rate limiting applied. It doesn't work that way: `auth.rate_limit.email_sent` (in `supabase/config.toml`) is the limit that specifically *governs* custom-SMTP-sent emails — its own comment says as much ("Requires auth.email.smtp to be enabled") — custom SMTP changes *which* limit applies, not whether one does. It was still at Supabase's default of 2/hour, trivially easy to exceed while testing. Raised to 30/hour (matching the file's other per-project defaults) and pushed via `supabase config push --yes`.

  While answering "what's the actual daily cap," traced every email-sending path in the app: Supabase Auth OTP emails are the only thing `email_sent` governs; the two Edge Functions (`notify-team-staff-added`, `notify-org-invite`) call Resend's API directly and aren't subject to that Supabase setting at all. A code comment in `_shared/emailTemplate.ts` confirms the project is on Resend's *free tier* — which almost certainly makes Resend's own account-wide plan cap (not anything in this app's config) the actual binding constraint across all email types combined. Flagged as directional, not verified (no access to the live Resend dashboard from here) — worth Jax confirming the real number there rather than trusting either Supabase limit as the ceiling.

  Before pushing the config change, checked the one thing this file already warns about: `config push` unconditionally overwrites `site_url`/`additional_redirect_urls` to whatever's in the local file, regardless of what else is being changed — confirmed both were still correct production values (not stale localhost/dev placeholders) before pushing. Also learned something new worth recording: `config push`'s output reports every service (`api`, `db.settings`, `auth`, `storage`) as `up_to_date` on a **successful** push, not just on a no-op — the wording can't be used to distinguish "nothing changed" from "change applied successfully." Don't read `up_to_date` as "the push did nothing."


### Fourteenth session (2026-07-28) — Assistant Coach handoff (visibility, live-session control, notes)
- Worked from `ROP-AssistantCoach-Handoff.md` (a fresh spec doc, companion to this file). Before writing anything, checked what the spec assumed against what actually existed — this caught that roughly half of §1 (assistant visibility + read-only plan view) was **already fully built**: `PracticeDetail.jsx` already gated Edit/Plan/Cancel behind `isHeadCoach` while leaving Run available to everyone (exactly the "read-only plan, full run access" ask), and Settings → My Team Assignments already showed every team + role + a `show_on_home` toggle for every coach, not gated to Org mode as the spec assumed. Only two genuinely new pieces got built for §1: a role label on Home's team cards, and a one-tap "Practice Setup View" button that reuses the existing `/preview/:token` page (via `findOrCreatePreviewToken`) rather than building a second equipment-by-location view.
- **§1.3 (live-session control)**: the confirmed strict-single-controller model was already shipped almost exactly as specified (`isController` already gated every action, a non-controller already saw a "Take Control" button) — except mid-run plan editing (`LiveEditBuilder`) was gated on `isController` alone, with no head-coach check, contradicting the one explicit exception the spec called out ("stays gated on role, not on control"). Fixed (`isController && amHeadCoach`). Also rebuilt the take-control affordance: moved from a top banner with a bold primary button into the exact spot where advance/±1min/timer controls normally render, styled deliberately low-emphasis (outline) — the spec's own reasoning was that a bold button in that specific tap zone risks an accidental control-seizure mid-drill, since that's where a thumb lands out of habit. Added a non-blocking toast for whoever just lost control (recommended default in the spec, not asked-for-then-confirmed, but adopted since the doc explicitly invited flagging if unwanted and none came).
- **§2 (notes) had a real near-miss worth remembering**: the spec's own §0.1 said "notes were built once and removed... this is a new system, not a resurrection," referring to the old `NotesTab`/`app_data`-blob notes deleted two sessions ago. That framing was correct about *that* system but incomplete — a **different, currently-live** `notes` table (added 2026-07-14, already wired into the live CommandScreen's quick-note/end-of-practice UI and into both `HistoryViewer` and `SessionHistoryDetail`'s read paths) covers almost the entire staff side of this spec already. This wasn't caught until *after* a full parallel system (`session_notes`/`note_player_tags` tables, `create_session_note`/`submit_session_note_by_token` RPCs, a new composer UI) was designed, migrated to production, and verified working — only surfaced while wiring up `SessionHistoryDetail` and noticing `fetchNotesForPractice` already existed and was already imported there. Root cause: trusted the spec's framing about "no existing notes system" without independently checking for one under a different name, the same class of mistake this file's own Session Log has flagged before (thirteenth session, doc-vs-code drift) — should have grepped for any table already shaped like this before designing a new one, not after.
  
  Corrected same-day, before real usage: a follow-up migration dropped the parallel `session_notes`/`note_player_tags`/both RPCs entirely (zero rows had ever been written to them outside rolled-back test transactions) and extended the **real** `notes` table instead — `author_kind`/`author_label`/`submitted_via_token_id` columns (nullable/defaulted, safe since the table had zero rows), a `note_player_tags` join table keyed on `notes(id)`, an archive UPDATE policy (the table had never had one — "quick-capture-only" per its original 2026-07-14 comment), and one new anonymous RPC (`submit_practice_note_by_token`, matching the existing anon-RPC pattern exactly: token validated server-side via the existing `validate_token()`, never a client-asserted author). The staff-side UI change shrank to match: rather than a new composer/panel, the existing quick-note input and end-of-practice textarea got `@`-mention support added directly. Only `HelperView` (anonymous) got a genuinely new UI surface, since it had no note-taking capability at all before.
  
  A second real bug surfaced by testing, not review: the archive UPDATE policy was added without the underlying `GRANT UPDATE ON notes TO authenticated` — RLS policies are inert without the base grant they sit on top of. Caught by testing the actual archive path end-to-end (`SET LOCAL ROLE authenticated` + real coach JWT claims, inside a rolled-back transaction) rather than assuming the policy alone was sufficient, matching this file's established verification convention. Fixed with a one-line follow-up migration.
  
  **Confirmed before migrating** (per the spec's own instruction not to guess on this): both the 500-character length cap and the 40-notes-per-token rate limit were built in from the start, not deferred.
  
  Every migration this session (four total: the initial parallel system, its correction, the grant fix, and none others needed) was dry-run inside `BEGIN;...;ROLLBACK;` before applying for real, and the final anonymous-write path was verified end-to-end in a rolled-back transaction — a real token created, a real note + player tag submitted through the actual RPC, a stranger's direct table insert confirmed rejected — before calling any of this done.

- **Real-usage bug, same day**: an assistant coach viewing a team's Schedule tab could still see and use `+ Practice`/`+ Series` for a team they don't head-coach. Root cause: `ScheduleScreen.jsx`'s `canScheduleAny` checked `data.teams.some(t => isHeadCoach(t, coachId))` — "is this coach head coach of *any* team at all" — correct for the global cross-team Schedule tab, but never re-scoped to the *specific* team being viewed when reached via a team workspace (`fixedTeamId` set). An assistant on Team A who happened to head-coach Team B saw the buttons on Team A's schedule because the check only ever looked at "any team," never "this one." Fixed by branching on `fixedTeamId`: scoped to that one team's `isHeadCoach` check when set, falling back to the original "any team" check only for the unscoped global view. Same session, also fixed the related UX gap this surfaced: `SchedulePracticeModal`/`SeriesWizard` always defaulted their team picker to `myTeams[0]` (whichever head-coached team sorted first), never the team you were actually standing in — added a `presetTeamId` prop (passed as `fixedTeamId` from `ScheduleScreen`), honored only if the coach actually head-coaches that team (falls back to `myTeams[0]` otherwise, so a stray id can't slip through).


### Thirteenth session (2026-07-27) — doc consolidation + dead code
- Six overlapping docs (this file plus `DATABASE-ARCHITECTURE.md`, `ROP-Goals-Nav-Snapshot.md`, `ROP-Org-Experience-Handoff.md`, `ROP-Public-Library-Spec.md`, `ROP-Sharing-Addendum.md`) had drifted out of sync with each other and with the actual code — most notably, `DATABASE-ARCHITECTURE.md` §7 documented a standalone `OrgHomeScreen.jsx` as shipped, but a later session had already retired it in favor of folding org content into `HomeScreen.jsx` under Coach/Org mode; and `ROP-Public-Library-Spec.md` self-described as "planning only, nothing built" when the Public Library feature it specified had, in fact, since been built and shipped. Rather than trust any one doc, the consolidation cross-checked claims against `git log` and the actual source before merging — caught both of the above, plus a stale "14-day agenda" claim in this file's own feature inventory (the agenda list is 7 days; a *different*, intentionally wider 14-day window exists only for the needs-a-plan nudge — not a contradiction once separated).
- Dead code removed from `src/`, each verified via a whole-repo grep (not just `src/`) before deletion: the orphaned `/team/:teamId/build` route, its `TeamBuildRoute` wrapper, and `BuildTab.jsx` (the tab bar link to it was already dropped in an earlier session per this file's own text, but the route/component were never cleaned up — reachable only by typing the URL directly); and nine unused exported functions from `supabase.js` — `fetchPracticeSeries`/`archivePracticeSeries` (series-scoped cancel/restore now goes through `cancelPractice`'s `scope:'future'` path instead), `reschedulePractice` (no call site anywhere — rescheduling, if built, went a different route), `fetchNoteCountsForPractices` (this file's own 2026-07-15 entry documents deleting its only call site and never removed the export), `endLiveSession` (superseded by a generic `updateLiveSession` call), and ~~four leftovers from the first org-model implementation that a later "Coach/Org mode" session reworked — `promoteDrillToOrgLibrary`, `fetchPendingOrgInvites`, `orgAssignTeamStaff`, `orgAssignPlayer`~~ **correction, 2026-07-28: `fetchPendingOrgInvites` was not actually dead — see the fifteenth-session entry below for the real bug this caused and its fix.** The other three (`promoteDrillToOrgLibrary`/`orgAssignTeamStaff`/`orgAssignPlayer`) were genuinely unreferenced anywhere, including within `supabase.js` itself, and stayed removed. `submitPublicFeedback` was deliberately **not** removed — see the Library section above, it's already-documented intentional dead code, not an oversight. `npm run build` verified clean after every removal.
- **A second, dedicated verification pass** (triggered by a direct question — "only Baseball/Basketball have categories" didn't match what the user knew to be true) re-checked every concrete factual claim in the Feature Inventory and Known Gaps sections against migrations/code directly, rather than trusting the first pass's synthesis. It found and fixed four more real errors, all now corrected in place above: (1) `skill_categories` actually covers 6 sports, not 2 — a 2026-07-18 migration added Football/Soccer/Lacrosse/Volleyball, and the per-coach tag-seeding function was never updated to match, so Baseball/Basketball coaches likely get duplicate-looking tags (personal copy + global) while the 4 newer sports don't; (2) `org_staff.role`'s CHECK constraint only allows `'director'` — `admin` doesn't exist as a value yet, despite being described as already-differentiated; (3) `app_data` was wrongly described as dead/unreferenced — it turned out to be the live backing store for `App.jsx`'s legacy `data`/`update()` state (see the next entry for the full trace and its resolution); (4) a wrong Edge Function name (`org-invite-notify` → `notify-org-invite`). The root cause across all four: the first pass verified some claims against code/git directly but carried others over from the source docs' own framing without redoing that check.
- **Full trace of `App.jsx`'s legacy `data`/`update()` state, and its removal.** Asked to finish what the second verification pass started: what does `app_data` actually still do? Traced `fullData` (`App.jsx`, what every screen actually receives) to `Object.assign({},data,{teams},library,planning)` — since later arguments win, `library`/`planning` overwrite every field they share a name with (`teams`, `locations`, `assets`, `activityLibrary`, `templates`, `practices`), so the *only* survivor from the raw `app_data`-backed `data` object was `.notes`, and nothing read it once `NotesTab` was gone (previous entry). On the write side, a repo-wide grep for actual `update(...)` invocations (not just the prop being passed around) found exactly one call site, inside `ModalLayer.jsx`'s `"editTemplate"` modal branch — and that modal type was never once opened anywhere (`openModal("editTemplate", ...)` has zero call sites; its sibling `"addTemplate"` had no submit handler at all even if reached). Templates are actually edited today through `TemplateWorkspace` (`NewLibraryScreen.jsx`, a real `saveTemplateTree`-backed flow) — the modal branch was a leftover from before that existed. Removed the dead modal branch (variable, submit handler, form JSX, two orphaned `TITLES` entries), which left `update()` with zero call sites anywhere in the app.
  
  With both sides of `app_data` proven dead, removed the whole pathway: `data`/`setData` state and the `update` callback in `App.jsx`; `loadData`/`saveData`/`flushSave`/`setCoachKey` in `supabase.js`; `INIT`, `DEMO_INIT` (an entirely unused demo-coach seed object — not imported anywhere despite a comment claiming otherwise), and `migrateData` (a shape-repair function for the old blob, still carrying a hardcoded `c_jaxon2`→`c_jaxon1` id fix from the pre-rewrite POC era) in `constants.js`; and the now-pointless `update` prop threaded through `HomeScreen`, `ScheduleScreen`, `RostersTab`, `BuilderScreen`, `CommandScreen`/`HistoryViewer`, `PracticeDetail`, `NewLibraryScreen` (which didn't even destructure it), and `ModalLayer`. `fullData` was renamed to plain `data`, now built directly from `teams`/`library`/`planning` with no `app_data` blob underneath it at all.

  One real behavior change, not just deletion: `loaded` (gates the "Loading your data..." spinner) used to flip once `loadData()` resolved. Re-wired to a single effect that runs `Promise.all([refreshTeams(),refreshLibrary(),refreshPlanning()])` on `coachId` change and flips `loaded` after all three resolve — the three individual mount-triggering effects that used to call each one separately were folded into this one. Verified via `npm run build` (clean, slightly smaller bundle) and a dev-server load of the signed-out landing page (no console errors) — the signed-in `loaded` path itself couldn't be click-tested, same standing credentials limitation noted throughout this file's history.

  **This shipped a real production incident.** `Promise.all` has no `.catch()` — a rejection in any one of the three fetches leaves `loaded` stuck at `false` forever, since `.then()` never fires. The old gate never had this failure mode (it was checking something fully decoupled from these three fetches), so this was a new bug introduced by the refactor, not a pre-existing one surfaced by it. It hit in production immediately after deploy (app stuck on the loading screen for the real user). Fixed same-day by switching to `Promise.allSettled`, which always resolves regardless of individual rejections — logs which fetch failed via `console.error` rather than silently hanging. Lesson for next time this pattern comes up: gating UI on `Promise.all` of several independent data fetches is only safe if every one of them is guaranteed to resolve (they're not, in general) — `allSettled` (or a per-fetch `.catch()`) is the default-safe choice.

- **Legacy POC tables dropped** (`app_data`, `coaches`, old `live_sessions`) once `app_data`'s last reference was gone (previous entry). Before dropping anything on the live production database, verified rather than assumed: queried current row counts (5/2/76, matching the already-documented orphaned-POC snapshot), checked `app_data`'s actual keys and confirmed none matched the real coach's actual `auth.users` uuid (all were old POC-era ids like `coach_coach_demo_wwql`), and ran the `DROP TABLE` wrapped in `BEGIN;...;ROLLBACK;` first to confirm no dependency errors (views, FKs) before committing to it for real. Also had to reconcile 2 migrations (`20260724000000`, `20260724010000`) that were already live in the schema but unrecorded in the CLI's migration-history table — the exact drift this file had flagged as pending since the 2026-07-21 session — via `migration repair --status applied` before the drop migration could push cleanly. Applied via `supabase db push`; confirmed post-drop that all three tables are gone and the new migration is correctly recorded with no new drift.

- **Baseball/Basketball skill-tag duplication, fixed.** Root cause confirmed by querying the real coach's actual tags directly rather than reasoning about it in the abstract: every Baseball/Basketball category had an identically-named `scope='coach'` row (from the 2026-07-11 per-coach seeder) sitting next to the `scope='global'` row the 2026-07-18 migration added — 947 duplicate rows across all 17 coaches who'd ever signed in (mostly disposable test accounts), confirmed via direct query before touching anything. A coach's own genuinely custom tags were never at risk — the fix only ever matches on an *exact* `(category_id, name)` collision against an existing global tag, so tags like "Passing on the move," "Layups," "Cutting," and "Transition defense" (none of which are in the seed function's hardcoded list) were confirmed untouched afterward.

  Before deleting anything, checked what actually referenced the duplicate rows: 15 real `drill_tags` rows pointed at a duplicate instead of its global twin (0 `player_focus_areas` rows did). Deleting the duplicates outright would have cascade-deleted those 15 real tag associations (`drill_tags.skill_tag_id` is `ON DELETE CASCADE`), so the migration re-points them to the global tag first — dropping only the rare case where a drill was already tagged with *both* the duplicate and its global twin (which would otherwise violate `drill_tags`' `UNIQUE(activity_library_id, skill_tag_id)`) — then deletes the now-unreferenced duplicates. Dry-run confirmed `drill_tags`' total row count was identical before and after (202), meaning every real tagging survived, just re-pointed.

  `seed_default_skill_tags_for_coach` was also updated (`CREATE OR REPLACE`) so it no longer special-cases Baseball/Basketball — every sport now falls through to the same empty-array no-op. The function and its trigger are kept in place rather than removed, since `ensureDefaultSkillTags` is still called client-side on every sign-in and a future sport could legitimately want true per-coach starter tags again; today it just does nothing for any sport, matching how Football/Soccer/Lacrosse/Volleyball already worked. Applied via `supabase db push`; confirmed post-migration that zero duplicates remain, `drill_tags`' count is unchanged, and the coach's real custom tags are intact.


*This file supersedes and replaces `DATABASE-ARCHITECTURE.md`, `ROP-Goals-Nav-Snapshot.md`, `ROP-Org-Experience-Handoff.md`, `ROP-Public-Library-Spec.md`, and `ROP-Sharing-Addendum.md`, all removed 2026-07-27. Their content is folded in above where still accurate, or captured in the Session Log where superseded. Recover any of them from git history if needed: `git log --all --diff-filter=D -- '<filename>'`.*
